Same for `HealthReport`'s `mr_review` check — it counts review failures under `review_failed` / `review_failures_exhausted` whatever produced them, and its `/admin/health` + `/healthz` messages announced "mr-review failed on N issue(s)" — and for the `web_project_edit_desc_label_attention` description, which listed "failing mr-review" among the causes of an abandon. All of them now name the step. The check's own name, its key and the two activity keys are unchanged, so nothing that selects on them moves. This was outside the ticket's inventory, which held the `mr_review_timeout` description to be the only user-visible occurrence; these five are user-visible too, and wrong on exactly the same terms.

## [1.0.0-alpha.47] - 2026-08-11

### Fixed

- **The documented "give it back to autodev" gesture no longer parks a ticket for good (Autodev #52).** GitLab allows one label per scope, so on a project whose todo label sits in the workflow scope — ff/fast/core configures `labels_todo: ["Development::ToDo"]` against `label_doing: "Development::Doing"`, and powerpanne/core carries both `To do` and `Development::ToDo` — reposing the todo label on an **active** row makes GitLab drop `label_doing` in the same edit. `LabelHandover#suspect` read that absence as `doing_removed`, closed the row, and posted a comment telling the reader to repose the todo label and reassign — which they had just done. `PollRouter#todo_reapplied_after?` then refused the reentry it gates, because it wants the todo applied *after* `finished_at` and this one preceded it: the ticket sat in `closed` until somebody removed and re-applied the label by hand. `doing_removed` is the weakest of the three signals — an absence, not an edit naming a destination — so it now yields whenever a todo label is present on the row: somebody asking for work is never a reason to stop, and the absence is left to `PollRouter` to read. The two positive signals (`done_added`, `workflow_moved`) are untouched.
- **An operator reset is no longer undone at the next poll (Autodev #53).** `PollTracker#seed_watch_clock` only fills a NULL `checking_pipeline_since`, so the two `update_all` entries into `checking_pipeline` have to *arrive* at NULL — and they did not clear the column, they inherited whatever the row was carrying. Not every exit from the state clears it either: `handle_stagnation` writes `status: 'done'` directly, so the AASM callback that owns the column never runs and the row keeps the clock of the watch that just ended. A ticket bailed out by stagnation months ago therefore came back from **Réinitialiser** with a months-old clock, and the new age bound abandoned it at the very next poll — back to `done` + `pipeline_watch_expired`, plus a `label_done` write and a GitLab comment, silently undoing the operator's action. `reset_for_retry!` and `revive_stalled!` now clear the column explicitly (closing every careless-exit path at once), and `handle_stagnation` clears it on the way out, mirroring the `give_up_on_watch` it is modelled on.
- **The active sweep runs before anything is enqueued (Autodev #52).** `dispatch_existing` ran `dispatch_pipelines` first, and `IssueProcessJob#perform` reloads the row without re-checking the status it was enqueued for — so with Solid Queue picking the job up immediately, the worker won the race against the inline sweep almost every time. That cost #52 its most common case: a pipeline resolving on the same cycle as a handover drove the row to `done` through `handle_green`, `done` is not in `ACTIVE_STATUSES` so the sweep never looked at it again, and `apply_label_done` overwrote the workflow label the human had just set (GitLab dropping `Development::Awaiting CR` to make room). `dispatch_unassignment` — the pass that decides whether a row is still ours at all — now runs first. Pass order was already load-bearing in this method; this makes it so for one more reason.
- **A pipeline watch is now bounded in time (Autodev #53).** Nothing stopped one. `dispatch_pipelines` re-enqueues every `checking_pipeline` row on every cycle with no condition beyond the status, and the only existing bound — stagnation — is fed exclusively from the red branch: `update_stagnation_signature(_, :pipeline, _)` is called only from `infra_skip?` and `check_stagnation_and_fix`, both under `when 'failed'`. A pipeline that is never red — `manual`, `canceled`, `skipped`, or a head pipeline stuck at `created` — therefore accumulated no signature, `bail_on_stagnation?` was unreachable for it, and the row polled forever; `CLAUDE.md` said so out loud ("indefinitely"). Production issue #15894 polled 29 773 times. A poll that ends **without a transition** on a watch older than the new `pipeline_watch_max_days` (default 14, `0` disables) now gives the ticket up: `done` + `needs_attention` with a dedicated `pipeline_watch_expired` reason, `label_done`, and a localised GitLab comment (FR + EN) saying the MR stays open and a human should take it from here. The check is the last statement of the poll and runs only while the row is still `checking_pipeline`, so "the poll ended without a transition" is a condition rather than an enumeration of the branches that go nowhere — a green pipeline on day 15 still completes, a red one still enters the fix cycle, a GitLab outage raises before reaching it, and the branches Autodev #51 is rewriting are covered without naming any of them. The reason is deliberately not `stagnation_pipeline`: `dispatch_infra_recheck` selects exactly that value and would re-arm the row. Clock: a new `issues.checking_pipeline_since`, written by a single AASM callback (so "reset on any transition" needs no clear at each of the six exits) and seeded by `PollTracker` for the rows that arrive through `update_all` — `reset_for_retry!` and `revive_stalled!` bypass the state machine. The migration backfills rows already in the state from their newest `transition` event, falling back to `issues.created_at`, so the bound applies to the existing backlog instead of granting it a fresh two weeks at the release. Why 14 days: above the longest legitimate human absence (a two-week holiday, a shutdown week with a weekend either side), far below the six-week horizon the ticket calls "no longer worth watching", and far above the bounds that already cover the diagnosable cases (~25 min for a red loop at `stagnation_threshold`, ~5 h for `infra_recheck_max × backoff`) — a safety net must only fire on what the specific mechanisms cannot see.
- **A poll no longer writes an `activity_events` row (Autodev #53).** `replace_pattern:` has always meant "this entry supersedes its previous occurrence", but only the GitLab note obeyed it: `persist_event!` ran first and always INSERTed. Production carried **477 827 `pipeline_checking` rows out of 898 424 (53 %)** in a 264 MB SQLite file, 29 773 of them on issue #15894 alone — enough that `/issues/15894` showed 200 poll lines and nothing else, that its GitLab thread was unreadable, that the "Activité de la semaine" sparkline measured polling rather than work, and that `/stream` got one Turbo frame per poll per watched row. One rule now covers both sinks: a replaced note line has a replaced row, which also fixes `pipeline_red`, `pipeline_infra` and `pipeline_evaluating` (the other three `replace_pattern` call sites, which grow the same way on a red-stuck ticket). **What this does to Autodev #50's invariant: nothing, by construction.** Supersession is an `update_columns` that moves `created_at` forward — for a collapsed row that means "last occurrence", the same thing the note line's leading timestamp means — so a row polled last cycle still carries an event from last cycle and `Issue.without_activity_since` reads unchanged inputs. Logging only on pipeline-status change was rejected for exactly that reason: it would have made correctness rest on `checking_pipeline` being absent from `Issue::STALLED_STATES`, a constant defined in another file for another purpose. That absence is nonetheless pinned by a new guard test (against `STALLED_STATES` and both halves of `HealthReport`'s stuck card, plus an end-to-end check that `DormantAudit#candidates` ignores a watched row silent for a week), so a future change to it fails loudly rather than letting the audit reposition a row a live worker holds. Skipping `after_create_commit` is the second intended effect: a re-occurrence pushes no SSE frame.
- **The 477 827 accumulated rows get a purge, as code (Autodev #53).** New `bin/rails autodev:compact_activity_events` applies the collapse retroactively — keep the newest occurrence of a collapsible key per issue, delete the superseded ones — which makes it idempotent by construction, leaves the current "still watching since X" line in place, and touches nothing else (`transition`, `heartbeat`, the system rows and every other `danger_claude` key survive). It reports and deletes nothing unless `APPLY=1`; `VACUUM=1` is a second opt-in because reclaiming the file needs free disk equal to its size and an exclusive lock. A rake task rather than a migration: `auto_migrate` runs migrations at boot in three processes, a 478 000-row DELETE plus a VACUUM must not happen on a schedule nobody chose, and VACUUM cannot run inside the transaction AR wraps a migration in. **It has not been run against any real database** — the production purge is a manual operation and its procedure (dry run, backup, delete, VACUUM, verification queries) is in the design spec. New tests: `activity_logger_collapse_test.rb`, `pipeline_watch_invariant_test.rb`, `pipeline_watch_bound_test.rb`, `models/checking_pipeline_since_test.rb`, `migrations/backfill_checking_pipeline_since_test.rb`, `tasks/compact_activity_events_test.rb`. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-bound-pipeline-watch-design.md`.
- **A human can now stop autodev by moving the ticket's workflow label, and autodev notices within one poll cycle (Autodev #52).** Autodev never re-read a ticket's labels, so a human taking the work back had no way of saying so. On `powerpanne/core#15894` the workflow label went from `Development::Doing` to `Development::Awaiting CR` on 24/07 at 09:24, one minute after a "test sur review ok" comment — and autodev kept polling that MR's pipeline for more than two weeks; `#16237`, `#16258` and `#16341` are the same story. `dispatch_unassignment` already fetched each active row's GitLab issue and threw the `labels` array away, exactly as it used to throw `state` away before #44, so the check rides along on the read that already happens. Three edits stop the work: `label_doing` removed, `label_done` applied, or the ticket moved to another label in autodev's own workflow scope. **The scope is derived, not configured**, from `label_doing` + `label_done` — the two labels autodev owns and writes. A literal "a label autodev does not know" rule is unusable: real tickets permanently carry `PM::Evolution`, `Fourrière`, client names, `Backlog`, and that rule would close every ticket on the project. `labels_todo` is deliberately left out of the derivation and merely exempted from the verdict: it is the entry point a human uses, and on `powerpanne/core` it is GitLab's stock unscoped `To Do` while the other two are `Development::…`, so including it (as first proposed) would have disabled the rule on the very project the bug came from. When the two do not share a scope the rule **self-disables** down to the two presence checks, so a project that does not follow the convention gets the documented fallback with no false-close risk and nothing to configure either way. New `Autodev::LabelHandover` (`app/services/autodev/label_handover.rb`), new `test/label_handover_test.rb` (19 cases). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-reread-labels-and-assignment-design.md`.
- **Autodev's own label writes are told apart from a human's by reading GitLab's resource label events, at no cost on a healthy ticket (Autodev #52).** Autodev applies and removes these very labels in normal operation, so "by somebody else" has to be established rather than assumed. Inferring it from the state machine's expectation — while a row is active, autodev holds `label_doing` and has not applied `label_done`, so any deviation is somebody else's — is free but races: `apply_label_done` writes the GitLab label a few hundred milliseconds before the row's status reaches `done`, from inside the per-issue `limits_concurrency` lock the poll cycle does not hold, so a sweep landing in that gap would close a ticket autodev was about to deliver and post a comment blaming a human who did nothing. Authorship is therefore read from `client.issue_label_events`, but **staged**: stage 1 works off the labels already in the issue payload and produces no candidate on a healthy ticket, and only a candidate spends the extra call — once, for a row that is closed at the end of the same cycle. Reading the events unconditionally, as the ticket costed it, would have been one extra call per active row per cycle forever (~240/hour at current volume). Both cost claims are pinned by tests, because the two-stage shape is easy to collapse back into one call by accident. Everything unknown resolves to "do not stop" — no event, no matching event, an action contradicting the suspicion, an author that is autodev, or a GitLab error: a missed handover costs what the bug already costs today, a wrong stop kills live work.
- **A ticket autodev was unassigned from is now `closed` and says so on GitLab (Autodev #52).** `stop_unassigned` wrote `done` and left nothing but one line appended to the folded activity note, so the person who unassigned autodev never learned whether it had actually stopped. `done` was also the wrong word: a ticket a human pulled back mid-implementation was never delivered, and #44 had already settled that `closed` says more than `done` for the sibling case of a GitLab closure. The row now goes through the same `close!` + `finished_at` + `needs_attention`-cleanup writer as `close_externally` (new shared `ExternalState#close_row!`, so the two terminal outcomes cannot drift apart), and a real GitLab comment goes out first — through the `unassigned_stop` template that had been sitting in `notifications.{fr,en}.yml` **with no call site** since the pre-Rails poller, reworked with a `done_nominal`-style hand-back line. **One consequence is deliberate:** `dispatch_done_unassigned` selects `done` rows with an open MR that autodev is no longer assigned to, so a row stopped mid-flight used to land in it and fire the `post_completion` hook over a half-finished MR one cycle later (verified against the pre-change code). It no longer does — the hook is a delivery hook. The population it exists for is untouched and is pinned by new `test/post_completion_after_unassignment_test.rb`: every nominal completion path reaches `done` *before* `reassign_to_author` hands the ticket back, and `dispatch_unassignment` only sweeps active rows, so a delivered ticket is still `done` and still unassigned when the next cycle looks at it. Six new locale keys in `fr` and `en`.
- **A stopped ticket can be handed back to autodev the documented way, from `closed` (Autodev #52).** `closed` was terminal — `PollRouter#route_by_state` re-entered from `done` and from nothing else — so moving the unassignment stop to `closed` would have turned the loop every guide describes ("repose the todo label, reassign autodev", after a KO test or a KO code review) into a dead end, and made the GitLab notice the stop posts a lie. `reenter` and `reenter_to_check_pipeline` now also accept `closed`, **gated on a threshold**: only a `labels_todo` label applied *after* `finished_at` counts. That gate is what keeps the dashboard's *Clôturer* button an off-switch — there the todo label was already on the ticket when the operator clicked, so its `add` event predates the close and the row stays closed — while a human reposing the label after autodev stopped produces a fresh `add` and re-enters. The question it asks is the honest one: did somebody ask again *after* we stopped? It costs one `issue_label_events` call per `closed`-but-still-todo-labelled row per cycle, a population that empties itself (a reentry leaves `closed`; the merged-MR branch strips the label), and a `done` row pays nothing. New cases in `test/poll_router_reenter_test.rb`.
- **The dormant audit no longer re-arms a ticket a human has already moved on (Autodev #52).** `DormantAudit#route` gains the same label check, between "unassigned" and "revived", so the ordering stays closure > unassignment > handover > re-arming. This is #48's lesson applied rather than re-learned: a decision about whether a ticket is still ours must not live in one pass while the other population is never swept. Without it, a dormant row carrying `Development::Awaiting CR` would be handed a fresh retry budget and restart work that is no longer autodev's.
- **A ticket being worked on can no longer be repositioned under the worker handling it (Autodev #50).** `dispatch_dormant_audit`'s active arm treats "no `activity_events` row for `stuck_active_after` (2 h)" as "the worker died", and mutates by `update_all` from the poll cycle — outside the `limits_concurrency` that serialises `IssueProcessJob`. That was already false at default settings: `PipelineFixer` emits `:pipeline_fixing` once on entering the state, then loops over the N failed jobs with **two** danger-claude calls each and no activity event in between, so silence in `fixing_pipeline` reaches `N × 2 × dc_timeout` — two slow jobs already reach the window and three exceed it, and a closure or unassignment landing inside it can leave an MR the row no longer records, silently, since the model runs `whiny_transitions: false`. Every **issue-scoped** danger-claude call now writes a liveness row (new `activity_events` kind `heartbeat`, DB-only — no GitLab round-trip) from `DangerClaudeRunner`, the single point both `danger_claude_prompt` and `danger_claude_commit` funnel through, bounding silence to one call in every loop, including ones not yet written. Two call sites bypass `DangerClaudeRunner` entirely (`Autospec::ProjectBriefer`, `Autodev::UsageChecker`) — harmless today since neither is issue-scoped, but named so a future issue-scoped call in their style isn't assumed covered. The rows are invisible to the UI (new `ActivityEvent.user_visible` scope, applied to the issue timeline, the dashboard sparkline, and an SSE guard) and deliberately **counted** by `Issue.without_activity_since`, which is the whole mechanism. `mr-review` — run via `Open3` with no timeout, in the `reviewing` state — gets the same heartbeat immediately before its call, at both its call sites, bounding that state's silence to one review run instead of leaving it unbounded (not a fixed duration: that run's own duration was still unbounded at this point — closed by the Autodev #54 entry below).
- **`HealthReport#stuck_active_after` is now derived instead of only configured (Autodev #50).** It is `max(configured, TIMEOUT_SLACK_FACTOR × the longest configured timeout)` — over `dc_timeout` **and** `post_completion_timeout` (whose shell command gets no heartbeat, so its silence equals its timeout exactly), across `projects` rows and not-yet-imported YAML entries — so raising a per-project timeout widens the window instead of breaking the invariant, and the two settings can no longer be configured into disagreement. Nothing changes at default settings (2 × 1 800 is under the 7 200 s floor); an explicit `monitoring.stuck_active_after_seconds` becomes a floor rather than a ceiling, and the effective value is reported as `window_seconds` on the `stuck_issues` check. The constant is named `TIMEOUT_SLACK_FACTOR`, not `HEARTBEAT_FACTOR` — it multiplies a timeout, not a heartbeat interval, and is a heuristic rather than a bound for as long as any inter-call work (screenshot uploads, `job_trace` fetches, git operations) stays untimed. The same fix makes the "Issues bloquées" card catch long-but-live runs it used to flag — though its meaning shifted alongside it: it now reads "no danger-claude call started", not "no business activity", so a wedged-but-chatty worker (no such loop exists in the codebase today) sits outside what it can see.
- **`post_completion_timeout`'s baked default moves out of a literal (Autodev #50).** The `|| 300` in `lib/autodev/pipeline_monitor/post_completion.rb` becomes `Config::POST_COMPLETION_TIMEOUT`, read by both the runtime and the `stuck_active_after` formula, so the two can no longer drift. New tests: `activity_event_heartbeat_test.rb`, `danger_claude_runner_heartbeat_test.rb`, `pipeline_monitor_review_heartbeat_test.rb`, `controllers/issues_controller_heartbeat_test.rb`, `services/health_report_stuck_window_test.rb`, plus a widened-window regression pair in `dormant_audit_selection_test.rb` and a sparkline exclusion in `weekly_activity_counts_test.rb`. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-10-live-worker-silence-invariant-design.md`.
- **A review that never returns can no longer leave a ticket exposed (Autodev #54).** `mr-review` ran via a raw `Open3.capture3` with **no timeout**, in the `reviewing` state — which is in `Issue::STALLED_STATES`, so `dispatch_dormant_audit` repositions rows out of it with `update_all`, outside the per-issue concurrency lock. #50 bounded that silence at one `mr-review` run by writing a heartbeat before the call, but a wedged run (process hung, pipe never closed) was still unbounded, and no configured value sized the stuck-window for it — `reviewing` was recorded as the one exception the derived window could not reach. It now runs under `ProcessRunner#run_with_timeout`, capped by a new per-project `mr_review_timeout` (baked default 3600 s, sized on 317 production reviews whose longest successful run took 2641 s) — `run_with_timeout` gained an additive `timeout:` kwarg, and `dc_timeout` no longer caps `mr-review` at all. `HealthReport#longest_worker_timeout` **gains the term** (across the baked default, `projects` rows and YAML entries): window-neutral at the default, since `2 × 3600` is exactly the existing 7200 s floor, but a project raising its review cap now widens the window automatically — and the state leaves the exceptions list, `running_post_completion` now the only one remaining. A timeout stays **non-fatal**, as before: the wrapper raises, `Reviewer#execute_mr_review`'s existing rescue answers `false`, and `launch_review` counts a review failure (`review_failure_count`, threshold 5) rather than dropping the request to `error` — behaviour that already held and is now pinned by tests, alongside assertions that the call is wrapped at all and that its working directory stays the process's own cwd (which `Open3.capture3` inherited implicitly). Relatedly, `IssueProcessor::MrManager`'s `run_review` / `execute_review` / `command_exists?` — a parallel review path with no caller anywhere, duplicating `Reviewer` — was deleted rather than kept in sync; `PipelineMonitor::Reviewer` is now the only review path, which it already was in practice.
- **A failed `mr-review` now says why, instead of logging an empty line (Autodev #49).** The call kept `stderr` and threw `stdout` away — and `mr-review` fails writing nothing to `stderr`, so across every retained production log the message is identical and useless: `mr-review failed (non-fatal):` followed by nothing, **15 times, with no exception**. Those 15 are `5 × 3`: three tickets exhausted `REVIEW_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` on them and were delivered with **no review at all** (`#16415`/`!11343`, `#16224`/`!11187`, `#12852`/`!11286`), and the cause is *still* unknown, because the only copy of it was discarded at the call site. Each attempt took ≈1 s once `execute_mr_review`'s 15 s pre-sleep is subtracted — a crash at startup or a rejected argument, not the wedge #54 was about. The failure message now reports **both streams**, each labelled and each rendered `(empty)` rather than as a dangling colon, capped at 2000 characters per stream (up from 300) with the number of dropped characters stated instead of the cut being silent, and the whole thing passed through `Redactor` — the logger on this path in production is `Autodev::JobLogger`, which unlike `AppLogger` does **not** scrub, and `mr-review` holds the same GitLab PAT autodev does. It also reports the **exit status**, which required `ProcessRunner#run_with_timeout` to hand back the `Process::Status` as a fourth, optional element (surplus on a three-variable destructuring, so both `danger-claude` callers are untouched — pinned by a test, along with the first real-subprocess test in the suite): on the one failure shape actually observed, where neither stream says anything, the status *is* the diagnostic, and `exit 127`, `exit 1` and a termsig are three different investigations. Finally, `give_up_reviewing` now persists `@dc_stdout` / `@dc_stderr` onto the row, in the same `issues` columns every other failure in the product uses, so the diagnostic outlives log rotation (no view renders them for a `done` row yet — surfacing them is a follow-up; losing them was not recoverable). **`REVIEW_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` is deliberately left at 5**: all 15 attempts failed deterministically from the first, so no threshold value would have saved those three MRs, and picking a new number against no data is the mistake #54 already made and corrected. What is genuinely missing is aggregation — `review_failure_count` is per-issue, so a globally broken `mr-review` costs 5 rounds *per ticket* and raises N unrelated flags instead of one alert saying the tool is down; recorded as the follow-up, with its threshold and window to be chosen against production numbers. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-mr-review-diagnostic-capture-design.md`.
- **A migration that fails to apply can no longer leave autodev running in silent total outage (Autodev #55).** `config/initializers/auto_migrate.rb` rescued every `StandardError` from the migration pass and answered with one `Rails.logger.warn`, so "the peer that won the boot race got there first" and "nobody applied this and the schema is now incomplete" read identically in the log. The first is expected — SQLite reports `supports_advisory_locks? == false`, so Rails does not serialise the two migrators the supervisor produces, and the loser fails on `duplicate column name` or on the UNIQUE insert into `schema_migrations` while the winner's column is already there. The second is a **total processing outage**: `Project#to_project_config` calls `public_send` for every config column, so one missing column raises `NoMethodError` on every job, for every ticket, announced by a single log line. Nothing of the sort has been observed in production — it needs an external writer to hold the SQLite write lock through the whole 30 s `busy_timeout` during a restart — but the blast radius is total, which is what made it worth hardening. The fix splits one `rescue` into two independent questions, in a new `Autodev::MigrationStatus`. **Classification** (`benign_race?`) is a heuristic over the exception message and picks only a log level: `warn` for a recognised race, `error` otherwise, the error branch naming the versions actually left unapplied and pointing at `/admin/health` — and saying so explicitly when a failed pass turns out to have left a complete schema anyway. **Verification** (`pending`) is the migration files minus the `schema_migrations` rows, per database, read through each pool's own connection (`ActiveRecord::Base`, `SolidQueue::Record`) with no `establish_connection`; it is the only predicate that gates anything, so no misclassification can either abort a boot or hide an incomplete schema. `bin/autodev` gates on it: it already played the pass first and alone (it requires `config/environment` long before `run_supervisor`), and now **refuses to spawn its children** when anything is left unapplied, with a `Config error:` naming the database, the versions and the two ways out. That refusal is deliberately confined to the supervisor — the path that starts the workers. `--status` / `--errors` / `--reset` exit before the gate, and `bin/rails runner`, a standalone `bin/rails server` and the test suite never reach it: those are the tools needed to diagnose the condition being gated. For them, a new **Migrations** card on `/admin/health` reports it (`down`, so `/healthz` answers 503 and an external probe pages, with the unapplied versions in `meta`). A rollback to an older autodev leaves `schema_migrations` rows with no file; the difference is computed one way only, so that reads as complete rather than tripping the gate. Also corrected: the initializer's comment still claimed Autodev had "no concurrent writers", which the supervisor invalidated long ago. New `test/services/autodev/migration_status_test.rb` (17 cases) plus two on the health report. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-auto-migrate-failure-handling-design.md`.
- **A finished ticket whose pipeline ends on a manual job is now delivered instead of watched forever (Autodev #51).** GitLab reports `manual` when every job that could run has run and what is left needs a human to press play. `PipelineMonitor#dispatch_status` knew three outcomes — running, `success`, `failed` — so `manual` landed in the `else`, which logs a line and returns; the row stayed in `checking_pipeline` and **nothing bounded the wait**, since stagnation detection is fed from `handle_red` only. On a project whose MR pipelines end with a manual `deploy_review`, `manual` is the *normal* end state of a green MR, so every ticket whose MR went green parked permanently. Measured on `powerpanne/core` on 2026-08-10: pipeline 215229 (MR !11154) had all four blocking jobs green and was read **12 729 times in 18 days**, once every two minutes; four tickets (#15894, #16237, #16258, #16341) sat finished with `review_count` 0 — never reviewed, never `label_done`, never reassigned — until three different humans relabelled them by hand, between a day and four weeks later. The same #15894 had already spent 13 days in that `else` on `canceled` before it. The verdict now comes off the **jobs** rather than the roll-up, which cannot express "what gates the merge is green, what gates a deploy awaits a human": the blocking subset excludes `allow_failure: true` jobs (GitLab itself says the result does not count) and unplayed `manual` gates (no result, and never one without a human), and the question asked of it is *did anything that counts fail?* — not *did everything succeed?*, because a blocking job may legitimately sit `created` or `skipped` downstream of the gate and demanding `success` would rebuild the same infinite wait one level down. Nothing can be mid-run: a running job would make the roll-up `running`. `skipped` joins the same path — it means CI decided nothing should run, the same absence of verification that `dispatch_pipeline` already treats as green when there is no pipeline at all, so an MR with no CI shipping instantly while an MR whose CI skipped itself waits forever was an inconsistency, not a policy. The new `fetch_pipeline_jobs` returns **`nil`**, not `[]`, when GitLab is unreachable: `[]` reads as green on this path, so a swallowed API error would be enough to ship an unverified ticket. **`canceled` is deliberately unchanged** and keeps the documented "No blocked state" behaviour: an interrupted run has no verdict to read (its blocking jobs are `canceled`, not `failed`, so the same rule would deliver a ticket whose tests were killed mid-flight), and unlike `manual` the wait usually resolves on its own because a new pipeline supersedes it and `head_pipeline` re-points — the residual unbounded tail belongs to the generic time bound (Autodev #53), not to a second status-specific clock. No activity-log line was added: `handle_green`'s `:pipeline_green` already records the delivery, and a line emitted before it would sit on the wrong side of the Claude-quota gate, appending to the GitLab note on every poll and heading for the 1M-character cap during an outage — so the diagnostic (`Pipeline manual for MR !… : N blocking job(s), none failed → treating as green`) goes to the technical log, and the ticket reads "Pipeline vert" for a pipeline GitLab paints orange. New `test/pipeline_monitor_manual_status_test.rb` (11 cases, including the empty-pipeline, only-manual-jobs, allowed-failure and unreachable-endpoint edges, plus a pin that `canceled` still never even fetches the jobs). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-manual-pipeline-status-design.md`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.46] - 2026-08-10

### Fixed

- **A ticket that has stopped progressing is now caught automatically (Autodev #47).** A `pending` row with a NULL `next_retry_at` was invisible to every poll pass: not rediscovered via GitLab (it still carries `label_doing`, not `label_todo`), and not picked up by `dispatch_retries` either, which requires a non-NULL stamp. 14 such tickets sat frozen on one project, the oldest since April, 13 of them still open and still assigned — real requested work, never done, with nobody told.
- **A ticket closed or reassigned on GitLab while parked `pending` or `error` is now detected (Autodev #48).** The sweep only ever looked at active rows, on the assumption that a row always eventually moves — false for a dormant one. Two real tickets (one closed, one handed back to a human) sat in a false state as a result.
- **A ticket frozen by a worker dying mid-run (`cloning`, `implementing`, `answering_question`, `fixing_discussions`…) is now revived without waiting for a service restart.**

### Changed

- **`dispatch_error_recheck` becomes `dispatch_dormant_audit` and now covers three dormant populations instead of one.** Config keys `error_recheck_max` / `error_recheck_backoff` become `dormant_audit_max` / `dormant_audit_backoff`; the legacy names are still accepted.

- **Both usage guides refreshed for this release.** Functional (`/help`): the "what each card asks of you" list gains the new *La demande ne progresse plus* case — Autodev noticed on its own that a request had stalled, tried several times to restart it, and could not; the answer is to check the ticket on GitLab, since most of the time it is simply no longer relevant. Technical (`/admin/help`): the polling list, the config-fields table, the Claude-quota gate matrix and the error catalog all follow `dispatch_error_recheck` → `dispatch_dormant_audit`, with the three arms, the three outcomes, the `dormant_audit_*` keys and their legacy fallbacks, the two migrations behind the `dormant_recheck_*` columns, and a new row for a run interrupted in `fixing_discussions` / `answering_question`. Screenshots not recaptured — no screen layout changed.

## [1.0.0-alpha.45] - 2026-08-06

### Fixed

- **A CSM can now find the merge request to deploy from a ticket number (Autodev #45).** The report was "these Ready-for-QA tickets can't be deployed"; the three MRs turned out to be perfectly deployable — `deploy_review` present and actionable on every head pipeline (`success` → retry, `manual` → play), memberships fine, the page in the sidebar. What was broken was the way in. `/deploy_review` speaks *merge requests*; someone working a "Ready for QA" board speaks *tickets*, and the page answered with 100 unsorted, unsearchable cards. Two defects came out of the investigation. **Pagination**: `per_page: 100` with no `.auto_paginate` on a project with **129 open MRs** left 29 unreachable, silently — the same failure mode `test/gitlab_helpers_pagination_test.rb` already documents for four other endpoints. **No way in**: nothing mapped ticket → MR. New `Autodev::DeployReviewSearch` resolves a query two ways and unions them — free text over title / source branch / iid (which covers the common case on its own, since branches are named `<ticket-iid>-<slug>`), plus GitLab's `related_merge_requests` when the query is a bare number. The second path is what the convention can't do: ticket #16294's own MR was merged and the open MRs carrying its work are named after another ticket, so only GitLab knows the link. Related MRs are intersected with the open-MR list, which filters out merged/closed ones for free. The related lookup is an enhancement, never a dependency — if it fails, the text matches still come back; a failing *list* is the whole answer, so it stays an explicit error rather than an empty page reading as "nothing to deploy". An empty search result says "no match" instead of reusing "no open MR". Also added: a `untracked=1` filter (58 of the 129 open MRs are already tracked), a result count, and preservation of both the query and the filter across the trigger redirect, so a deploy doesn't dump you back into the full list. New `test/services/autodev/deploy_review_search_test.rb` (18 cases) + 7 controller cases. Verified against the three reported tickets on the real project: 16294 → !11284 + !11283 (both actionable), 15966 → !11203, 16432 → !11292, and 129 MRs fetched instead of 100.

- **The web layer no longer reads its state vocabulary off the CLI display module.** `Web::Helpers` and `Web::IssuesFilter` resolved `Dashboard::ACTIVE_STATES` from `lib/autodev/dashboard.rb` at six call sites, so every dashboard, issues-list and KPI render depended on the legacy CLI tree being loaded. Invisible in production (the `load_autodev_config` initializer requires `lib/autodev`), but any test booted with `AUTODEV_SKIP_LEGACY=1` got `NameError (uninitialized constant Web::Helpers::Dashboard)` → HTTP 500: six controller test files passed only when the full suite happened to have loaded the constant first, and failed when run on their own. The list is now canonical on the model as `Issue::ACTIVE_STATES`, next to the AASM states that define the vocabulary, and the CLI resolves it through `Dashboard.active_states` — at call time, not load time, since requiring an autoloaded constant from an initializer would pin a stale class across Zeitwerk reloads. A test asserts the two are the same object, so the copy can't quietly come back and make `--status` and the dashboard disagree about what "en cours" means.

- **A Claude quota outage no longer freezes the tickets that don't need Claude (Autodev #46).** `AutodevPollJob` was gated on `UsageChecker#available?` and returned before a single dispatch pass, so an exhausted quota didn't just pause implementation — it froze every already-implemented ticket whose remaining path depended only on GitLab. Observed in prod on 2026-08-06 while validating #44: the poller fired every minute and executed nothing, with 21 MRs parked in `checking_pipeline` (a pipeline going green was never seen, so no review and no delivery) and GitLab closures going unnoticed for the whole outage. The gate moved down a level. New `Autodev::UsageGate` probes once per cycle and persists the verdict as a system `ActivityEvent(kind: 'usage')` — everyone downstream reads that state passively instead of shelling out to `danger-claude` themselves, which matters because `UsageChecker` is instantiated per call and its TTL cache never spans two jobs (probing per consumption point would have meant one Docker round-trip per tracked ticket per cycle). The verdict then gates **per pass**: `dispatch_new_issues`, `dispatch_discussions` and the `:retry_stuck` branch of `dispatch_retries` are skipped, while `dispatch_pipelines`, `dispatch_unassignment` (including #44's closure detection), `dispatch_done_unassigned`, `dispatch_error_recheck`, `:retry_errored` and `dispatch_infra_recheck` keep running — all GitLab reads, DB transitions and shell hooks. `:check_pipeline` keeps being dispatched, so `PipelineMonitor` holds the gate at the two points where it would call Claude: a green pipeline with `review_count == 0` (mr-review) and a red pipeline's code-fix branch. Both leave the ticket exactly where it is — the green deferral sits *before* `log_activity` so a long outage can't append a note per poll and blow past GitLab's 1M-char cap, and the red one returns *before* `update_stagnation_signature` so a cycle that never looked at the failure can't burn the stagnation budget and give the ticket up. `IssueProcessJob` carries a second, defensive gate for a job enqueued just before the outage; each of the three consuming actions leaves its row in a state the next cycle rediscovers. Everything fails **open**: no probe, an unreadable payload, or a verdict older than `max(2 × poll_interval, 600s)` all read as "available" — failing to *observe* the quota must never be what stops the pipeline. Visibility, which the ticket asked for: a warn banner on `/` for every signed-in user (not admin-gated — the pause holds up everyone's tickets) saying implementation is paused while tracking and deliveries continue, and `HealthReport#check_claude_usage` now reads the gate (written at probe time) rather than the end-of-cycle heartbeat. No throttle was added on recovery: `dispatch_discussions` already re-enqueues its rows every cycle in normal operation, so the burst shape is unchanged and `queue.yml`'s `threads` (`AUTODEV_MAX_WORKERS`, default 3) already serializes. New `test/services/autodev/usage_gate_test.rb`, `test/usage_gate_dispatch_test.rb`, `test/pipeline_monitor_usage_gate_test.rb` and `test/controllers/dashboard_usage_paused_banner_test.rb` (35 cases), plus new coverage on the poll job, the issue job and the health report. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-usage-gate-per-pass-design.md`.

### Changed

- **Both usage guides refreshed for this release.** Functional (`/help`): the *Déploiement review* section now leads with the case it was missing — you arrive with a ticket number, you type it in, Autodev finds the merge request (including when the branch name doesn't carry the number); plus the "hide MRs already tracked" shortcut and the fact that a deploy returns you to the same search. The dashboard section gains the **Quota Claude épuisé** banner, worded so it doesn't read as an outage: implementation pauses, tracking and deliveries carry on, nothing to do. Technical (`/admin/help`): new *Gate de quota Claude (par passe)* section with the per-pass matrix, the two `PipelineMonitor` switch points and why each guard sits where it does, and the fail-open rule; the *Quota Claude* health card now documented as reading `Autodev::UsageGate` rather than the heartbeat; the `/deploy_review` routes updated with `q` / `untracked` and `.auto_paginate`; the error catalog row for an exhausted quota rewritten; `PollDispatcher#run_once` corrected to `#dispatch`. Screenshot `19-deploy-review.png` recaptured on demo data to show the new search row.

## [1.0.0-alpha.44] - 2026-08-06

### Fixed

- **GitLab uploads are actually downloadable now — the image import shipped in alpha.43 never worked, and neither did the issue images in danger-claude's context (Autodev #37 follow-up).** Verified against `source.modulotech.fr` while testing #37 on prod: a project's `<host>/<project>/uploads/<secret>/<file>.png` path is **session-cookie only**, so a `PRIVATE-TOKEN` request gets `200 text/html` — GitLab's sign-in page — not the image. Two call sites built exactly that URL: `Autospec::IssueImageImporter#download_url` (new in alpha.43, so every imported ticket kept its original `/uploads/` links and created no attachment) and `GitlabHelpers::ImageDownloader#replace_reference`, which is far older — it feeds `fetch_issue_context`, so **every issue image handed to danger-claude has been a `[Image: … format non supporté (text/html)]` placeholder rather than the picture**, for as long as that path has existed. Both now go through one new `ImageDownloader.upload_api_url`, which targets the token-authenticated `GET /api/v4/projects/:url_encoded_path/uploads/:secret/:filename` (fully escaping the project path, since a nested namespace like `modulosource/powerpanne/powerpanne/core` would otherwise read as path segments and 404). That endpoint answers `application/octet-stream` for a valid PNG, so the `content_type.start_with?('image/')` check would have rejected the very bytes the fix goes to fetch: validation moved to magic-byte sniffing (`ImageDownloader.sniff_image_type`, PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP), which also keeps refusing the HTML sign-in page — the guard that stopped an error page being attached to a draft as a "screenshot" — and supplies the real MIME type stored on the attachment. End-to-end check against the real upload that failed in prod: `200`, 935 KB, sniffed `image/png`. New `test/gitlab_upload_download_test.rb` (10 cases) plus an octet-stream case on the importer; its test file was split into happy-path and degraded halves over a shared `ImageImporterSupport`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.43] - 2026-08-06

### Added

- **Importing a GitLab ticket now brings its screenshots along (Autodev #37).** `Autospec::GitlabImporter` only ever copied the issue's title and description, so every `![capture](/uploads/…)` reference in the body stayed a project-relative GitLab path — unresolvable from the autodev dashboard, and backed by no `AutospecAttachment` row, so the imported draft lost its screenshots outright. New `Autospec::IssueImageImporter` is the exact inverse of `GitlabSubmitter`: it matches the same `![alt](/uploads/…){width=…}` pattern the danger-claude context path already handles, downloads each image over the existing redirect-following `PRIVATE-TOKEN` GET (`GitlabHelpers::ImageDownloader.http_get_with_redirects` — one HTTP implementation, not a second one), attaches it to the draft as an `AutospecAttachment`, and rewrites the reference to `rails_blob_path` — which is precisely the string `GitlabSubmitter#rewrite_markdown` matches on when the draft later ships, so an imported image round-trips back to a GitLab upload instead of shipping as a dead local link (asserted by a dedicated test, since a drift between the two would fail silently). The import is deliberately **degraded, never aborted**: an unreachable image, a non-`image/*` response (a GitLab login page answers `200 text/html`), or missing credentials costs that one image — it keeps its original GitLab link and adds a line to `GitlabImporter#warnings`, which `create_from_import` surfaces as a flash on the draft page (new `web_autospec_import_images_failed` key, FR + EN) rather than bouncing the CSM back to the import form. Non-image uploads (`[debug.log](/uploads/…)`, no leading `!`) are left untouched: `AutospecAttachment` is an image surface. New tests: `test/services/autospec/issue_image_importer_test.rb` (15 cases) + `gitlab_importer_images_test.rb` (5 wiring cases).

- **Closing a ticket on GitLab now closes it on autodev too (Autodev #44).** A GitLab closure was only noticed at the very start of a processing run (`IssueProcessor#process`'s early return and the `clone_complete!` guard), so a row parked in `checking_pipeline` or `fixing_discussions` kept working — polling pipelines, fixing discussions, running reviews — on a ticket nobody wanted anymore. The check now rides along with `dispatch_unassignment`, which already fetched each active row's GitLab issue to test assignment and threw the `state` field away: detecting the closure therefore costs **zero extra API calls** (a test asserts the single read, so a future refactor can't silently double it). A closed ticket transitions to the existing `closed` state — not `done`, which means "autodev finished the work" and would blur the dashboard counters — with `finished_at` stamped and the `needs_attention` trio cleared, mirroring `IssuesController#close_issue!` minus the audit actor since nobody clicked anything. Closure wins over unassignment: a closed ticket is closed whether or not it is still assigned. New `activity_closed_externally` entry (FR + EN) records it on the ticket's activity log. Scope decision: only active rows are swept, so a ticket closed while parked in `pending` or `error` is noticed whenever it next moves rather than proactively, and reopening on GitLab does **not** reopen locally — `closed` stays terminal, reopened by hand via the existing reset action, exactly like the manual close. New `test/closed_on_gitlab_dispatch_test.rb` (10 cases).

- **A spent retry budget now gets a bounded second chance (Autodev #34, follow-up of #33).** Exhausting `max_retries` is the right outcome for a genuine code failure but the wrong one for a transient failure — a network blip, a GitLab/registry outage, the `git push` stale-info case from #33 — where the row sat in `error` forever once the cause cleared, needing a manual `UPDATE` (the orphan pattern #31 fixed for infra stagnations). New `PollDispatcher#dispatch_error_recheck` pass, running just before `dispatch_retries` so a grant is consumed in the same cycle. It does **not** reimplement the retry mechanics: it re-arms the spent budget (`retry_count` back to 0 plus a due `next_retry_at`) and lets the existing `:retry_errored` / `:retry_stuck` path do the work, labels and activity log included. Each grant costs one bounded attempt from the new `error_recheck_count` column (cap `error_recheck_max`, default 3) and is spaced by `error_recheck_backoff` (default 1 h) via `error_recheck_at`, both overridable per project — so a real code failure burns a few extra rounds over a few hours and then rests terminal instead of looping. One GitLab read per candidate decides whether it's still ours to retry (issue still `opened` *and* still assigned to the autodev user); a declined candidate still spends its attempt, so a closed-and-forgotten ticket can't make the poller call GitLab forever. The error is deliberately **not** classified: `JobClassifier` reads GitLab CI `failure_reason` values, not Ruby exceptions, so classifying here would mean a new brittle heuristic over `error_message` — the cap provides the bound instead. New migration `AddErrorRecheckToIssues` (`if_not_exists`-aware, like every other) and `test/error_recheck_dispatch_test.rb` (13 cases over the candidate query, the re-arm, and the worth-it gate).

### Changed

- **Refreshed both usage guides for this release.** `docs/usage/autodev-functional-usage.md` (`/help`) and `autodev-technical-usage.md` (`/admin/help`): the *Clôs* tab and *Clôturée* state now say a ticket can also be closed from GitLab, with the note that reopening there does not reopen locally; *Réinitialiser* / *Réessayer maintenant* no longer claim the request always restarts from scratch (a request with an MR resumes at the MR check); the error card explains that autodev now retries by itself and grants a spaced second chance before a human needs to act; and the import note says screenshots come across with the text. Technical side: `max_retries` documented as a count of retries with its default of 1 and its inclusive comparison, the new `error_recheck_max` / `error_recheck_backoff` keys, the dispatch list grown to 8 passes with the closure detection folded into `dispatch_unassignment`, the `close` event marked as manual *or* automatic, `#reset` rewritten around `Issue.reset_for_retry!`, two new error-catalog rows, and the image rapatriation on the import route. Screenshots not recaptured — no screen layout changed.

### Fixed

- **The dashboard's reset button actually restarts the ticket, and the three reset paths now share one rule (Autodev #34, item 3).** Putting a row back where the poller will find it again takes two halves: a row that already has an MR resumes at `checking_pipeline` (polled unconditionally by `dispatch_pipelines`), and a pre-MR row restarts as `pending` **with a due `next_retry_at`** — `fetch_retryable` requires a non-NULL stamp, and `dispatch_new_issues` only rediscovers `labels_todo` while an errored ticket still carries `label_doing`, so without the stamp the row is orphaned in `pending` (task #26's pattern). Three call sites re-derived that rule and disagreed: the CLI `--reset` had it right, `Issue.recover_errored!` dropped the stamp on its pre-MR branch (so a rescued orphan moved from `error` to `pending` and stayed just as stuck), and `IssuesController#reset` dropped both — it forced `pending` with `next_retry_at: nil` for every row, meaning the "Réinitialiser / Réessayer" button an operator clicks from `/errors` left the ticket orphaned unless they also re-added the todo label in GitLab by hand, and sent MR-bearing rows into a full re-implementation over an existing MR. All three now delegate to one new `Issue.reset_for_retry!(scope, reset_budget:, clear_attention:)`. `reset_budget:` distinguishes the two intents that were previously conflated: an operator-driven reset zeroes `retry_count` (clean slate), while automatic startup recovery preserves it — handing out a fresh budget there would restart a genuinely broken ticket on every boot. New `test/models/reset_for_retry_test.rb` (11 cases) and `test/controllers/issues_controller_reset_state_test.rb` (4 cases) pin the resulting state, which no test covered before.

- **An errored ticket is retried again instead of being orphaned on its first failure (Autodev #34, prod cases from #36).** `max_retries` counts *retries*, but every call site compared it with a strict `<`, so a budget of N granted only N-1 retries. Since the baked default is `1` (`Config::DEFAULTS`) — and a *global* `max_retries` in `config.yml` is ignored with a deprecation warning (`IGNORED_GLOBAL_FIELDS`), so only a per-project override can raise it, and `Project#to_project_config` omits NULL columns — the common case granted **zero**: the first failure set `retry_count = 1`, `1 < 1` was false, `next_retry_at` stayed NULL, and the row was then invisible to every recovery path at once (`PollDispatcher#fetch_retryable` requires a non-NULL `next_retry_at` *and* `retry_count < max`; `dispatch_new_issues` only rediscovers `labels_todo` while an errored ticket still carries `label_doing`; `Issue.recover_errored!` — the one path that does accept a NULL `next_retry_at` — applied the same strict filter). Every first error therefore orphaned its ticket permanently, needing a manual `UPDATE` to restart, which is exactly what was hit in prod on #16076/#15971. All four comparisons now use the inclusive form (`retry_count <= max_retries`, and `>` for `exceeded_retries?`), so a budget of 1 grants one retry as its name says and startup recovery rescues the rows already stuck in that state. The three former call sites also each carried their own fallback and silently disagreed whenever the key was absent (`|| 3` in `IssueProcessor::ErrorHandler`, none at all in `PollDispatcher` — `nil.to_i` → 0, i.e. retries disabled outright); they now all resolve through one new `Config.max_retries(project_config, config)`, which can never return 0. The rate-limit parking path is unaffected: `handle_rate_limit` stamps `next_retry_at` without touching `retry_count`. New `test/retry_budget_test.rb` covers the resolution order, the inclusive boundary at each site, and the startup rescue.

- **AutoSpec draft errors no longer show the user a raw i18n key.** The flash banner renders `flash[:alert]` verbatim, but `AutospecDraftsController` handed it bare key strings — so a mistyped import URL literally displayed `import_invalid_url` on screen, and an unknown project `import_project_not_found` (same for `import_project_not_visible`, `import_issue_not_found`, and `project_unavailable` on the new-draft path). Five user-facing strings that never went through `Locales.t`, i.e. the exact failure mode the localisation rule exists to prevent. All five now resolve through `t_web` against new `web_autospec_import_error_*` / `web_autospec_new_project_unavailable` keys (FR + EN), the four import paths via a small `redirect_to_import` helper. The three existing controller tests that asserted the raw keys now assert the translated value, so the invariant is locked rather than merely fixed.

- **The AutoSpec editor no longer stays dead when a draft is opened by clicking through the app (Autodev #41 + #42 — one root cause).** `autospec.js` bound its `init` to `DOMContentLoaded` alone. The script tag lives in the page body (`Web::Views::AutospecDrafts::Show`), which Turbo Drive replaces on every in-app navigation and re-executes — but by then `DOMContentLoaded` has long since fired, so `init` never ran and nothing it wires existed: the Édition/Aperçu tab toggle (reported as "l'aperçu du ticket est cassé", #42), the capture dropzone and its click-to-pick fallback ("le drag&drop de capture est cassé", #41), and — unreported but more damaging — the debounced autosave together with its `localStorage` backup, so edits typed after such a navigation were dropped entirely. A hard reload of the same URL bootstrapped normally, which is what made the failures look intermittent and surface as two separate UI bugs. `init` is now wired on `turbo:load` **and** the cold-load path (`DOMContentLoaded`, or an immediate call when the DOM is already parsed), and made idempotent by a `data-autospec-wired` flag held on the workspace element rather than on `window` — the element is a fresh node after each Turbo body swap, so the flag clears exactly when the handlers do need re-binding, while `turbo:load` firing on the first load no longer double-binds every handler (which would have uploaded each dropped file twice). `wireChatComposer` was already called at script-execution time behind a `window.__autospecChatWired` guard, which is why the chat composer kept working and masked the scope of the breakage. New `test/autospec_js_turbo_bootstrap_test.rb` locks the three invariants (turbo:load wiring, cold-load wiring, idempotence guard) at source level — the suite has no JS runtime, same tactic as `DeployReviewFrameTest` for the sibling Turbo-semantics bug (#28).

## [1.0.0-alpha.42] - 2026-07-10

### Added

- **"Livrée (à vérifier)" cards now tell the CSM who to contact (task #35).** The yellow "Intervention manuelle requise" panel shown for `needs_attention` rows (`stagnation_pipeline`, `stagnation_discussions`, `review_limit_reached`, `review_failures_exhausted`) explained the problem but never said who could act on it. `WatchCards#render_cause_panel` now renders a common contact line (new `web_errors_contact` i18n key) under the cause body, gated on `row[:needs_attention]` — it does not appear on the plain `/errors` failures or on pending `needs_clarification` questions.
- **The AutoSpec drafts list now shows each draft's author on every tab (task #40).** The row meta line only showed "project · status", so on the "À valider" tab a validator saw drafts written by other users with no indication of who wrote them. `Web::Views::AutospecDrafts::Index#render_row_body` now appends "· Par <auteur>" (new `web_autospec_author` key) to the meta line on every tab, using a new private `author_display` helper (`draft.author.name`, falling back to `email`). Both data sources already eager-load the author (`includes(:project, :user)` in the controller and in `AutospecDraft.awaiting_vote_of`), so no additional query is introduced.
- **AutoSpec drafts can now be deleted before they're submitted (task #39).** `AutospecDraft#deletable_by?(user)` allows a hard delete (messages/attachments/approvals cascade via existing `dependent: destroy`) on any state that isn't `submitted` — `drafting`, `pending_approval`, and `rejected` are all "not yet validated" — for the draft's author, a project owner, or a platform admin; a `submitted` draft (already exported to GitLab) always refuses with a `draft_not_deletable` conflict. New `POST /autospec_drafts/:id/destroy` member route (declared as a raw route rather than a `resources` member action, since Rails folds member routes named `destroy` back onto the bare `/autospec_drafts/:id` path). The Show page gets a "Supprimer" button next to the retract form, gated on `deletable_by?` and guarded by a `data-confirm` prompt — never rendered on the `/autospec_drafts` index cards (each row is already an enclosing `<a>`, and a nested form there would be invalid HTML).

### Changed

- **Project owner is now a 100% manual designation, no longer derived from GitLab access level (Autodev #38).** `Autodev::GitlabMembershipSync` mapped GitLab access_level ≥ 40 (Maintainer) straight to the local `owner` role and reconciled destructively on every login and every nightly sync — on a project like PowerPanne this made every maintainer an owner, and owners gate AutoSpec draft approval, which doesn't hold. `role_for_access_level` now maps any access_level ≥ 20 (Reporter and up) to `contributor` only; the owner threshold is gone from the mapping. `reconcile_memberships!` excludes `owner` rows entirely — they're never downgraded or revoked by the sync, whatever GitLab reports (a manually-designated owner who loses all GitLab access stays owner until removed by hand, an accepted v1 gap). A new data migration (`ConvertProjectOwnersToContributors`) downgrades every pre-existing `owner` row to `contributor`, so no project has an owner post-deploy until one is designated. The project page's Team tab (previously a "coming soon" placeholder) now manages owners: lists current owners with a Remove button, and — for an admin or an existing owner (`can_manage_owners?`) — an "add owner" select restricted to the project's current members (contributors already synced from GitLab, no extra API call) with an Add button. New `ProjectOwnersController` (`POST`/`DELETE /projects/:slug/owners[/:user_id]`) enforces that an owner must be a project member and records `project.owner_granted` / `project.owner_revoked` audit entries.
- **Deploy a review environment for a merge request autodev never tracked (task #43).** New `/deploy_review` page (sidebar entry "Déploiement review"): pick a project from `current_user.visible_projects`, then see its open MRs (title, iid, source branch, author) via the GitLab API, each with a Déployer/Redéployer button whose availability is resolved lazily (same UX as the existing per-issue button). An MR already tracked by autodev (`Issue.where(project_path:, mr_iid:)`) is annotated with a "Suivie par autodev" badge linking to its ticket rather than hidden — redeploying is idempotent either way. `Autodev::DeployReview` needed zero changes: it already only reads `project_path`/`branch_name`/`mr_iid`, so the new surface drives it with a lightweight `Autodev::DeployReview::Target` struct instead of an `Issue` row. `Web::Views::DeployReviewFrame` (the lazy turbo-frame button) is generalized to accept an explicit `frame_id`/`src`/`submit_action`/`hidden_fields` instead of being hardwired to `issue_id`, with the original ticket-page behavior as the default — one component now serves both surfaces. New `DeployReviewsController` (`#index`, `#availability`, `#trigger`) gates `#availability`/`#trigger` behind `authorize_project!` (admin, or `project_path` among the user's visible projects) — the security boundary an arbitrary MR needs but a tracked issue gets for free via `issues_dataset` filtering. New tests: `test/controllers/deploy_reviews_controller_test.rb`, `Target`-based cases in `test/services/autodev/deploy_review_test.rb`, and a parameterized-frame variant in `test/components/web/views/deploy_review_frame_test.rb`.
- **Refreshed the user & technical usage guides for the tasks above (#35/#38/#39/#40/#43).** `docs/usage/autodev-functional-usage.md` and `autodev-technical-usage.md` (rendered live at `/help` and `/admin/help`): documented the new "Déploiement review" screen + sidebar entry, the manual owner management in the project Team tab (and replaced the now-false "GitLab team sync coming soon" FAQ), the draft author shown across the AutoSpec list tabs, the delete-a-draft action, and the contact line on delivered-review cards; added the new routes (`/deploy_review*`, `/projects/:slug/owners`, `/autospec_drafts/:id/destroy`) and the `deletable_by?` / `authorize_deletable!` details to the technical routes table and AutoSpec permissions matrix. Screenshots not yet recaptured (text-only refresh).

## [1.0.0-alpha.41] - 2026-07-07

### Fixed

- **Re-implementing an existing branch no longer dies at `git push` with `! [rejected] (stale info)` (task #33).** Surfaced while relaunching the task #32 tickets in prod: once a recette-KO reentry routes to a full re-implementation, `IssueProcessor` reuses the existing branch, rebases it on the (meanwhile-advanced) target (`rebase_branch_on_target`), then pushes — and `RepoOperations#push_with_lease_fallback` failed on the `--force-with-lease` retry. Root cause (reproduced deterministically, git 2.50.1 = prod): the **bare** `git push --force-with-lease` implies `--force-if-includes` since git 2.30, which requires the remote-tracking tip to be integrated into the pushed history; the rebase rewrites history so the previously-delivered remote commit is no longer an ancestor, and the check rejects the push as `stale info`. It bit specifically because the `clone_depth: 1` clone is `--single-branch`, so `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>` exists only via autodev's one-off fetch (a full clone tracks the branch and passes). The force retry now uses an **explicit-value lease** `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<sha>`, where `<sha>` is the fetched `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>` (`RepoOperations#remote_lease`): it keeps the anti-clobber guarantee (still refused if the remote moved past `<sha>` since our fetch) but does not trigger `--force-if-includes`, so a deliberate post-rebase overwrite of our own prior delivery goes through. Falls back to the bare lease when there is no remote-tracking ref (brand-new branch — no force needed anyway). This also covers `MrFixer`'s rebase-then-push path. New `test/repo_operations_force_push_test.rb` reproduces the rebased-branch push (asserts it succeeds) and asserts the lease still refuses a push when the remote genuinely moved since the fetch. Follow-up still open on #33: the error path leaves `next_retry_at` NULL, so a push that ultimately errors is not auto-retried (same orphan pattern as #26).

## [1.0.0-alpha.40] - 2026-07-07

### Fixed

- **A ticket sent back to *To do* with recette-KO feedback in an ISSUE comment is now re-implemented instead of re-delivered identically (task #32).** When a delivered ticket failed recette, testers re-added the `labels_todo` label and posted a new comment *on the GitLab issue* explaining what was wrong — but autodev looped and shipped the exact same MR. Root cause was in `PollRouter::ResumeHandler#reenter_destination`: a `done` issue whose MR is still **open** was unconditionally routed to `:pipeline_check` (→ `checking_pipeline` → `MrFixer`), on the assumption that reentry feedback lives in unresolved **MR review threads**. But `MrFixer#fetch_unresolved_discussions` and `PipelineMonitor#green_post_review` only read `merge_request_discussions` — never `issue_notes` — so with the feedback sitting on the issue there were zero discussions to act on: `green_post_review` took the `no_unresolved_discussions?` AASM guard straight back to `done` (`done_nominal`), re-delivering the identical MR. The only path that injects issue comments into the danger-claude prompt (`IssueProcessor#run_implementation` → `GitlabHelpers.fetch_full_context` → `IssueFormatter.append_comments`) was the `:reimplementation` branch, which was unreachable while the MR stayed open. `reenter_destination` now checks, for an open MR, whether a human posted a new issue comment **after the last delivery** (`GitlabHelpers.human_comment_since?(client, path, iid, issue.finished_at)` — non-system, not an `**autodev**` note, `created_at > finished_at`): if so it routes to `:reimplementation`, which reuses the existing branch/MR (`clone_and_prepare` reuse path + `MrManager#find_existing_mr`) and re-runs the implementation with the new comment in context, pushing onto the same MR. With no new issue comment (label re-added alone, or genuine unresolved MR threads) the existing `:pipeline_check` → `fixing_discussions` behavior is preserved, so the #11859 regression that path guards against is unaffected. `clarification_answered?` was refactored to delegate to the new `human_comment_since?` helper (same non-system/non-autodev/after-threshold predicate), behavior unchanged. New regression tests in `test/poll_router_reenter_test.rb` cover the new-comment → reimplementation route, a stale pre-delivery comment staying on `:pipeline_check`, and an autodev-authored comment being ignored.

## [1.0.0-alpha.39] - 2026-07-07

### Fixed

- **The deploy-review button no longer flashes in and vanishes on the issue page.** A follow-up to the Autodev #28 fix: with the frame now always rendered, the button appeared for a fraction of a second on page load and then disappeared. Root cause was in `Web::Views::DeployReviewFrame`: the component emitted the outer `<turbo-frame>` with `src` + `loading="lazy"` for *every* state — including the lazy-fetch **response**. Turbo 8 treats a response `<turbo-frame>` that still carries `src`/`loading="lazy"` as a fresh lazy frame: it blanks the just-injected content and re-navigates, leaving the frame empty (reproduced with a controlled A/B against the bundled `turbo.js` — a single `200` response with the button HTML, yet the frame ends up empty; omitting `src`/`loading` keeps the button stable). The lazy `src` + `loading="lazy"` now ship **only** on the frame the issue page embeds (state `:loading`); the resolved response (`:available` / `:no_job` / `:blocked` / …) renders a bare `<turbo-frame id>` + content, the canonical Turbo lazy-frame pattern. `GET /issues/:id/deploy_review` itself was always `200`/correct — this was purely the client-side frame swap. New `test/components/web/views/deploy_review_frame_test.rb` asserts the `:loading` frame carries `src`/`loading` and the resolved frames omit them.

## [1.0.0-alpha.38] - 2026-07-06

### Changed

- **Usage-doc screenshots regenerated** to match the alpha.37 UI: the ticket author's name on the requests list, the failing-job detail (`deploy_review (script_failure)`) on the *Livrée (à vérifier)* card, and the deploy-review button on the issue-detail page (enabled on a branched issue, disabled-with-reason on a branch-less one). Also fixed the functional-guide caption that still referred to the removed `/errors` page (its concerns are now the `/issues` card tabs).

## [1.0.0-alpha.37] - 2026-07-06

### Added

- **The ticket author's name is now shown next to every request on `/issues`, across all tabs (task #27).** Each request already carried an opaque `issue_author_id`, but nothing on the page told a user *who* filed it — the errors/waiting/delivered_review watch cards even rendered the raw id (`#7`) in the "requested by" line. The GitLab author display name is available on the issue payload at ingest time, so a new `issue_author_name` column is populated from `gl_issue.author.name` in `PollDispatcher#find_or_create_issue` and surfaced everywhere requests are listed: the dense desktop table (under the title, next to the project path), the mobile stacked cards (in the footer), and the three watch-card tabs (in the avatar strip, with initials derived from the name). A shared `Web::I18nHelpers#author_display` falls back to `#<author_id>` for rows created before the column existed and to an em-dash when neither is known — no blocking GitLab calls from the view. New `web_issues_author` string (fr + en); `web_errors_requester` reworded to show the author name instead of the issue iid. Migration `20260706000001_add_issue_author_name_to_issues` (idempotent, `if_not_exists`). New tests: `test/components/web/views/issues_author_test.rb`, `test/services/autodev/poll_dispatcher_author_test.rb`.
- **The concrete infra blocker (failing job + reason) is now surfaced instead of discarded.** When a pipeline bails out on an `:infra`/deploy failure (`PipelineMonitor::FailureHandler#infra_skip?`), autodev only ever said "Echec infrastructure (en attente)" and, at stagnation, "les mêmes jobs échouent… intervention manuelle requise" — the *specific* failing job and its GitLab `failure_reason` were dropped even though they were already in memory. A real case: the blocker was `deploy_review` failing with a werf-download 404 (exit 127), invisible without digging through GitLab. `format_failure_detail` now builds a concise string like `deploy_review (script_failure)` (comma-joined for several jobs, with the job's GitLab URL appended when the API exposes it). That detail is (1) **persisted** on the issue in a new `attention_detail` column (migration `20260706000003_add_attention_detail_to_issues`, `if_not_exists: true`; cleared on reentry / manual reset / close like the sibling attention columns), (2) **threaded** into the `stagnation_pipeline` notification and the `activity_stagnation_pipeline` / `activity_pipeline_infra` activity lines via a new `%{detail}` placeholder (fr + en), and (3) **shown** on the dashboard needs_attention / delivered_review watch card (`web_errors_attention_detail`, fr + en). `attention_reason` is unchanged (`stagnation_pipeline`), so callers keying off the give-up reason are unaffected — the new detail lives only in `attention_detail`. Extended `test/pipeline_monitor_infra_stagnation_test.rb`, `test/locales_test.rb`, `test/components/web/views/delivered_review_card_test.rb`.
- **A ticket that stagnated on an INFRA/deploy failure is now automatically re-attempted once CI recovers, capped and backed off.** Previously a `stagnation_pipeline` give-up (`done` + `needs_attention` + `attention_reason: 'stagnation_pipeline'` + `label_done`) was terminal: `dispatch_retries` only targets `error`/`pending`, `dispatch_new_issues` only rediscovers `labels_todo`, and the sole resume was a human re-adding the todo label or a manual reset — so a run of MRs blocked on a broken shared CI/deploy job (e.g. the werf binary) stayed blocked long after the infrastructure was fixed (~17 MRs in the trigger incident). New `PollDispatcher#dispatch_infra_recheck` pass (wired into `dispatch_existing`) re-enqueues the still-open, under-cap, backoff-elapsed `stagnation_pipeline` tickets as `IssueProcessJob(:recheck_infra)`. `PipelineMonitor#recheck_infra_recovery` (new `pipeline_monitor/infra_recheck.rb`) re-classifies the MR's **current** head pipeline with the existing `pre_triage`/`job_classifier` — infra-vs-code is decided at recheck time on the currently-failing jobs, never a stored verdict, so this branch is independent of the sibling infra-detail work. Only a *recovered* pipeline (green / nothing failing) re-enters, via the exact reset `ResumeHandler#reenter_via_pipeline_check` already performs (back to `checking_pipeline`, `needs_attention` cleared, review/fix counters reset, label doing re-applied, plus `infra_recheck_count`/`infra_recheck_at` reset so a later stagnation starts from a clean recheck budget). A pipeline still failing on infra keeps waiting; one now failing on **code** is deliberately left untouched (a real failure to fix by hand); `stagnation_discussions` is never targeted. Two new `issues` columns (migration `20260706000002_add_infra_recheck_to_issues`): `infra_recheck_count` caps automatic rechecks (`PipelineMonitor::DEFAULT_INFRA_RECHECK_MAX`, default 5; overridable via `infra_recheck_max`) and `infra_recheck_at` spaces them out (`DEFAULT_INFRA_RECHECK_BACKOFF`, default 3600 s; overridable via `infra_recheck_backoff`) exactly like `next_retry_at` gates the error-retry path. Every non-recovery outcome records one bounded, backed-off attempt, so the recheck self-limits at the cap and can never loop — a never-recovering infra failure stays in `needs_attention` permanently. Preserves the "No blocked state" + stagnation safety-net design as a bounded, self-limiting recovery. New `test/pipeline_monitor_infra_recheck_test.rb` and `test/infra_recheck_dispatch_test.rb`.

### Fixed

- **The deploy-review button is now discoverable on every issue page.** A user reported "I can't find the button to deploy a ticket on autodev" (Autodev #28). `IssueShow#render_deploy_review` short-circuited with `return unless @issue[:mr_iid] || @issue[:branch_name].to_s != ''`, so on any issue that had neither an MR nor a branch yet — every early-lifecycle state (`pending`, `cloning`, `checking_spec` before the branch is created, `needs_clarification`) plus question tickets that never branch — the whole lazy `<turbo-frame>` was omitted: the Actions card showed only *Réinitialiser* + the transition form, with **no deploy button and no explanation**, leaving the user hunting. The gate is removed; the frame now renders unconditionally. Its own availability endpoint (`GET /issues/:id/deploy_review` → `Autodev::DeployReview#availability`) already handles the branch-less case — it returns `:no_branch` *before* building a GitLab client, so the frame costs one cheap local round-trip and shows a **disabled button + "L'issue n'a pas encore de branche"** reason (via `web_issue_deploy_review_no_branch`) instead of a silent gap. When a branch/pipeline with an actionable `deploy_review` job exists, the button resolves to an enabled *Déployer/Redéployer* exactly as before. New `test/components/web/views/issue_show_deploy_review_test.rb` asserts the frame renders both on a branch-less `pending` issue and on a `checking_pipeline` issue with a branch.
- **`test/services/autodev/deploy_review_test.rb` no longer fails when run on its own.** The 13 availability/trigger cases only passed when some earlier-loaded test file happened to have defined the top-level `GitlabHelpers` constant; run in isolation (`bin/rails test test/services/autodev/deploy_review_test.rb`) every probe hit `NameError: uninitialized constant Autodev::DeployReview::GitlabHelpers` — swallowed by the service's `rescue StandardError` and degraded to an `:error` outcome, so the expected `:available`/`:blocked`/`:running`/`:triggered` assertions failed. The latent dependency was introduced by the alpha.34 refactor that routed `DeployReview#field` through `GitlabHelpers.field`: `GitlabHelpers` lives in `lib/autodev` (required at boot via `lib/autodev.rb`, which the test env skips with `AUTODEV_SKIP_LEGACY=1`). `test/rails_helper.rb` now `require`s `autodev/gitlab_helpers` alongside the `autodev/locales` / `autodev/config` requires it already declares for the same reason — a light dependency (only `require 'time'`). Full suite unchanged (985 runs, 0 failures); the file now also passes standalone.

## [1.0.0-alpha.36] - 2026-07-03

### Fixed

- **A pipeline whose only blocking failure is classified `:infra`/deploy no longer polls `checking_pipeline` forever.** `PipelineMonitor::FailureHandler#infra_skip?` short-circuited *before* the stagnation tracker ran: once the single auto-retrigger was spent, an `:infra` verdict just re-logged `pipeline_infra` and stayed in `checking_pipeline` on every poll, with no counter, cap, or timeout. A failure that never clears — e.g. a broken shared CI deploy job — looped indefinitely: powerpanne/core#16081 accumulated ~7,646 activity events over 4 days (and 19 issues on that project were stuck the same way, ~563k of ~581k total activity events being this loop's noise). Its sibling #16079 only escaped because an extra `runner_system_failure` job pushed the verdict to `:uncertain`, which flows into the stagnation path and ends as "Livrée (à vérifier)" (`done` + `needs_attention`). `infra_skip?` now tracks the failure signature the same way the code-fix path does (`compute_pipeline_signature` + `update_stagnation_signature`) and, once the same infra job set has recurred `stagnation_threshold` times (default 5), bails out via `handle_stagnation(issue, :pipeline)` → `done` + `needs_attention` + `attention_reason: 'stagnation_pipeline'` — the exact end state a stagnated code failure reaches. Bounds the unbounded activity-event growth as a side effect. New `test/pipeline_monitor_infra_stagnation_test.rb`. Trigger ticket: powerpanne/core#16081.

## [1.0.0-alpha.35] - 2026-07-01

### Fixed
