- **`autodev --reset` no longer orphans the issues it resets.** `perform_reset` set `next_retry_at: nil` while flipping errored issues to `pending`, but `PollDispatcher#fetch_retryable` only re-enqueues rows with `next_retry_at IS NOT NULL`, and `dispatch_new_issues` only re-discovers issues carrying a `labels_todo` GitLab label (a failed issue still carries `label_doing`, which neither the error path nor `--reset` reverts). So a reset row sat in `pending` forever, never reprocessed — the same orphan pattern `Issue.recover_stuck_processing!` was fixed for in alpha.33. `perform_reset` now mirrors that recovery: a pre-MR error becomes `pending` **with `next_retry_at` stamped** (re-enqueued via `:retry_stuck`), and an error that already has an MR resumes at `checking_pipeline` (picked up by `dispatch_pipelines`) instead of re-implementing from scratch. Message reworded to "relancée(s)". New tests in `dashboard_reset_test.rb` cover the stamp and the with-MR path. Found while re-running the prod error backlog (task #26).

## [1.0.0-alpha.34] - 2026-07-01

### Added

- **Deploy-review button: guard against an accidental double-click.** The "(Re)deploy review" submit button now carries `data-turbo-submits-with`, so once the confirm dialog is accepted Turbo disables it and shows a "Triggering…" label for the duration of the (full-page) submission — a proportionate, confirm-safe guard against re-triggering the `deploy_review` job by double-clicking. Outcome of the task #18 analysis of `POST /issues/:id/deploy_review` concurrency: the action is **not** hardened server-side (no per-issue lock, no idempotency key) because the real risk is low (GitLab itself rejects a redundant play/retry, so there is no double deploy; `Audit.record!` fires only on the `:triggered` outcome) and its exposure is identical to the un-serialized `reset`/`transition`/`close` actions — singling it out would be inconsistent. The GET-probe + POST double-fetch is likewise kept: the POST's re-fetch re-validates the job state at click time and is the very thing that narrows the race. New `web_issue_deploy_review_submitting` string (fr + en); `Components::Button` gained an additive `data:` passthrough.
- **Pre-push size guard: oversized commits now fail fast with an actionable message instead of an opaque GitLab rejection.** When `danger-claude` committed a build artifact, DB dump or vendored dependency by mistake, the push was rejected by GitLab with `remote: fatal: pack exceeds maximum allowed size (50.00 MiB)` (an HTTP 500 dressed up as a `GitError`), the culprit files were only visible in autodev's own logs, and the issue dropped to `error` and kept retrying the same doomed push (trigger ticket: powerpanne/core#16079). `RepoOperations#push_with_lease_fallback` now runs `check_push_size!` first: it sizes exactly the objects a push would upload (`git rev-list --objects HEAD --not --remotes`, which is correct for both the initial push and an MR re-push) and, if their on-disk size clears GitLab's 50 MiB pack limit (`MAX_PUSH_PACK_BYTES`), raises an `ImplementationError` naming the largest offending files and suggesting a `.gitignore` fix — before the wasted round-trip. The message reaches the ticket via the existing error notification. The size probe is isolated (`push_object_sizes`) so a probe failure returns `[]` and never blocks an otherwise-valid push. The old blob-listing helper was refactored into a shared `collect_objects(work_dir, revs)`; `log_large_blobs` remains as the server-rejection safety net. New `test/repo_operations_push_size_test.rb` covers the object-range logic against a real bare origin + shallow clone, plus the threshold decision and message.

### Changed

- **Refactor: one canonical `GitlabHelpers.field(obj, name)` for reading GitLab API values, replacing ~half-a-dozen drifted copies.** The `obj.respond_to?(:f) ? obj.f : obj['f']` duck-typing accessor (gitlab gem object with readers vs. a raw Hash) was duplicated across `deploy_review.rb`, `gitlab_submitter.rb`, `autospec/chat.rb`, `pipeline_monitor.rb`, `pipeline_monitor/api_helpers.rb` (×6), `pipeline_monitor/stagnation_detector.rb`, `discussion_snapshot.rb`, both discussion formatters, and `gitlab_importer.rb` — and the copies had **diverged** (most tried only a string key, `pos_field` tried string + symbol, `deploy_review#field` string only). All now delegate to `GitlabHelpers.field`, which tries the reader, then a string key, then a symbol key, and — unlike the old `h['k'] || h[:k]` — preserves falsey values (important for `allow_failure: false`). Pure refactor, no behavior change intended. New `test/gitlab_helpers_field_test.rb`.
- **Default `dc_timeout` raised from 600 s (10 min) to 1800 s (30 min).** A 10-minute cap was too tight for an agentic implementation loop on a full feature: `danger-claude -p` was killed mid-work (`ProcessRunner#handle_timeout` → `ImplementationError`), the issue dropped to `error`, and each retry re-ran the same oversized prompt and timed out again — the most frequent failure mode on the `core` project (trigger ticket: powerpanne/core#16207). The baked `Config::DEFAULTS['dc_timeout']` is the effective global fallback (the field is in `IGNORED_GLOBAL_FIELDS`, so a `config.yml` global is ignored), and `process_runner.rb` still lets a per-project value override it. The dashboard project-edit form's "Défaut : N" hint was updated to match. For projects that need more headroom, prefer a per-project `dc_timeout` and/or enabling `split_implementation` (code + tests in two passes, each with its own budget) over pushing the global default higher.

### Fixed

- **Test suite: fixed an order-dependent flake where a leaked `error` issue polluted later tests.** `DatabaseTestHelper` (used by the non-transactional `Minitest::Test` DB tests) wiped `issues`/`activity_events`/`audit_logs` only at *setup*, so a test that left an issue in `status: 'error'` behind leaked it into whatever ran next — intermittently failing transactional tests that read global issue state (e.g. `HealthReportTest#test_issues_error_ok_when_no_issue_is_in_error` → `:warn` instead of `:ok`), depending on Minitest's random run order. The wipe was extracted into `wipe_business_tables` and is now also run from an `after_teardown` hook, so these tests clean up after themselves and no longer leak into successors. Verified by reproducing the failure on random seeds without the change and confirming a clean suite with it (task #25).
- **Functional help doc (`/help`): corrected the "Envoyer la demande" section.** The guide described a single **Créer le ticket** button that "proposes a destination" — but no such button exists. The editor shows the submit button(s) directly labelled by destination: project owners see two (**Envoyer à un dev** + **Envoyer à AutoDev**), other members see one (**Envoyer à un dev**). Reworded to match the real UI (`web_autospec_submit_human` / `web_autospec_submit_autodev`) and removed the phantom button. Reported by an AutoSpec user (Autodev task #22).
- **CI job logs with non-UTF-8 bytes no longer crash the pipeline fixer.** `PipelineMonitor::ApiHelpers#write_single_job_log` wrote the GitLab trace straight to disk with `File.write`, but the trace comes back tagged `ASCII-8BIT` (raw bytes); a job log containing accented characters or `€` raised `Encoding::UndefinedConversionError` ("\xC3" from ASCII-8BIT to UTF-8). Because the write failed on every pipeline-fix attempt, the issue was wrongly counted as `stagnation_pipeline` and delivered with an alert. The trace is now force-decoded as UTF-8 and scrubbed (`trace.dup.force_encoding('UTF-8').scrub`) before writing, so valid UTF-8 round-trips intact and genuinely invalid bytes become `�` instead of crashing. Trigger ticket: powerpanne/core#15819. New tests in `pipeline_monitor_write_job_logs_test.rb` cover an ASCII-8BIT trace with accented bytes and one with a lone invalid byte.

## [1.0.0-alpha.33] - 2026-06-30

### Changed

- **The standalone "/errors" page is gone — its three distinct concerns are now three card tabs under `/issues`.** The old `/errors` ("À surveiller") page lumped together three unrelated situations behind one count: failed requests, requests waiting on a human answer, and delivered requests flagged for a manual check. Each now has its own tab, rendered with the same rich watch cards (cause panel + plain-language message + context CTA) the errors page used: `/issues?tab=errors` (status `error` → *Réessayer*), `/issues?tab=waiting` (`needs_clarification` → *Voir la question*), and the new `/issues?tab=delivered_review` (`done` + `needs_attention` **or** a non-null `post_completion_error` → *Clôturer*). The "Clôturer" CTA is gated like `IssuesController#close` (only shown to project collaborators + admins via a per-page `closable_ids` set; everyone else sees just "Voir le détail"), and `#close` now honors a `safe_return_to` so a close bounces back to the delivered-review list for serial triage. The dashboard KPI grid replaces the single red "À surveiller" card with a renamed **Erreurs** card (→ `tab=errors`) and a new **Livrée (à vérifier)** card (→ `tab=delivered_review`); the sidebar/top-nav "Erreurs" entries and the dashboard's red failure banner all repoint to `/issues?tab=errors` (the banner now counts only retryable `error` rows). The sidebar lists all three watch tabs as quick links with their own count badges — **Erreurs** (red) plus **Question en attente** and **Livrée (à vérifier)** (yellow warn tone) — and highlights the matching entry when its tab is open; the badge counts come from a shared `Web::Views::Base#sidebar_counts`. The card copy/CTA logic moved verbatim into a shared `Web::Views::Concerns::WatchCards` mixin; the `Web::Views::Errors` view, `ErrorsController`, and the `/errors` route were removed (the URL now 404s), along with the now-unused `to_watch`/`web_kpi_to_watch`/`web_nav_to_watch`/`web_errors_{title,subtitle,none,banner_*}` keys. New `web_tab_delivered_review`, `web_kpi_{errors,delivered_review}`, and `web_errors_{action_close,confirm_close}` strings (fr + en). The errors-tab retry's `return_to` is now `/issues?tab=errors`. New `IssuesFilter#delivered_review_scope` (shared by the tab filter, its pill count, and the dashboard KPI). The alert panel on the *En attente* and *Livrée (à vérifier)* cards uses the yellow warn tone (matching their status badge) instead of the red technical-failure tone, which is reserved for the *Erreurs* tab (`.cause-panel--warn`, applied to any non-`error` card). Tests: new `issues_filter_delivered_review_test.rb` and `delivered_review_card_test.rb`; `errors_auth_failure_test.rb` rewritten to render via the errors tab; `needs_attention_dashboard_test.rb` and `issues_controller_reset_redirect_test.rb` updated.
- **Sidebar nav grouped into labelled sections.** The flat sidebar list is now organised into sections, each introduced by a separator line + an uppercase label: an unnamed top group (**Tableau de bord**, **Aide** — Aide moved up from the bottom), **Autodev** (Demandes, Erreurs, Question en attente, Livrée (à vérifier)), **AutoSpec** (Conversations), **Configuration** (Projets, visible to all as before), and an admin-only **Admin** section (Utilisateurs, Santé, Jobs, Aide technique) that is hidden — header included — for non-admins. Item ids are unchanged, so active-tab highlighting and the count badges still work. The horizontal fallback top-nav (`layout.rb`, shown on centered-layout pages) was realigned to mirror the same section order with group separators — `Tableau de bord · Aide | Demandes | Conversations | Projets` (admin links omitted; it carries no admin context) — replacing the old Dashboard/Erreurs/Toutes les issues links. New `web_nav_section_{autodev,autospec,configuration,admin}` strings (fr + en); the now-unused `web_nav_all_issues` key was dropped. `sidebar_watch_items_test.rb` extended to cover the section labels and the Admin-section gating.

- **"Activité de la semaine" bars now show their counts and a readable scale.** The dashboard sparkline rendered seven bars with no figures, and similar daily counts all maxed out at the same height (the only difference being "today"), so it was hard to read. Each bar is now topped with its numeric activity count (today's in the accent color), the chart reserves vertical room for those labels so a full bar plus its number fit, and a zero-activity day renders as just its "0" with no bar (instead of the previous 6px stub).

### Fixed

- **Dashboard: spacing between the "Brouillons à valider" card and the "En traitement" list.** The drafts-to-vote card only carried a top margin, so its bottom edge sat flush against the split grid below. Added a matching bottom margin so the two cards breathe.
- **Pre-MR issues no longer get orphaned in `pending` after a supervisor restart.** When the supervisor restarted while an issue was mid-implementation but before its MR existed (`cloning`…`creating_mr`, `mr_iid` still null), `Issue.recover_on_startup!` (`recover_stuck_processing!`) reset it to `pending` — but the GitLab label was already `label_doing`, so `dispatch_new_issues` (which only re-discovers `labels_todo` issues) never re-enqueued it, and `dispatch_retries` skipped it too (`next_retry_at` was null). The row sat in "En attente" forever with no Solid Queue job behind it. `recover_stuck_processing!` now stamps `next_retry_at: Time.current` on the pre-MR reset branch so `dispatch_retries` re-enqueues it via `:retry_stuck` on the next poll, independent of the GitLab label state.

- **Usage docs synced with the dashboard rework (`/help` + `/admin/help`).** Functional guide: the dashboard KPI list is now 6 indicators ("À surveiller" → **Erreurs**, new **Livrée (à vérifier)**); the left-menu section rewritten as grouped sections (Autodev / AutoSpec / Configuration / Admin, Aide moved to the top); the standalone "À surveiller" page section reframed as the three attention tabs (Erreurs / Question en attente / Livrée (à vérifier)) of the demandes list, with the "Intervention manuelle requise" / Clôturer case added; the demandes-list tab table updated (Échecs → Erreurs, + Livrée (à vérifier)); the AutoSpec drafts section gained its tab table (incl. **À valider**); the weekly-activity and post-completion FAQ wording refreshed. Technical guide: removed the `/errors` route row (folded into `/issues?tab={errors,waiting,delivered_review}`), documented the `/autospec_drafts ?tab=` values + `AutospecDraft.awaiting_vote_of`, and repointed the health-panel link + error-catalog reference off `/errors`. Both `date:` stamps bumped. All 18 screenshots re-captured from a local dev server (1440×900, light/FR) against seeded demo data, so they show the new sectioned sidebar, the 6-KPI dashboard, the Erreurs/Question en attente/Livrée (à vérifier) card tabs, and the tabbed AutoSpec list.

### Added

- **`/autospec_drafts` gains a tabbed view + sidebar quick-links for drafts.** Until now the only place to find drafts awaiting your validation was the dashboard widget. The drafts list is now tabbed on the /issues model: **Toutes** (default) · **En rédaction** · **En attente de validation** · **À valider** · **Rejetés** · **Approuvés**, each with a count badge. The status tabs filter your own drafts (`drafting` / `pending_approval` / `rejected` / `submitted`); **À valider** is the owner-vote set — `pending_approval` drafts on projects you own that still await your vote (same semantics as the dashboard widget). The sidebar's AutoSpec section replaces the single "Conversations" link with three: **En rédaction**, **En attente de validation**, and **À valider** (yellow warn badge), each carrying its count. The shared set lives in a new `AutospecDraft.awaiting_vote_of(user)` (2 queries, no N+1) that the dashboard widget, the tab, and the sidebar badge all call, so they never drift. The pill-tab markup was extracted from the issues view into a shared `Web::Views::Concerns::FilterTabs` mixin used by both lists. New `web_autospec_tab_*` strings (fr + en); the per-page draft counts feed the sidebar via `dashboard_kpis`. New tests: `autospec_draft_awaiting_vote_test.rb`, the tab-filtering flows in `autospec_drafts_html_test.rb`, and the AutoSpec sidebar links in `sidebar_watch_items_test.rb`.
- **"Issues bloquées" health check surfaces orphaned issues a green dashboard would otherwise hide.** The `/admin/health` page and `/healthz` showed all-green while 23 issues sat in `pending` with no job ever scheduled — the panels only watched infrastructure (poller, workers, queue, quota), never whether business issues were actually advancing. New `HealthReport#check_stuck_issues` encodes the invariant that every issue in a non-terminal, non-human-wait state must keep producing activity: it flags (`:warn`) any `pending` row older than the poller-staleness window (`max(poll_interval × poll_stale_factor, 900s)`) or any active-workflow row (`cloning`…`creating_mr`, `reviewing`, `fixing_*`, `running_post_completion`, `answering_question`) whose last `activity_events` row is older than `STUCK_ACTIVE_AFTER` (2h, overridable via `monitoring.stuck_active_after_seconds`) — using each issue's latest activity timestamp, so a long but live danger-claude run isn't flagged. Excluded by design: `done`/`closed` (terminal), `error` (own panel + backoff), `needs_clarification` (waits on a human), `checking_pipeline` (waits on an external pipeline, re-polled every cycle). The panel renders automatically on the health dashboard (new `web_admin_health_check_stuck_issues` string, fr + en) and bubbles into the overall status. New tests in `health_report_test.rb` (pending-stale, fresh-pending, live-vs-dead active issue, terminal/human-wait exclusions) and the CHECKS-keys assertion updated.
- **Redeploy the review environment from an issue page.** The issue actions card gained a "Redéployer la review" action that (re)triggers the branch pipeline's `deploy_review` job — *played* if the job is still manual (never run), *retried* if it already ran (new `Autodev::DeployReview` service, `app/services/autodev/deploy_review.rb`). Availability is resolved lazily: the button ships as a `<turbo-frame loading="lazy">` (`Web::Views::DeployReviewFrame`) whose `src` (`GET /issues/:id/deploy_review`) probes the relevant pipeline for a job named exactly `deploy_review`, so the GitLab round-trip never blocks the page render. The probed pipeline is the **MR head pipeline** when the issue has an MR (same pipeline `PipelineMonitor` watches — this catches detached/MR pipelines a `ref=branch` query would miss), falling back to the latest branch pipeline pre-MR. Availability is status-aware: a still-manual job offers **Déployer** (play), a finished job (`success`/`failed`/`canceled`) offers **Redéployer** (retry), and a job GitLab can't act on yet renders the button disabled with the reason — `created`/`skipped` (upstream stage failed / incomplete), `running`/`pending`/`preparing` (deploy already in flight), or an unrecognised status — so the click never 4xxes into an error flash. When no job/pipeline/branch is found the button is likewise disabled with a one-line reason (the same reason map drives both the disabled caption and the POST flash). Triggering posts to `POST /issues/:id/deploy_review` (targeting `_top`), confirmed via the existing dialog, and reports back through a new transient flash banner (threaded through `Web::Views::Base`/`Layout` + `view_kwargs`, reusable by other actions). Open to every signed-in user; each trigger writes an `issue.deploy_review` audit row. New strings in `web.{fr,en}.yml`; new `Autodev::DeployReview` unit test and `issues_controller_deploy_review_test.rb`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.32] - 2026-06-29

### Changed

- **"Réessayer maintenant" on the errors page now comes back to the errors page (mass retry).** The retry button used to redirect to the reset issue's detail page, so retrying several failed issues meant bouncing through each one's page and navigating back. The errors-page retry form now ships a `return_to=/errors` hidden field, and `IssuesController#reset` honors it (via a `safe_return_to` guard that only accepts in-app relative paths — protocol-relative `//…` and off-site URLs are ignored, falling back to the issue page) so the user lands back on the list and can immediately retry the next one. The issue detail page's own reset button is unchanged (no `return_to` → still lands on the issue). New `issues_controller_reset_redirect_test.rb` covers the default redirect, the `/errors` bounce, and the open-redirect rejections.

### Added

- **Dedicated "À surveiller" message when Autodev loses its Claude connection.** A Claude API authentication failure (e.g. `danger-claude -p` exiting on `API Error: 401 Invalid authentication credentials`) used to surface on the errors dashboard as the generic *Échec technique* card telling the user to "Relancez la demande avec « Réessayer maintenant »" — but retrying is pointless until the credentials are restored, so users churned the retry button to no effect. New `AuthFailureDetector` (mirrors `RateLimitDetector`) recognises the 401/"invalid authentication credentials"/"failed to authenticate" signature in danger-claude output and raises a typed `AuthenticationError`; the three workers' error handlers (`IssueProcessor`, `MrFixer`, `PipelineMonitor`) dispatch it to a `handle_auth_failure` that marks the issue failed **without scheduling an automatic retry** and logs an `auth_failure` activity entry instead of posting a misleading per-ticket error comment (same philosophy as the rate-limit path). The errors card now shows a dedicated message (`web_errors_explain_auth`, fr + en — "Autodev n'est plus connecté à Claude … Inutile de « Réessayer maintenant » … Prévenez l'équipe autodev") under the existing *Échec technique* title, and the contradicting "Réessayer maintenant" button is hidden for regular users — but kept for admins, who can re-kick the issue once they've restored the credentials. New tests: `auth_failure_detector_test.rb` (signature matching + non-matching cases), the `AuthenticationError` inheritance assertion, and `errors_auth_failure_test.rb` (view shows the dedicated message and drops the retry button, while a generic error keeps both).

## [1.0.0-alpha.31] - 2026-06-26

### Changed

- **"À surveiller" cards now tell the user what to do, not just what happened.** A user had to be told out-of-band that *Échec technique* means "ping the autodev team, it may be a bug" while *Question en attente* means "the CSM must answer Autodev on GitLab" — the on-screen wording didn't make the (different) expected response clear. Rewrote `web_errors_explain_failure` and `web_errors_explain_clarification` (fr + en) to spell out the next action: for a technical failure, retry then alert the autodev team (likely a bug) with the technical details; for a pending question, reply in a comment on the GitLab issue via "Voir la question". Synced the functional usage guide's "À surveiller" section (the two situations are now contrasted with their distinct expected responses, plus a per-action list) and added an FAQ entry for *Échec technique*. Re-shot `screenshots/03-errors.png` and `03b-errors-expanded.png` from the dev server (1440×900, light/FR) so the cards show the new wording.

## [1.0.0-alpha.30] - 2026-06-25

### Changed

- **Usage docs synced with the alpha.25→.29 features (refresh-usage-docs).** Refreshed both in-app guides (`docs/usage/`, `date:` bumped to 2026-06-25). Technical guide (`/admin/help`): added the new routes (`/projects/new` + `POST /projects`, `/projects/:slug/edit` + PATCH, `/projects/:slug/ticket_templates/*`); rewrote "Configuration d'un projet" for the now DB-backed per-project config (full editable-field table, `/projects/:slug/edit` access gates, DB-vs-YAML resolution via `IssueProcessJob#lookup_project_config` / `Project.runtime_configs`, `autodev:migrate_projects_from_yaml`); documented per-project ticket templates (`ProjectTicketTemplate`, `TicketTemplatesController`, the three-way `SystemPrompt#ticket_templates` branch, `autospec_drafts.ticket_template_id`), AutoSpec auto-evaluation on draft creation/import (task #15), the `/-/work_items/<iid>` import URL form; completed the recurring-tasks list (`LogJanitorJob`, `ReapFailedJobsJob`) and the primary-DB table list (`project_ticket_templates`). Functional guide (`/help`): documented the ticket-template picker + "Gérer les modèles", Autodev's automatic quality assessment on a new draft, the dashboard "Brouillons à valider" block for owners, and the work-item import URL. Added four screenshots (`15-project-edit`, `16-project-new`, `17-ticket-templates-list` — populated, `18-ticket-template-form`) to the technical guide (`17` also illustrates the functional guide's templates section) and re-shot `12-autospec-new` (now shows the per-project template picker); all normalized to 1440px-wide to match the existing pool. Registered screens 15–18 in the `refresh-usage-docs` skill's screen→file mapping.

## [1.0.0-alpha.29] - 2026-06-25

### Added

- **Per-project ticket templates — AutoSpec follows your conventions automatically (task #14).** A project can now define one or more named ticket templates (e.g. "Évolution", "Bug"), and AutoSpec structures drafts to follow them instead of the CSM pasting a template into the chat. New `project_ticket_templates` table + `ProjectTicketTemplate` model (`name`, `slug` — derived from the name, lowercase/accent-stripped, unique per project — `body` markdown, `position`); `Project has_many :ticket_templates`. CRUD lives at `/projects/:slug/ticket_templates` (`TicketTemplatesController` — index/new/create/edit/update/destroy), gated exactly like the per-project config editor (admin or a project collaborator; a missing project row is a 404), with Phlex views `Web::Views::TicketTemplates::{Index,Form}` and two entry points (shown to editors): a **"Gérer les modèles" button in the project page topbar** (`/projects/:slug`) and a card on the project config edit page. The structure is injected into the AutoSpec system prompt (`Autospec::SystemPrompt#ticket_templates`): when the project defines templates they're listed and AutoSpec is told to follow the matching one (and ask which kind when ambiguous); when it defines none, a generic **default structure** (Contexte / Comportement attendu / Critères d'acceptation / Notes, in the draft's locale via `web_autospec_default_template_body`) is injected as the fallback. On the new-draft form, a per-project template picker (`/autospec_drafts/new`) pre-fills the markdown body with the chosen template — driven by `app/assets/static/js/autospec_new.js` reading an embedded JSON map — and the controller applies the same body server-side (`#initial_markdown`) so it works with JS off. The form's template-slug field is sent as `template_slug` to avoid colliding with the route's `:slug` (project) param. New i18n keys (fr + en) for the templates UI, the picker, and the default structure. New tests: `project_ticket_template_test.rb` (validations, slug derivation/uniqueness/format, ordering, dependent destroy), `ticket_templates_controller_test.rb` (CRUD + admin/collaborator/outsider gating + 404s), system-prompt injection cases (project templates vs default block), and new-draft template-application cases in `autospec_drafts_html_test.rb`. _Follow-up: document the new screen + add screenshots to the usage guides (refresh-usage-docs)._
- **AutoSpec verifies a chosen ticket template, or proposes one when none is chosen (task #14 follow-up).** The new-draft template picker's choice is now persisted (new nullable `autospec_drafts.ticket_template_id`, FK to `project_ticket_templates`, nullified on template delete; `AutospecDraft belongs_to :ticket_template`). `Autospec::SystemPrompt#ticket_templates` now branches three ways: (1) **a template was explicitly chosen** → AutoSpec follows it and, on every quality assessment, explicitly verifies the ticket against it — listing the template's sections that are missing/empty and any extra ones (so the auto-eval from task #15 reports conformance, not just generic quality); (2) **no template chosen but the project defines some** → AutoSpec proactively proposes the best-fit template, says which and why, and offers to restructure the ticket (asking only when genuinely ambiguous); (3) **the project defines none** → the generic default structure (unchanged). The controller records the pick on create (`#chosen_template`, replacing `#initial_markdown` — markdown still falls back to the template body when JS is off). New tests: the chosen-template verify block and the propose block in `system_prompt_test.rb`, and create-records-the-template in `autospec_drafts_html_test.rb`.
- **AutoSpec auto-evaluates a new draft's quality on creation (task #15).** When a draft is created from `/autospec_drafts/new` with some initial content, the author now lands on the editor with AutoSpec's first turn already in the conversation: a quality assessment (title, type/priority/tags, context, expected behaviour, acceptance criteria, mockup) plus the next priority question to answer — the same result you got by manually sending *"Évalue la qualité du ticket."*, now automatic. `AutospecDraftsController#create` calls a new private `auto_evaluate_quality(draft)` that sends that prompt through the existing `Autospec::Chat` service (resolved in the draft's locale via the new `web_autospec_auto_eval_prompt` key, fr + en). It is **skipped for a blank-canvas draft** (no title and no markdown — nothing to assess yet) and when no Anthropic key is configured, and is **best-effort**: any failure is logged and never blocks or rolls back the draft creation. The assessment relies on the existing AutoSpec persona (no system-prompt change). Runs inline in the create request (consistent with the existing synchronous `#chat` action) so the evaluation is present on first render — there is no live-update plumbing for the conversation yet (deferred to the step 9c streaming work). New tests in `test/controllers/autospec_drafts_controller_test.rb` cover the content-present path (user prompt + assistant turn persisted), the blank-draft skip, and the failure-doesn't-block-creation guarantee.

### Fixed

- **AutoSpec chat: immediate feedback when sending a message (no more "frozen" 10s).** The chat turn is a blocking request (~10s on the Anthropic call; token streaming is the deferred step 9c), and there was no optimistic UI: after clicking Send nothing happened until the request returned and the page re-rendered both the message and the reply at once. Now, on submit, `autospec.js` immediately echoes the user's message and shows a pulsing **"AutoSpec réfléchit…"** indicator, clears the empty-state, and disables the Send button; the request then proceeds and the server re-render replaces the optimistic bubbles with the authoritative conversation. Implemented as a single delegated `submit` listener on `document` (guarded, so it survives Turbo body replacements — unlike the `DOMContentLoaded`-bound editor handlers). New `web_autospec_thinking` key (fr + en); `.autospec-thinking` pulse in `app.css`; composer form + messages container tagged with data hooks. Verified in headless Chrome (echo + indicator + empty-state removal + button disabled). Does not change the ~10s latency itself — that's the streaming work still to come.
- **AutoSpec import now accepts GitLab `/-/work_items/<iid>` URLs (not just `/-/issues/<iid>`).** GitLab's newer work-items UI links to `…/-/work_items/<iid>`, and `Autospec::GitlabImporter` only matched the legacy `/-/issues/` form, so pasting a work-item URL into the import screen raised `InvalidUrl` and bounced back with an error. At project level the `work_items/<iid>` number is the same issue IID, so `ISSUE_URL_RE` now matches both forms and maps them to the same `client.issue(path, iid)` call. New test covering the work-items URL form.
- **AutoSpec auto-evaluates imported drafts too (task #15).** `create_from_import` now also runs the quality evaluation, so a draft backfilled from a GitLab issue lands with the same first-turn assessment as a manually created one (best-effort; skipped without an Anthropic key).

## [1.0.0-alpha.28] - 2026-06-25

### Fixed

- **AutoSpec chat scroll — real fix for the page scrolling instead of the conversation (task #12, follow-up to alpha.27).** The alpha.27 fix (`min-height: 0` on the chat scroll div) was necessary but insufficient: the whole height chain was unbounded, so the editor still scrolled the entire page (topbar and editor column included), not the message list. Root cause: the global `.app-shell` is `min-height: 100vh` (indefinite), so `overflow:auto/hidden` never engaged and the body became the scroller. The editor `show` page now carries an `autospec-shell` class pinning it to `height: 100vh; overflow: hidden`, and `.autospec-workspace` gets `grid-template-rows: minmax(0, 1fr)` to bound its single row — so `main → workspace → columns` all have definite heights and both the conversation list and the editor column scroll **inside their own containers**. The topbar and composer stay fixed. Verified in headless Chrome on a faithful reproduction of the height chain: before → page scrollable (755px overflow), chat div not a scroller (client = scroll height); after → page not scrollable (0 overflow), chat scrolls internally (client 573 < scroll 1032). Holds on desktop and on the mobile tabs layout (≤960px, same check at 600px width).

## [1.0.0-alpha.27] - 2026-06-24

### Changed

- **Clarified where a request is written: in AutoSpec, not on GitLab (task #13).** A user reported reading the guide and concluding she had to write her ticket "on Git" — she had actually done the right thing (drafting in AutoSpec) but the doc made her doubt it. The functional guide's "Comment lui confier une demande" section led with "**Tout part de GitLab**" and buried the AutoSpec drafting path in a parenthetical; it now opens with the two entry points as equals — **"Vous voulez écrire une nouvelle demande"** (write it directly in AutoSpec, no need to write it on GitLab first — the simplest starting point) and **"Vous avez déjà un ticket GitLab"** (assign + label) — and a closing note that importing an existing GitLab ticket is an optional way to start, not a requirement (`docs/usage/autodev-functional-usage.md`, `date:` bumped). In-app, the new-draft screen (`GET /autospec_drafts/new`) gains an onboarding hint above the form stating the request is written here and that GitLab import is just an optional shortcut, with a link to the import screen (`Web::Views::AutospecDrafts::New#render_onboarding_hint`; new `web_autospec_new_onboarding_{title,body,import}` keys in fr + en). No screenshots needed (the prose/labels changed, not a screen layout that warrants recapture).

### Fixed

- **AutoSpec chat: long messages no longer get clipped horizontally (task #11).** In the conversation column (`.autospec-chat-col`, fixed 380px with `overflow: hidden`), a message containing a long unbroken token — a very long word, a URL, or a code line — used to overflow its bubble and be cut off. The classic flexbox `min-width: auto` was letting the message row refuse to shrink below its content's intrinsic width. Fix in `Web::Views::AutospecDrafts::Show`: `message_row_style` gains `min-width: 0` (so the row can shrink inside the column) and `bubble_style` gains `max-width: 100%` + `overflow-wrap: anywhere` (so long tokens break instead of overflowing). Messages now wrap inside the bubble; no horizontal page scroll. Holds on desktop and on the mobile tabs layout (≤960px).
- **AutoSpec chat: scrolling the conversation no longer scrolls the whole ticket (task #12).** Scrolling the message list used to move the entire page (the ticket editor on the left included) instead of just the conversation. The flex scroll container in `Web::Views::AutospecDrafts::Show#render_chat_column` had `flex: 1; overflow: auto` but no `min-height: 0`, so its default `min-height: auto` let it grow to the full height of all messages — it never became a real scroll container and the wheel event fell through to the page. Adding `min-height: 0` makes the message list scroll inside its own container; the "CONVERSATION AVEC AUTODEV" header and the composer stay fixed, and the editor column on the left no longer moves. The surrounding height chain (`.app-shell` → `main` → `.autospec-workspace`, already bounded — the editor column scrolls on its own) is unchanged. Holds on desktop and the mobile tabs layout (≤960px).

## [1.0.0-alpha.26] - 2026-06-23

### Added

- **Projects can be created and managed entirely in the DB — the `config.yml` `projects:` block is no longer required (phase 4 of task #9, completing the migration).** An admin can add a project from the dashboard (**Projets → Nouveau projet**, `GET /projects/new` + `POST /projects`): `gitlab_path` drives the derived `slug`/`name`, `default_locale` the per-issue language, and every per-project option is set on the same form as the phase-3 editor. Creation is admin-only (a non-admin's access derives from memberships on projects that already exist); on success the membership sync (`SyncGitlabMembershipsJob`) is enqueued so the new project's collaborators populate. Project **discovery** now reads the DB: `AutodevPollJob` and `bin/autodev`'s boot validation resolve projects via the new `Project.runtime_configs` (every `projects` row, unioned with any not-yet-imported YAML `projects:` entry as a soft fallback, same DB-then-YAML precedence as the phase-2 read path) instead of `config['projects']` directly. Consequently the supervisor no longer refuses to boot when `config.yml` has no `projects:` block, as long as the DB has at least one project. `Config::TEMPLATE` drops the `projects:` example (replaced by a note pointing at the dashboard / the still-supported `autodev:migrate_projects_from_yaml` seed rake), so a fresh config is much shorter. New tests: `test/controllers/projects_controller_create_test.rb` (admin gating, derived slug/name, membership-sync enqueue, invalid → 422), plus `Project.runtime_configs` coverage in `project_config_test.rb` and a DB-discovery case in `autodev_poll_job_test.rb`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.25] - 2026-06-23

### Added

- **Per-project config is now editable from the dashboard (phase 3 of task #9).** A project collaborator (contributor/owner) or admin can edit the per-project config from the project page: the **Configuration Autodev** tab gains an **"Modifier la configuration"** button (shown only to those who may edit) linking to a new `GET /projects/:slug/edit` form, which `PATCH /projects/:slug` persists onto the `projects` row. The form (`Web::Views::ProjectEdit`) renders one input per config column grouped into **Général / Exécution / Avancé** sections — text/number/textarea/tri-state-`<select>` per field type — driven by new `Project::CONFIG_STRING_FIELDS` / `CONFIG_INTEGER_FIELDS` groupings (single source of truth shared with the controller). Both actions are gated exactly like `IssuesController#close` (`ProjectsController#can_edit_project?` → admin or `contributor_of?` the project; a missing `projects` row is a 404, so editing only works once `autodev:migrate_projects_from_yaml` has seeded it). `#update` never mass-assigns — `project_config_params` builds the attribute hash field-by-field (so only config columns are writable, never `gitlab_path`/`slug`) and normalizes "unset" to `nil` so a cleared field falls back to the global default: blank string → `nil`, blank/invalid number → `nil`, the boolean select's "" → `nil` (tri-state unset/true/false), an empty list textarea → `nil` (one entry per line otherwise). The phase-1 model validations reject a bad edit, re-rendering the form with the errors (HTTP 422) instead of saving. Each field carries an inline hint stating its **baked global default** (`Défaut : 600` for `dc_timeout`, etc.) or a descriptive note where there's no fixed value (branch, labels, prompts, agents), and the form sits beside a right-hand **"Aide à la configuration"** panel explaining the `project > global > default` precedence and the list/boolean conventions (with a technical-guide link for admins). Relatedly, the project page's config/overview now reads the **DB row** (`Project#to_project_config`) when one exists — matching the phase-2 runtime read path — and only falls back to the YAML hash for a project with no row yet (e.g. configured but not yet imported). The `app:` block (structured/nested `project_app_commands`) is not editable here yet. New `web_project_edit_*` i18n keys (fr + en). New tests: `test/controllers/projects_controller_edit_test.rb` (GET/PATCH membership gating, scalar/list/boolean-tri-state persistence, blank→nil clearing, invalid-edit 422).

## [1.0.0-alpha.24] - 2026-06-22

### Deprecated

- **Per-project config under `projects:` in `config.yml` now emits a deprecation warning at load.** Every per-project key is read from the DB at runtime (task #9 phase 2), so editing one in the YAML alone has no effect once the project has a DB row — the dangerous silent case. `Config.load` now warns (`[DEPRECATION] per-project '<key>' in config.yml is now stored in the database…`) once per DB-backed key found under `projects:` (`Config::DB_BACKED_PROJECT_FIELDS` — the 20 config keys plus `app`; `path` is excluded as project identity). The warning fires at config load (the runtime boot path), **not** in `YamlProjectImporter` — the importer is the one place the YAML value is meant to be consumed, and warning there would be self-defeating. The YAML value still seeds the DB via `autodev:migrate_projects_from_yaml` and remains the runtime fallback for a project with no row yet; it stops being read entirely when the `projects:` block is removed (phase 4). New tests in `test/config_load_test.rb` (warns for `target_branch`/`parallel_agents`; `path`-only entry stays quiet).

### Added

- **The per-project "advanced" config keys are now columnized too.** Phase 1 left `model`, `effort`, `parallel_agents`, `split_implementation`, and the per-role agent overrides (`implementer_agent`/`test_writer_agent`/`mr_fixer_agent`) YAML-only because they were undocumented and unvalidated. Migration `20260622000001` adds the seven columns (two booleans, five strings; nullable, no default — an unset key stays NULL and is omitted by `#to_project_config`, exactly like an absent YAML key); `Project` gains validations (strings non-blank-when-set, booleans `true`/`false`-when-set) mirrored YAML-side by the new `AdvancedValidator` (split out like `AppValidator`); `YamlProjectImporter` imports them; and `Config::TEMPLATE` documents them under an "Advanced" sub-block of the `projects:` entry. Consequently every per-project key now has a column, so `IssueProcessJob::YAML_ONLY_CONFIG_KEYS` and the YAML-layering branch of `#lookup_project_config` are gone — a `projects` row is now the complete, authoritative config (YAML is only consulted as a fallback when no row exists). New tests across `project_config_test.rb`, `yaml_project_importer_config_test.rb`, `config_validate_projects_test.rb`, and `issue_process_job_test.rb`.

### Changed

- **Per-project config is now read from the DB at runtime (phase 2 of task #9).** `IssueProcessJob#lookup_project_config` no longer reads the `projects:` array of `~/.autodev/config.yml` directly — it now resolves `Project.find_by(gitlab_path:)&.to_project_config`. When a `projects` row exists it is the **complete, authoritative** config (the phase-1 importer mirrors the YAML into columns, so the cutover is behaviour-neutral on a DB that has been imported); with **no** row yet (e.g. a project added to the YAML before the next `autodev:migrate_projects_from_yaml` run) the full YAML entry is used — a soft transition that never regresses a project. Consequence to note during the transition: once a project has a DB row, editing a key in the YAML alone has no effect until the importer is re-run (the DB-side edit form is phase 3). New tests in `test/jobs/issue_process_job_test.rb` cover the branches (DB row authoritative incl. advanced keys, YAML fallback with no row).

### Fixed

- **The per-project `stagnation_threshold` override actually applies now.** It is a documented per-project key (a `projects` column, validated on `Project`, emitted by `#to_project_config`, advertised as "overrides global" in the config template), but both stagnation sites read only the **global** value — `PipelineMonitor::StagnationDetector#stagnated?` and `MrFixer::StagnationChecker#discussion_stagnated?` resolved `@config['stagnation_threshold']`, never `@project_config`'s. A project that tuned its own threshold was silently ignored and fell back to the global default of 5. Both now resolve `@project_config['stagnation_threshold'] || @config['stagnation_threshold'] || 5` (the standard project-over-global precedence used by `dc_timeout`/`max_retries`/`retry_backoff`). Surfaced while auditing the phase-2 read path for keys that are columnized but not consumed per-project. New tests: `test/pipeline_monitor_stagnation_threshold_test.rb`, `test/mr_fixer_stagnation_threshold_test.rb`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.23] - 2026-06-19

### Changed

- **Usage docs synced with the manual-close feature.** Both guides (`docs/usage/`, rendered at `/help` and `/admin/help`) now cover the `closed` state: the functional guide adds the **"Clôs"** issues-list tab, the **"Clôturer"** issue-detail action, the **"Clôturée"** + **"Livrée (à vérifier)"** state pills, and an updated "comment annuler" FAQ; the technical guide adds the `POST /issues/:id/close` route, bumps the AASM state count to 17, documents the `close` event (any state → terminal `closed`, membership-gated) plus the `emit_audit_log!` transition hook. Refreshed two screenshots (`02-issues-list` showing the new "Clôs" tab, `06-issue-detail` showing the "Clôturer" action). Dates bumped to 2026-06-19.

## [1.0.0-alpha.22] - 2026-06-19

### Added

- **Per-project config can now live in the DB (phase 1 of moving it out of `config.yml`).** New flat columns on `projects` (migration `20260619000001`) for the 13 per-project keys — `target_branch`, `labels_todo`/`label_doing`/`label_done`, `extra_prompt`, `dc_timeout`/`max_retries`/`retry_backoff`/`stagnation_threshold`, `clone_depth`, `sparse_checkout`, `post_completion`/`post_completion_timeout` (lists as JSON; the `app:` block already lived in `project_app_commands`). `Project` gains validations mirroring `lib/autodev/project_validator.rb` (positive-int fields, clone_depth ≥ 0, label-workflow completeness, string arrays, post_completion pairing) and a `#to_project_config` method that rebuilds the YAML-shaped hash (including `app:` from `project_app_commands`). `YamlProjectImporter` now mirrors these keys onto the columns on import (clearing dropped keys on re-import, same exact-mirror contract as app commands). **Additive only** — the runtime still reads the YAML hash (`IssueProcessJob#lookup_project_config`); switching the read path onto `Project#to_project_config`, the web edit form, and removing the YAML `projects:` block are later phases of task #9. Advanced keys (`model`, `effort`, `parallel_agents`, `split_implementation`, `*_agent`) stay YAML-only for now. New tests: `test/models/project_config_test.rb`, `test/services/yaml_project_importer_config_test.rb`.

- **Log housekeeping** so `~/.autodev/log` no longer grows unbounded. New `Autodev::LogJanitor` service + daily `LogJanitorJob` (recurring in `config/recurring.yml`, production only, 04:30): (1) prunes the date-stamped AppLogger JSONL files (`<log_dir>/**/*.jsonl`, already rotated daily) and rotation archives older than the 30-day retention window; (2) **copy-truncates** the single held-open files — the Rails `<env>.log` and the launchd-captured `autodev-stdout.log` / `autodev-stderr.log` — once they pass a 50 MB cap (copy to a timestamped archive, then truncate in place, because a rename can't rotate a file several long-lived processes hold open). Retention + size cap are baked constants (no new config keys, keeping with the minimal-config direction). New test: `test/services/autodev/log_janitor_test.rb`.

- Both in-app help pages (`/help` + `/admin/help`) now show a **table of contents** ("Sommaire" / "Contents") above the article, linking to each h1/h2 section (matching the docs' `toc-depth: 2`). `HelpDoc` gains `with_toc_data: true` (so headings carry `id` anchors) and a `toc` method that builds the contents **from the rendered body** rather than Redcarpet's `HTML_TOC` renderer — the two disagree on anchors for apostrophes/accents (e.g. `s'adresse` → `s-adresse` vs `sadresse`), which would have left ~1 in 6 links dead; reusing the body's own ids guarantees every link resolves. Rendered once in the shared `Web::Views::Help` shell. New i18n key `web_help_toc_title`. New tests assert every ToC anchor resolves to a heading id (`test/help_doc_test.rb`) and that the view renders the ToC (`test/help_version_test.rb`).

- Both in-app help pages (`/help` functional + `/admin/help` technical) now show the running Autodev version (`Autodev::VERSION`) as a muted `Autodev v…` badge in the topbar's right-hand slot — rendered once in the shared `Web::Views::Help#render_topbar`, so both pages get it. Not localized (brand + version is a technical token). New test: `test/help_version_test.rb`.

- **Manual close** — a project collaborator can now close a ticket in Autodev, and it moves to a new **"Clôs"** tab. New terminal AASM state `closed` + `close` event (fireable from any non-closed state), a membership-gated `POST /issues/:id/close` action (`IssuesController#close` → admin or `contributor_of?` the issue's project; outsiders can't see the issue so they get a 404), and a "Clôturer" button on the issue detail page (shown only to those who can close, hidden once closed). Closing clears the `needs_attention` flag and stamps `finished_at`; reopen via the existing reset action (→ `pending`). The poller already skips any status != `pending`, so a closed ticket is never re-picked even if its GitLab label/assignment is untouched — by design, close is Autodev-side only (no GitLab mutation). The `close` event is excluded from the generic "force transition" dropdown (`permitted_events_for`) so closing only happens through the gated button. New "Clôs" tab in the issues list (`IssuesFilter` TABS / `apply_tab` / `tab_counts`), a neutral `closed` status pill, CLI `--status` hides closed issues unless `--all`, and i18n strings in both locales. New tests: `test/issue_close_test.rb` (AASM + filter + dropdown exclusion) and `test/controllers/issues_controller_close_test.rb` (membership gating, needs_attention clearing, audit log).

### Security

- **The GitLab PAT no longer leaks into error messages, logs, or GitLab comments.** Autodev embeds the token in clone/push URLs (`https://oauth2:<token>@host/…`); on a git failure `ShellHelpers.run_cmd` put the full command — token included — into the `GitError`, which flowed into `issues.error_message`, the log files, and the error comment posted on the GitLab issue (a PAT was found in cleartext in months-old log files). New `Autodev::Redactor.scrub` masks `://user:pass@` URL credentials and bare `glpat-…`/`gldt-…` tokens; it's applied at the source (`run_cmd` before raising) and at the log sink (`AppLogger#write`), so the token can't reach `error_message`, the activity-log comment, or the log files. New tests: `test/redactor_test.rb`, `test/shell_helpers_redaction_test.rb`. **Operational follow-up: the exposed PAT must be rotated** — redaction stops new leaks but can't unpublish the token already sitting in old logs/GitLab comments. (The token is still passed in the git argv, visible via `ps` to a local user on the host — acceptable under the single-user/loopback trust model; moving it to an env-injected credential is a possible hardening follow-up.)

### Fixed

- **The "Failed jobs" tab no longer fills up with transient process-lifecycle failures.** When the box sleeps or the supervisor restarts, a Solid Queue worker holding a job is pruned/killed, and Solid Queue marks its in-flight job failed with `SolidQueue::Processes::ProcessPrunedError` / `ProcessExitError` — even though no work is lost (a pruned `check_pipeline` issue is re-dispatched on the next poll, and `AutodevPollJob` is recurring). Two fixes: (1) `Autodev::FailedJobReaper` + hourly `ReapFailedJobsJob` (recurring.yml, production) discard *only* those two infra error classes — genuine application failures stay visible in the tab; (2) the supervisor (`lib/autodev/supervisor.rb`) no longer tears all children down when one exits **cleanly** (status 0) — only a non-zero crash triggers teardown, since tearing down on every orderly stop just churns restarts and strands more in-flight jobs as pruned failures. New tests: `test/services/autodev/failed_job_reaper_test.rb` + supervisor clean-exit/crash coverage. (Separately surfaced while investigating: the GitLab PAT leaks into clone error messages — tracked as its own task.)

- **"Livrée" issues that actually need a human are now surfaced as "À surveiller"** instead of masquerading as clean deliveries. Autodev has four give-up paths that move an issue to `done` while a manual intervention is still required on GitLab — review-round limit reached, mr-review failures exhausted, pipeline stagnation, discussion stagnation — but nothing distinguished such a "gave-up done" from a clean delivery, so the dashboard showed both as a green "Livrée" and the "À surveiller" surfaces never listed them. New `issues.needs_attention` (boolean) + `attention_reason` columns (migration `20260618000001`) are set at all four give-up sites (reason matches the MR notification key: `review_limit_reached` / `review_failures_exhausted` / `stagnation_pipeline` / `stagnation_discussions`) and cleared on reentry (`PollRouter::ResumeHandler`) and on a manual reset (`IssuesController#reset`). Surfaced three ways: (1) the "À surveiller" page (`ErrorsController#errored_issues`) and the CLI `--errors` command (`Dashboard::ErrorDisplay`) now list these issues with a per-reason explanation; (2) a new `done_attention` synthetic status renders a **warn-tone pill** ("Livrée (à vérifier)") via `Web::I18nHelpers#issue_status`, distinct from the green clean-done pill; (3) the dashboard "À surveiller" KPI card + sidebar badge now count the full watch set (`dashboard_kpis[:to_watch]`) — error / needs_clarification / failed post-completion / needs_attention — and the KPI card links to `/errors` (the page that shows them all) instead of the narrower `/issues?tab=errors`. New tests: `test/needs_attention_dashboard_test.rb` (display status + watch count), plus coverage added to `test/pipeline_monitor_review_failure_test.rb` (give-up flags the issue) and `test/dashboard_errors_test.rb` (CLI lists needs_attention issues).

### Changed

- **Slimmed `~/.autodev/config.yml`.** Several global keys are no longer read from the YAML — `dc_timeout`, `max_retries`, `retry_backoff`, `stagnation_threshold` (their per-project overrides still apply; the global value now comes from the baked `DEFAULTS`), `pickup_delay` (uses its default), and `database_url` (vestigial — ActiveRecord uses `config/database.yml`). `web.enabled` is gone too: the web UI was already always started (`build_supervisor_children` never gated on it), so the flag only ever validated. Setting any of these — listed in the new `Config::IGNORED_GLOBAL_FIELDS` — now emits a `[DEPRECATION]` warning at load and is ignored. The generated config template (`Config::TEMPLATE`) drops all of the above plus the deprecated `labels_to_remove` / `label_to_add` project lines, so a fresh config is much shorter. The dead `-d` / `--database-url` CLI flag was removed (it set a key nothing read). The previous deprecation warnings (global `trigger_label` / `max_fix_rounds`; per-project `labels_to_remove` / `label_to_add` / `label_mr` / `label_blocked` / `max_fix_rounds`) were removed — those keys are now silently ignored. Per-project config is still YAML-only and unchanged; migrating it into the DB is tracked as a separate task. Tests updated (`config_load_test`, `config_validate_web_test`, `config_validate_labels_test`, `autodev_bin_test`) with new coverage for the ignore-and-warn behavior.

- The functional guide (`/help`) now shows each project's **real GitLab label names** instead of the hardcoded French descriptions *à traiter* / *en cours* / *livré*, which confused users whose projects use other label names (e.g. GitLab's default *To Do*). The guide's label references became `{{label_todo|à traiter}}`-style tokens that `HelpDoc.render(:functional, labels:)` substitutes with the configured `labels_todo`/`label_doing`/`label_done` values (falling back to the inline default when a project leaves them unset — the default also keeps the raw markdown readable on disk). Which project's labels are shown follows a B+C rule (`Web::Helpers#help_label_resolution`): nothing configured → defaults; all of the user's visible projects share one label set → that set, no UI; sets differ → the selected project's labels plus a project dropdown on the page (`web_help_project_selector`, GET `?project=`, validated against `visible_project_paths` so an arbitrary value can't surface a hidden project's labels). The technical guide (`/admin/help`) and the on-disk PDFs are unchanged — they already presented the names as configurable config keys. New tests: `test/help_doc_test.rb` (token substitution + technical-doc-untouched), `test/help_label_resolution_test.rb` (the three B+C branches + selected-path validation).

## [1.0.0-alpha.21] - 2026-06-17

### Added

- Usage docs now cover AutoSpec end-to-end (shipped at `1.0.0-alpha.18` but undocumented in the guides). **Functional guide** (`/help`) gains a "Rédiger une demande avec Autodev" section — starting a draft (new / GitLab import), shaping it by chat, attachments, choosing a destination (à un dev / à Autodev), and the owner validation flow — and the "Conversations" / "Nouvelle demande" entries are no longer labelled *(à venir)*. **Technical guide** (`/admin/help`) gains an "AutoSpec — rédaction assistée de tickets" section (the `AutospecDraft` AASM states, the §J permission matrix, the `Autospec::*` services, the project-briefing job + columns, and the new tables), plus the `/autospec_drafts*`, `/help`, and `/admin/help` routes in the routes table.

### Changed

- Synced the technical guide with the current code: Mission Control "Recurring tasks" now lists all four recurring jobs (`AutodevPollJob`, `SyncGitlabMembershipsJob`, `RefreshProjectBriefingsJob`, `clear_solid_queue_finished_jobs`); the on-disk primary-DB table list includes the AutoSpec + ActiveStorage tables. Functional guide: the `needs_clarification` status pill now reads "En attente d'une précision de ta part" to match `web.fr.yml`. Both guides' `date:` bumped to 2026-06-17. Four new screenshots added (`11-autospec-list`, `12-autospec-new`, `13-autospec-editor`, `14-autospec-approval`), captured at 1440×900 light/FR.

## [1.0.0-alpha.20] - 2026-06-17

### Fixed

- `/healthz` now returns **503 only on a real outage** (`down`: poller stale, no worker alive, database unreachable). A `warn` status (failed jobs, issues in error) now returns **200** — it's a degraded-but-up condition, and mapping it to 503 made an uptime probe fire permanently on routine operational states (e.g. tickets sitting in `error`). The JSON body still carries the `warn` status so a secondary, lower-severity probe can alert on it (e.g. BetterStack `required_keyword: "status":"ok"`). `HealthReport` is unchanged; only the HTTP status mapping in `MonitoringController` changed. Docs updated (`docs/observability.md`, technical guide).

## [1.0.0-alpha.19] - 2026-06-17

### Added

- System observability surface — a technical admin can now confirm Autodev is actually running, and external probes (Datadog, BetterStack) can scrape its health. **No outbound alerting** (no mail/Slack/GitLab) by design: Autodev exposes state, the external probe alerts. New `Autodev::HealthReport` service (`app/services/autodev/health_report.rb`) is the single, passive source of truth (no danger-claude shell-out, no GitLab call) computing six checks — `poller`, `workers`, `queue`, `claude_usage`, `issues_error`, `database` — each `ok`/`warn`/`down`, rolled up to a worst-severity overall status. Surfaced three ways: (1) unauthenticated JSON endpoints for monitoring (`MonitoringController`, skips the SSO gate like `AssetsController`) — `GET /up` (Rails liveness), `GET /healthz(.json)` (full report, **HTTP 200 if ok else 503**), `GET /healthz/:check` (one component); optional `monitoring.token` config gates them (Bearer or `?token=`, `secure_compare`), nil = open per the 127.0.0.1/NetBird trust model. (2) Admin page `GET /admin/health` (`Admin::HealthController` + `Web::Views::Admin::Health`) rendering the same report as status cards, with a sidebar nav entry. (3) **Poller heartbeat** — `AutodevPollJob` now emits the previously-dormant `ActivityEvent` kinds: `kind: 'poller'` (issue_id nil) on each cycle (with `usage_ok` flag + duration), and `kind: 'error'` on a cycle-level exception (then re-raises so Solid Queue records the failure). `HealthReport#poller` reads the latest heartbeat to answer "is the poller still ticking?" (stale after `poll_interval × monitoring.poll_stale_factor`, 15-min floor). System events (issue_id nil) are deliberately neither broadcast to the `/stream` SSE feed (`ActivityEvent#broadcast_to_event_bus`) nor counted in the weekly sparkline (`weekly_activity_counts`), so 5-minute heartbeats don't spam the live feed or drown real activity. New migration `20260617000002` makes `activity_events.issue_id` nullable (the model already declared `optional: true`). New config block `monitoring: { token:, poll_stale_factor: }`. Full reference in `docs/observability.md` (components, heartbeat, endpoints, curl/Datadog/BetterStack examples, config, and a TODO list: on-demand active probe, trend metrics, quota forecast); `docs/usage/autodev-technical-usage.md` updated (routes table + "Admin — Santé du système" section). New tests: `test/services/health_report_test.rb`, `test/controllers/monitoring_controller_test.rb`, `test/controllers/admin/health_controller_test.rb`, plus heartbeat/no-broadcast/sparkline-exclusion coverage.

### Fixed

- Dashboard "Activité de la semaine" sparkline silently showed all-zero bars in production despite thousands of daily activity events. Root cause: `activity_events.created_at` (and `issues.created_at`) are TEXT columns inherited from the pre-rails Sequel schema — the Rails migration's `create_table … if_not_exists: true` is a no-op on the existing prod tables, so the column type stayed TEXT. Because neither model declared `attribute :created_at, :datetime`, ActiveRecord treated `created_at` as a `:string` and serialized the timestamp via `Time#to_s`, storing `"2026-06-11 11:13:03 UTC"` — with a literal ` UTC` suffix. SQLite's `date()` returns NULL on that format, so `weekly_activity_counts`' `GROUP BY date(created_at)` collapsed every recent row under a NULL bucket and the per-day lookup `rows["2026-06-17"]` always missed → `[0,0,0,0,0,0,0]`. (Pre-rails Sequel-written rows were stored without the suffix, so the chart degraded only as AR-written rows accumulated.) Three-part fix: (1) `ActivityEvent` and `Issue` now declare `attribute :created_at, :datetime` so AR emits the suffix-free `'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS'` format `date()` understands — `Issue` already did this for its other timestamp columns but omitted `created_at` (a latent identical bug on the next AR-written issue). (2) New migration `20260617000001_normalize_created_at_utc_suffix` backfills existing rows (`UPDATE … SET created_at = replace(created_at, ' UTC', '') WHERE created_at LIKE '% UTC'`) on both tables, idempotently. (3) `weekly_activity_counts` no longer relies on SQLite's `date()` at all: it range-counts each day with a single `SUM(CASE …)` over UTC day boundaries computed in Ruby, which sidesteps the NULL-on-bad-format trap entirely (and naturally tolerates a stray ` UTC`-suffixed row, since a lexical range test on the `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` prefix is correct for every stored format). The method was previously untested; new `test/weekly_activity_counts_test.rb` covers the bucketing (oldest-first, today rightmost), the 7-day window boundary, the empty case, and the ` UTC`-suffix regression directly.

### Changed

- The dashboard "Activité de la semaine" sparkline now buckets events by **local calendar day** instead of UTC day. `created_at` is stored in UTC, so before this an event at e.g. 00:30 Europe/Paris time landed on the previous day's bar (and the whole chart shifted on a non-UTC server). `weekly_activity_counts` computes its 7 day boundaries in a configurable display zone — new optional `web.timezone` config key (e.g. `"Europe/Paris"`), falling back to Rails' `Time.zone` then UTC — via `ActiveSupport::TimeZone` so the boundaries are DST-correct. AR keeps storing timestamps in UTC; only the bucketing is zone-aware. New `Web::Helpers#activity_time_zone` resolves the zone. Covered by a test that files a 00:30-Paris event (stored ~22:30 UTC the day before) into today's bar rather than yesterday's.

## [1.0.0-alpha.18] - 2026-06-16

Phase D (AutoSpec) shipped end-to-end + project briefing addon.
12 steps from `docs/autospec.md` §C livrés, plus l'addon §M.

### Added

- AutoSpec project briefing (phase D follow-up). The chat system prompt previously fed Claude only the persona + the current draft state, giving the model zero context about the target project — generic suggestions, no awareness of conventions or lexicon. New `Autospec::ProjectBriefer` service shallow-clones each project's `staging` branch (fallback to remote HEAD via `git ls-remote --symref` when `staging` doesn't exist) into a `Dir.mktmpdir`, runs `danger-claude -p <briefing prompt>` with the work_dir as cwd, and stores the resulting markdown on `Project.briefing_text` + stamps `briefing_generated_at`. The briefing covers domain & purpose, stack, architecture sketch, glossary, conventions, and recent direction — written by Claude Code with full repo access including `CLAUDE.md`. Three new columns on `projects` (`briefing_text:text, briefing_generated_at:datetime, briefing_error:text`) via `db/migrate/20260616000001`. `RefreshProjectBriefingsJob` ticks every hour (`config/recurring.yml`: `0 * * * *`) — production block only; dev stays empty so the local supervisor doesn't shell out to danger-claude unsupervised. Per-project failures are caught + logged so one broken clone doesn't abort the run. `Autospec::SystemPrompt#build` now emits a 3rd `text` block (2nd cache-control breakpoint, `ephemeral`) between persona and draft state whenever `Project.briefing_text` is present — the briefing is cached alongside the persona, so the only uncached chunk on the LLM call is the draft state itself. The briefing is omitted entirely when nil (fresh project, danger-claude unavailable, tests), keeping the old 2-block shape. **Why staging not main:** the briefing should reflect what ships next, not what's been merged and is waiting for a release. **Why hourly:** keeps the briefing fresh without slowing down draft creation — the chat path is read-only. **Failure mode:** keep the previous `briefing_text` intact, stamp the error on `briefing_error` (a stale briefing beats no briefing). Test seam: `Autospec::ProjectBriefer.stub_invoker = ->(work_dir, prompt) { … }` class-level Proc bypasses the danger-claude shell-out. 7 new tests (4 ProjectBriefer + 3 SystemPrompt). Suite total: 727 → 734 runs.

- Phase D step 12 — GitLab issue import (the §A "very nice to have" backfill path; closes phase D). New `Autospec::GitlabImporter` service parses a GitLab issue URL (regex tolerates trailing slashes, query/fragment, deeply nested group/sub/sub2/project namespaces), looks up the matching `Project` row by `gitlab_path`, checks the user's access (admin bypass OR `contributor_of?(project)`), fetches the issue via `client.issue(path, iid)`, creates an `AutospecDraft` pre-populated with the issue's `title` + `description`. 4 typed errors (`InvalidUrl`, `ProjectNotFound`, `ProjectNotVisible`, `IssueNotFound`) — the controller maps each to a stable `flash[:alert]` key for redirect-back UX. New routes `get :import` + `post :import → #create_from_import` on `resources :autospec_drafts` (collection block); the before-action skip-list grew to include both actions so neither `load_draft` nor `authorize_author!` fires for collection-route requests. New Phlex view `Web::Views::AutospecDrafts::Import` with a single URL textfield. New "Importer depuis GitLab" secondary button on `/autospec_drafts` next to the primary "Nouveau brouillon" CTA so the path is discoverable. Test seam: `Autospec::GitlabImporter.default_client` class-level accessor mirrors `Autospec::Chat.default_client`. 13 new tests (8 service + 5 controller) — URL parsing variants, admin bypass, the four error-key redirects. 8 new locale keys (FR + EN) under `web_autospec_import_*`. Suite total: 713 → 727 runs. **Phase D is now complete — all 12 steps from autospec.md §C shipped.**

- Phase D step 11 — AutoSpec workflow d'approbation + GitLab submission, closing the end-to-end flow. Adds the permission matrix (autospec.md §J) as model predicates on `AutospecDraft` (`viewable_by?`, `editable_by?`, `submittable_by?`, `destination_choosable_by?`, `retractable_by?`, `votable_by?`) which controllers and the Show view query — the `authorize_author!` before-action splits into `authorize_view!` (show, looser: admin OR author OR owner+post-submit), `authorize_voter!` (approve/reject, requires owner+pending_approval), and the existing `authorize_author!` (everything else). 4 new controller actions on `AutospecDraftsController`: `#submit_for_approval` (with `destination` param + the `autodev`-is-owner-only gate from §J), `#retract`, `#approve`, `#reject` (with mandatory reason). New `Autospec::ApprovalRecorder` service records one owner vote per draft per iteration atomically — first rejection triggers `mark_rejected!`, quorum (every owner approved at the current iteration) triggers `Autospec::GitlabSubmitter#submit!` then `finalize!`, all inside one DB transaction so a GitLab failure rolls back the local approval row + the AASM transition (operator retries by clicking Approuver again). New `Autospec::GitlabSubmitter` service: streams each AutospecAttachment blob to a Tempfile, calls `client.upload_file(project_path, tmp_path)`, naive-substring-replaces every local `/rails/active_storage/...` URL in the draft markdown with the returned GitLab `/uploads/...` URL, then `client.create_issue(project_path, title, description:, labels:)` — `labels_todo` from the matching project_config entry is sent only when `destination == 'autodev'`. Stamps `gitlab_issue_iid` + `gitlab_issue_url` + `submitted_at`. Test seam: `Autospec::GitlabSubmitter.disabled = true` class-level flag makes `#submit!` a no-op so the broader workflow tests don't need to stub the gitlab gem (the submitter's own tests inject `client:` via constructor instead). Show view gains: "Créer le ticket" buttons in the topbar action slot (1 for contributor-author = `human` destination, 2 for owner-author with `human` / `autodev` choice), "Rétracter" button when pending_approval + author, and a new **approval banner** at the top of the editor body in every non-drafting state — warn-tinted for `pending_approval`, err-tinted for `rejected`, accent-tinted for `submitted`. When the viewer is a voter (owner + pending_approval + not yet voted at current iteration), the banner carries `[Approuver]` + an inline `[textarea required] [Rejeter]` form; votes cast at the current iteration are listed below with ✓/✗ + voter email + reason. New dashboard widget "Brouillons à valider" on `/`, listing drafts in `pending_approval` on projects the current user owns where they haven't voted at the current iteration — hidden for non-owners. 29 new tests across 4 files (9 ApprovalRecorder + 7 GitlabSubmitter + 13 controller + 4 dashboard widget). 12 new locale keys (FR + EN) under `web_autospec_{submit_*, retract, approve, reject, reject_reason_placeholder, approval_*}` and `web_dashboard_drafts_to_vote*`. Suite total: 672 → 713 runs.

- Phase D step 10c — drag-drop attachments + mobile responsive layout on the AutoSpec editor. New `AutospecAttachmentsController` nested under `resources :autospec_drafts`: `POST /autospec_drafts/:autospec_draft_id/autospec_attachments` (multipart, single `:file`) and `DELETE /:id`. Author-only auth (mirrors AutospecDraftsController). Server-side validation: content-type must match `image/(png|jpe?g|gif|webp)`, size ≤ 10 MB (returns `:content_too_large` — note the deprecated `:payload_too_large` symbol). Returns serialised `{id, filename, byte_size, width, height, url, markdown_snippet}` per attachment. AttachmentCard grid rendered server-side below the markdown editor (220 px `minmax(auto-fill)` columns, 112 px preview, ✕ delete button top-right, footer with filename + size + copy-markdown ⧉ button). Perpetual `autospec-drop-target` slot at the end of the grid so the affordance is always visible — clicking it opens a native file picker. Full-column dropzone overlay (`position: absolute; inset: 0; pointer-events: none`) toggled by JS via `data-active="true"` on dragenter (with descendant-aware depth counter) and back to `"false"` on dragleave / drop. JS handles: upload via multipart fetch, append to grid in place, DELETE on ✕, `navigator.clipboard.writeText` on ⧉ with brief "copied" state. Mobile tabs **Édition | Discussion** at ≤ 960 px — same CSS grid (single column at that breakpoint) but the workspace's `data-autospec-active-tab` flips `display: none` on the inactive column; the tab bar itself is `display: flex` only inside the same media query. All new components reuse existing dark-mode tokens (no new ones introduced). Width / height stay nil at the MVP — ActiveStorage's image analyzer needs `mini_magick` or `ruby-vips` (neither shipped); the UI degrades gracefully (size shown alone), `serialise` already passes the metadata through so adding the gem lights the feature up without code changes. Submission-time pipeline (GitLab upload + URL rewrite) is step 11. 9 new tests covering create (auth, forbid non-author, persists, serialised payload shape, missing-file, bad mime, oversized) + destroy (success, forbid). 9 new locale keys (FR + EN). Suite total: 663 → 672 runs.

- Graceful handling when the Anthropic API key isn't configured. New class predicate `Autospec::Chat.api_key_configured?` rolls up the existing resolution chain (`ENV['ANTHROPIC_API_KEY']` → `Web.config.dig('anthropic', 'api_key')` → `Autospec::Chat.default_client` test seam) into a single check. Three call sites: (1) `AutospecDraftsController#chat` short-circuits to **503 + `{ error: 'chat_unavailable' }`** instead of letting `Autospec::Chat#client` raise `ConfigError` → 500. (2) `Web::Views::AutospecDrafts::Show` accepts a `chat_enabled:` kwarg from the controller and grays out the composer textarea + send button + replaces the placeholder with a "Chat indisponible" notice when false; the markdown editor + meta-chips stay editable (no Anthropic dependency). (3) `Web::Views::Dashboard` renders an admin-only warn banner above the KPI grid pointing operators at the config (env var or `~/.autodev/config.yml`). Non-admins don't see it — they can't fix it. 4 new locale keys (FR + EN). 10 new tests: 4 for `api_key_configured?` (4 paths: ENV / Web.config / default_client / nothing), 1 controller 503 contract test, 2 composer-disabled HTML tests, 3 dashboard banner integration tests (admin sees / member doesn't / admin-with-key doesn't).

- Phase D step 10b — AutoSpec editor on the Show page. Two-column workspace (editor centre, max-width 820 px; chat right, 380 px desktop / 320 px tablet, collapses to single column below 960 px). Sticky toolbar with pill-style Édition|Aperçu tabs, format buttons (B / I / `</>` / H / list / quote / link 🔗), and a save indicator (idle / saving / error / locked) driven by a `data-state` attribute. Inline title input (transparent, 26 px / 600), meta-chip row for `type` + `priority` (selects with values from `web_autospec_meta_chip_{type,priority}_options` locale strings) + `tags` (free-text comma-separated, rendered as `#tag` pills), plus read-only status + iteration chips. Markdown textarea (mono 13.5 px, min-height 320 px, resize vertical) AND a server-rendered preview pane swapped via the `hidden` attribute. New `Autospec::MarkdownRenderer` service (Redcarpet with `escape_html: true` — user content, contra `HelpDoc` which renders trusted internal docs) used by both the initial preview render AND the `preview_html` field included in the PATCH JSON response (so autosave refreshes the preview in place). New PATCH `/autospec_drafts/:id` (`#update`) carrying `title` / `markdown` / `meta_chips`, scoped to `drafting` state — returns **409 + `{ error: 'draft_locked' }`** otherwise. `meta_chips` is doubly sanitised: strong-params on the request shape, then `slice(*Autospec::SuggestionApplier::META_KEYS)` against the model's known keys (defence in depth — keeps the JSON column from accumulating fields the model doesn't understand). Vanilla-JS module at `app/assets/static/js/autospec.js` (served via `/assets/js/autospec.js`, Propshaft load_path) handles: tab toggle, caret-aware format insertion, ⌘+B/I/K shortcuts, ⌘+Enter to flush autosave, 2 s debounced PATCH, localStorage backup keyed `autodev:draft:<id>:<field>` (per-draft so multi-tab editing doesn't collide), per-element label dictionary for the save indicator built once from initial server-rendered text via an FR/EN heuristic (locale doesn't change mid-page), and meta-chip inline edit (click chip → swap to `<select>` or `<input>`, commit on blur/Enter, Escape cancels). 26 new locale keys (FR + EN) under `web_autospec_{tab,format,save,meta_chip,title_placeholder,footer_hint,preview_empty}_*`. New CSS block `/* ── AutoSpec editor workspace ──` in `app/assets/static/css/app.css` (workspace grid, chips, panes, save indicator dots, responsive breakpoints at 1279/959 px). 14 new tests: 6 for `MarkdownRenderer` (heading/emphasis/list/blockquote rendering, raw-HTML escaping, snake_case identifier preservation), 6 update JSON contract tests (auth, forbid non-author, persists title+markdown, persists meta_chips, drops unknown meta keys, conflict when not drafting, serialised response), 2 update HTML redirect tests (happy path + flash alert on locked draft). Drag-drop attachments + the mobile **Édition | Discussion** tabs layout + dark-mode polish remain step 10c; the explicit "Créer le ticket" submit transition remains step 11.

### Fixed

- CSRF token missing from every Phlex-rendered form on the dashboard (regression introduced silently in users-rollout PR3, surfaced when wiring AutoSpec's `POST /autospec_drafts` form). Root cause: `view_kwargs` in `app/helpers/web/helpers.rb` guarded the call with `respond_to?(:form_authenticity_token)` — but `form_authenticity_token` is **private** on `ActionController::Base`, so the default `respond_to?` (which excludes private methods) returned false and the helper silently set `csrf_token: nil`. Every view that depends on `@csrf_token` (Layout's `meta[name=csrf-token]`, the sidebar sign-out hidden input, the issues reset/transition forms, the new AutoSpec forms) therefore omitted the token. The sidebar sign-out form happened to be the only POST that still worked because `Users::SessionsController#destroy` calls `skip_forgery_protection only: :destroy` — the rest 422'd on any submission. Fix: pass `true` to `respond_to?` to include private methods.
