Verified end-to-end via Chrome DevTools MCP: `POST /autospec_drafts` with the meta tag + hidden input now redirects 302 to the draft show page instead of 422'ing.

### Added (earlier in this release)

- Phase D step 10a — wire the existing "Nouvelle demande" / "Démarrer" CTAs to `/autospec_drafts/new`. Four placeholders shipped as `coming-soon` in earlier phases now point at the AutoSpec draft creation form: the dashboard topbar action, the `/issues` topbar action, the project show topbar action, and the sidebar CTA card at the bottom. No new locale keys — `web_dashboard_new_request` and `web_sidebar_cta_button` were already in place. The `coming-soon` span + tooltip wrapper is removed at each site (and the bouton becomes a real link), so the affordance now matches the user expectation that AutoSpec IS the "new request" entry point.
- Phase D step 10a — AutoSpec frontend backbone. Four new actions on `AutospecDraftsController`: `index` (list current user's drafts, ordered by `updated_at`), `new` (form scoped to `current_user.visible_projects`), `create` (rejects project_ids outside the user's visibility, returns the operator to `/autospec_drafts/new` with an alert key), `show` (single-column layout: meta card + read-only markdown card + conversation card with messages + inline apply-suggestion buttons + composer form). `chat` and `apply_suggestion` now `respond_to` both HTML (redirect to show) and JSON (the existing 9c contract). Routes refactored to a `resources :autospec_drafts, only: %i[index new create show]` block with the two `:member` POSTs. Three new Phlex views under `app/components/web/views/autospec_drafts/` (Index, New, Show — Show carries the apply-button form per tool_call with `applied_at`-disabled state). Sidebar's "Conversations" nav entry flipped from `coming_soon: true` to a real link at `/autospec_drafts`. 29 new locale keys (FR + EN) under the `web_autospec_*` prefix cover the index/new/show pages, the 4 AASM status labels, and the composer chrome. 11 new integration tests in `autospec_drafts_html_test.rb` cover the HTML flow (gating + visibility scoping on index/new/create, forbidden on other-user draft show, full chat→apply round-trip). The 9c JSON tests now use `as: :json` explicitly since the actions respond to both formats. The two-column desktop layout (editor centre + chat 380px right), markdown editor with preview toggle, drag-drop attachments, and meta-chip editing target step 10b/10c — see `docs/design/spec_update/README.md` for the visual target.
- Phase D step 9 foundation — AutoSpec backend schema + models (cf. `docs/autospec.md` §A, §E, §F, §G, §J). Four new tables under `db/migrate/20260612000002..5`: `autospec_drafts` (status string column for AASM consistency with `Issue`, `current_iteration`, `meta_chips` JSON, nullable `destination` enum, GitLab pointer fields populated at submission), `autospec_messages` (Anthropic-shaped `role` + `content` + `tool_calls` JSON array — tool_results are synthesized on each chat round from `tool_calls[*].applied_at` stamps, never persisted), `autospec_attachments` (thin AR row anchoring a `has_one_attached :file`), `autospec_approvals` (one owner vote per draft per iteration, unique index on `(autospec_draft_id, user_id, iteration)` + matching model uniqueness validation, `reason` required when `action='rejected'`, `acted_at` auto-stamped). Four matching models under `app/models/autospec_*.rb`. `AutospecDraft` mounts AASM with 4 states (`drafting` → `pending_approval` → `submitted`, with `rejected` as a side branch) and 5 events (`submit_for_approval`, `retract`, `mark_rejected`, `resume_from_rejection`, `finalize`); `submit_for_approval` bumps `current_iteration` in a `before` callback so approvals snapshot the right window. Step 11 will add the orchestration service that records approvals and triggers `finalize!` / `mark_rejected!` when quorum or first-rejection thresholds hit. New `has_many :autospec_drafts` + `has_many :autospec_approvals_cast` on `User`; new `has_many :autospec_drafts` on `Project`. 32 new model tests cover validations, AASM transitions, JSON column round-trip, has_one_attached, and the DB-level uniqueness constraint on approvals.
- ActiveStorage enabled (`require 'active_storage/engine'` in `config/application.rb` + new `config/storage.yml` with `local` (Disk under `<Rails.root>/storage/`) and `test` (Disk under `<Rails.root>/tmp/storage/`) services + `config.active_storage.service` set per env). New migration `db/migrate/20260612000001_create_active_storage_tables.rb` creates the standard `active_storage_{blobs,attachments,variant_records}` schema (inlined from Rails 8.1's railtie template because our minimal railtie set doesn't ship the `bin/rails active_storage:install` command — same reason the rest of the migrations are hand-written rather than generator-driven). `if_not_exists: true` everywhere so the `auto_migrate.rb` initializer is a no-op against existing DBs. Backup story documented in `production.rb`: rsync/snapshot `<Rails.root>/storage/` alongside `~/.autodev/`.
- `test/rails_helper.rb` cleanup `TABLES` list extended with the seven new tables, ordered child-before-parent so SQLite's default-on FK enforcement does not reject the teardown DELETE chain.
- Phase D step 9c — HTTP surface for the AutoSpec chat loop: `POST /autospec_drafts/:id/chat` and `POST /autospec_drafts/:id/apply_suggestion`, both author-only (403 for non-author, redirect to sign-in if anonymous). `AutospecDraftsController` is the only handler; the apply_suggestion action returns 200 + the updated draft JSON, 409 on re-apply (idempotent via `applied_at`), 404 if the `tool_use_id` isn't on the message. Chat returns 200 + the assistant message JSON (token-level SSE deferred — see controller comment + autospec.md §L for the deferral rationale; the synchronous JSON path lets step 10 wire UI without committing to a streaming protocol prematurely). New `Autospec::Chat.default_client` class accessor provides the test injection seam — the controller can't pass `client:` through to the service constructor, so the test sets the class hook in setup and clears it in teardown. New `Autospec::SuggestionApplier` service dispatches by tool name (hash table over the 4 §G tools), wraps the markdown patch path through `Autospec::MarkdownPatcher`, and stamps `applied_at` via `tool_calls_will_change!` (the `:json` AR attribute does shallow change detection — mutating an array element doesn't dirty the column). New `Autospec::MarkdownPatcher` implements the 4 §G operations (`append_to_end`, `create_section`, `insert_after_heading`, `replace_section`) with case-insensitive + whitespace-trimmed heading matching and a `fell_back?` Result attribute the UI uses to surface the "section non trouvée, ajouté en fin" toast. 31 new tests (12 patcher + 9 applier + 10 controller).
- Phase D step 9 (second slice) — `Autospec::Chat` service wraps Anthropic's Messages API for the AutoSpec chat loop. `gem 'anthropic', '~> 1.4'` added to the Gemfile (loaded lazily inside `Chat#build_default_client` so tests inject a stub client without pulling the SDK). Four collaborators under `app/services/autospec/`: `Tools` (4 JSON schemas — `propose_markdown_patch` as default, `propose_full_rewrite` discouraged with mandatory `rationale`, `propose_title`, `propose_meta_change`; every tool carries a `summary` ≤50 char that drives the apply-button label), `SystemPrompt` (two-block payload — persona + tool guidance with `cache_control: { type: 'ephemeral' }` for the 5-minute Anthropic cache, draft state appended uncached so edits invalidate cleanly), `MessageBuilder` (translates the AR `autospec_messages` log into the API's user/assistant turn shape and synthesises `tool_result` blocks from `applied_at` stamps per autospec.md §G "strict feedback"), `Chat` (orchestration: persist user message → request → split text + tool_use blocks from the response → persist assistant message with `tool_calls` JSON). Default model `claude-sonnet-4-6` per autospec.md §A. API key resolved from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` then `anthropic.api_key` under `~/.autodev/config.yml`. 30 new service tests cover tool schema invariants, system prompt cache tagging, MessageBuilder edge cases (empty history, no-prior-tool-calls user turn, applied vs unapplied synthetic results, multiple tool_uses per assistant turn, assistant without text), and the Chat#reply persistence + request-shape contract using a stub client. Streaming endpoint + suggestion-applier (which stamps `applied_at` on a tool_call after the CSM clicks the apply button) remain step 9c.

## [1.0.0-alpha.17] - 2026-06-12

### Added

- New in-app help pages: `/help` renders `docs/usage/autodev-functional-usage.md` (open to all signed-in users), `/admin/help` renders `docs/usage/autodev-technical-usage.md` (admin-gated via `AdminApplicationController`). Both flow through the same pipeline: `HelpDoc.render(:functional | :technical)` strips pandoc artifacts (YAML frontmatter, `\newpage`) and rewrites image refs from `screenshots/X.png` to `/help/images/X.png`, then Redcarpet renders (GFM tables, fenced code, autolinking, strikethrough). The image endpoint is shared between both pages (`HelpController#image`, allowlisted filename pattern, `image/png` content-type, 1h browser cache) — screenshots are not sensitive and live outside the Propshaft pipeline since they're documentation assets, not application assets. The generic `Web::Views::Help` Phlex shell takes `content` + `active` + `title_key` + `subtitle_key` so a single view serves both pages. The sidebar gains an "Aide" entry (visible to everyone) and an admin-only "Aide technique" entry (icon `info`, `web_nav_help` / `web_nav_admin_help` locale keys); `web_help_title|subtitle` + `web_admin_help_title|subtitle` feed the topbars. New `.help-doc` CSS block scoped to the rendered article restores readable typography (h1/h2/h3 hierarchy, table styling, image framing, blockquote treatment, inline code chips). `redcarpet ~> 3.6` added to the Gemfile.

### Changed

- `docs/usage/` refreshed against v1.0.0-alpha.16. Functional guide: the "Se connecter" + "Tableau de bord" sections now describe the sign-out link as living at the bottom of the sidebar user strip (the floating top-right widget is gone). Technical guide: routes table gains a `delete /users/sign_out` row pointing at the new `Users::SessionsController#destroy` (with a one-liner explaining why `devise_for :users` doesn't emit the sessions resource here); the "Comportement temps réel" section documents the new client-side `pagehide` listener + the lowered `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL = 5` + the reference to `docs/autospec.md` §L for the deferred ActionCable + Solid Cable decision. All 11 screenshots recaptured against the current prod dashboard.

## [1.0.0-alpha.16] - 2026-06-12

### Added

- `docs/usage/autodev-functional-usage.md` and `docs/usage/autodev-technical-usage.md` — paired French guides with 11 screenshots captured against the prod dashboard. Functional doc is strictly for non-admin, non-technical users (no AASM names, no routes, no internal jargon); technical doc covers admin routes, `~/.autodev/config.yml`, CLI, AASM state machine, polling pass list, pipeline decision matrix, error catalog, and on-disk state. Both files compile to PDF via `pandoc --pdf-engine=xelatex` and are gitignored as build artifacts (`docs/**/*.pdf`).

### Changed

- Sidebar user strip now shows the signed-in user instead of a hardcoded `autodev` / `Support client` placeholder. The avatar uses the first letter of the email's local part (capitalized), the name line shows the local part itself, and a "Sign out" link replaces the role text. The floating `render_session_widget` in `Layout` is gone (and with it `SESSION_WIDGET_STYLE`, `SIGNOUT_BTN_STYLE`, `current_user_email:` on Layout, and the now-unreferenced `web_sidebar_user_role` keys in FR + EN). `Components::Sidebar.new` now takes `current_user_email:` + `csrf_token:`; every view that renders the sidebar threads them through. The sign-out form carries the hidden `_method=delete` and `data-turbo="false"` (Turbo Drive would otherwise intercept the submit and stall on the redirect). The button used to dead-end on `ActionController::RoutingError (No route matches [DELETE] "/users/sign_out")` because the User model only declares `:trackable, :omniauthable` — `devise_for :users` only emits sessions routes when `:database_authenticatable` is on, so `/users/sign_out` simply did not exist. New `Users::SessionsController#destroy` (skips the global `authenticate_user!`, `skip_forgery_protection only: :destroy`, calls `sign_out(:user)`, redirects to `/sign_in` with `:see_other`) is mounted at `delete '/users/sign_out'` in routes.rb. CSRF is skipped to match the behaviour Devise's own `SessionsController` ships with when `:database_authenticatable` is on — the hand-rolled form (POST + `_method=delete`) trips Rails' forgery check inconsistently, and the threat model of a forged sign-out (the user logs out) doesn't warrant the protection. The previous `render_session_widget` was wired to the same non-existent route and never worked.
- Dashboard KPI tiles are now clickable links. `Components::Kpi` gained an optional `href:` that, when set, wraps the inner Card in an `<a>` (`display: block; text-decoration: none; color: inherit;`). The five `Dashboard::KPI_DEFS` route to `/issues?tab=<active|pending|errors|waiting|done>` — `/issues` already has tabs covering exactly the same five groupings the KPI cards represent, plus search, pagination, date filters, and a mobile cards view. New `.kpi-link:hover .card` CSS adds a subtle lift on hover to signal interactivity.

### Removed

- `/list/:status` route, `ListController`, `Web::Views::List`, and the five `web_list_*` i18n keys (FR + EN). The page was orphaned (no view linked to it) and redundant with `/issues?tab=…` — it only filtered by a single AASM status, which `/issues` covers via tabs for the five user-meaningful groupings (pending / error / needs_clarification / done / active aggregate). The fine-grained per-AASM-state filtering `/list/:status` exposed (`/list/implementing`, `/list/pushing`, `/list/checking_pipeline`, …) is internal autodev plumbing rather than actionable user info, and was never linked from anywhere in the UI. Both `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/usage/autodev-technical-usage.md` lose their `/list/:status` row in the routes table.

- `.gitignore` widened from `docs/*.pdf` to `docs/**/*.pdf` so PDFs generated under `docs/usage/` and any future subfolder are also excluded from the repo.
- `Rakefile` now loads Rails tasks, so `bin/rails autodev:migrate_projects_from_yaml`, `bin/rails autodev:seed_admin`, `bin/rails autodev:sync_memberships`, and `bin/rails autodev:link_user` work directly. Previously `Rails.application.load_tasks` was skipped to avoid conflicting with the minitest `Rake::TestTask.new(:test)` — the phase C cutover (closed at v1.0.0-alpha.1) made that workaround obsolete, and the Rakefile-level workaround documented in `lib/tasks/autodev.rake` was painful to use in practice. New Rakefile pattern: call `Rails.application.load_tasks`, then `Rake::Task[:test].clear` + redefine the test task with the project's `test/**/*_test.rb` pattern. `rake test` still runs the full 520-test suite identically (0 failures). The obsolete invocation comment at the top of `lib/tasks/autodev.rake` is also gone.
- Detect another well-known dev-only failure on `/sign_in`: empty `projects` table. `GitlabMembershipSync` marks the user `disabled` when no project rows exist, so Devise refuses the first sign-in with 401 — and the symptom is only visible in the server log (`[gitlab_sync] WARNING: projects table is empty`). The page now renders a second banner (dev only) with the exact rake command to fix it: `bin/rails autodev:migrate_projects_from_yaml`. CLAUDE.md's "Local development" section gains the corresponding setup step.
- Surface missing Azure SSO credentials clearly in development. Previously, a fresh local checkout with no `azure:` block in `~/.autodev/config.yml` (and no `AZURE_AD_*` env vars) silently fell back to `stub-client-id` in `config/initializers/devise.rb`; the user only discovered the misconfiguration after clicking *Sign in with Microsoft* and bouncing off `AADSTS700016: Application with identifier 'stub-client-id' was not found`. Three changes: (1) new `Config.azure_stub_credentials?` helper mirroring the resolution order in devise.rb (ENV → YAML `azure:` block → stub); (2) `bin/autodev` prints a warning banner at boot when stubs are detected, with the exact env var names + redirect URI to register in Entra ID; (3) `/sign_in` page renders an inline error banner (dev only) with the same setup steps so the operator never gets to the Microsoft consent screen with broken creds. No bypass — local dev still uses the real prod OAuth flow. CLAUDE.md gains a "Local development" section documenting the setup end-to-end.
- Recurring jobs (`AutodevPollJob`, `SyncGitlabMembershipsJob`) no longer auto-fire when `RAILS_ENV=development`. The `development:` block in `config/recurring.yml` is now an empty hash, so Solid Queue's scheduler boots with zero recurring tasks. Workers + dispatcher keep running, so jobs enqueued manually still get processed. Motivation: a local `bin/autodev` was acting on production GitLab tickets while iterating on code. `bin/autodev` now prints a banner at boot in dev mode listing the disabled jobs and the `bin/rails runner` one-liners to trigger them by hand. Production behaviour is unchanged.
- `/admin/users` redesigned against the app's design system. The page used to render a flat HTML `<table>` with all-uppercase column headers, inline padding, and raw `"✓"` / `"active"` text under a thin top nav. It now uses the same `app-shell` + `Sidebar` + `Topbar` + `Card` chrome as the dashboard / issues / projects pages, with a CSS-grid `.admin-users-table` (avatar + name + email + GitLab handle + role pill + status pill with colored dot + per-project membership chips with owner-vs-contributor accent). All strings now go through `t_web` (new `web_admin_users_*` keys in FR + EN); the previously hardcoded "Admin — Users" / "EMAIL" / "NAME" / "active" labels are gone. Empty state moves into a `Card`. Mobile breakpoint stacks one user per card. The Sidebar gains a new "Users" entry (`web_nav_admin_users`) that points to `/admin/users` and only renders when `current_user.admin?` — wired by adding `current_user_admin:` to `view_kwargs` + `Web::Views::Base` and threading `admin: @current_user_admin` through each view's `render_sidebar` call. Defense-in-depth: the controller's `require_admin` gate still 403s non-admins who type the URL directly.
- Sidebar gains a second admin-only entry "Jobs" (`web_nav_admin_jobs`) pointing to `/admin/jobs` — the existing Mission Control — Jobs mount in `config/routes.rb`. Same admin gate as the Users entry; the URL was reachable before but only by typing it.
- `AssetsController` rewritten as a single catch-all `show` action on `/assets/*path` that resolves through Propshaft's load_path (digested URLs from `stylesheet_link_tag` and stable URLs hand-written in our Phlex layout both go through the same code path). Replaces three per-pattern routes (`/assets/turbo.js`, `/assets/css/:name.css`, `/assets/vendor/fonts/:name.woff2`) and lets Mission Control's CSS / JS (`/assets/mission_control/jobs/application-<sha>.css`, etc.) be served by the same controller without bespoke routes. Cache headers split by URL shape: digested URLs get `immutable`, stable URLs get `no-cache` so dev edits show up without a hard refresh. The AssetsControllerWiringTest collapses from 5 per-pattern tests to 4 (route mapping, nested-path extraction, digest-tolerant routing, load_path resolution).

### Fixed

- Dashboard freezing on repeated F5 / multiple open tabs, requiring a server restart. Root cause: each open browser tab parked one Puma thread on `Queue#pop(timeout: HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL)` in `StreamController#show`; `Queue#pop` does not observe TCP-level disconnects, so a tab that went away stayed parked until the next heartbeat tick caused a `write` to raise `ClientDisconnected`. With the previous 15-second interval and Puma's 10-thread pool, a few quick refreshes saturated the pool and froze every Rails request. Two mitigations: (1) a new `pagehide` listener in `Layout::APP_JS` calls `window.__autodevSSE.close()` so the browser sends a FIN immediately on F5 / tab close / cross-document navigation, releasing the server thread on the very next write instead of waiting for a tick; (2) `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL` lowered from 15 → 5 seconds as the safety net for silent client deaths (browser killed, network drop, machine sleep). The architectural fix — moving to ActionCable + Solid Cable, which would hijack the socket out of Puma's thread pool entirely — is deferred to the AutoSpec implementation; rationale and decision criteria captured in `docs/autospec.md` §L.
- `Web.config` (which holds `~/.autodev/config.yml`) reverted to `nil` after any code reload in development, because the loader fired in an `after_initialize` block that only runs once at boot, whereas `Web` lives in `app/services/web.rb` and is wiped by Zeitwerk's reloader along with its `@config` instance variable. Any post-reload request that read through `Web.config` then crashed — visibly during the OAuth callback, where `Autodev::GitlabMembershipSync.for_user!` raised `ConfigError (Missing GitLab API token)` despite `~/.autodev/config.yml` carrying a valid `gitlab_token`. Switched the loader to `Rails.application.config.to_prepare`, which still fires once at boot in production but re-runs after every reload in development. Updated the related comment in `config/initializers/devise.rb` so it doesn't point at the old `after_initialize` site.
- `/assets/css/*.css` 404'd in development with a bare `Content-Type: text/plain\nNot found` body — page chrome rendered unstyled and every page was effectively unusable from a fresh `bin/rails server`. Two compounding root causes: (1) `propshaft/railtie` auto-inserts `Propshaft::Server` into the middleware stack whenever `config.public_file_server.enabled` is true (Rails 8 default in development; unset in production where `RAILS_SERVE_STATIC_FILES` gates it); (2) `Propshaft::Server#call` requires a matching digest in the URL — `Asset#fresh?(nil)` returns false, so stable, hand-written URLs like `/assets/css/tokens.css` from our Phlex layout 404 without falling through to the router, regardless of whether the file exists on the load_path. Mission Control's `stylesheet_link_tag`-generated URLs (e.g. `/assets/mission_control/jobs/application-<sha>.css`) DID include a digest, so they hit a different failure mode (no matching Rails route once Propshaft was sidestepped). Fix: drop `Propshaft::Server` from the middleware stack (`config.middleware.delete Propshaft::Server` in `config/application.rb`) and consolidate every `/assets/*` URL onto the rewritten `AssetsController#show` catch-all that does the load_path lookup itself — digest-aware for MCJ's helpers, digest-tolerant for our hand-written URLs. Propshaft's railtie is still loaded (MCJ needs `config.assets.paths`); only the middleware is gone. Production was always on this code path (no static-files flag set) so this restores dev/prod symmetry.

## [1.0.0-alpha.15] - 2026-06-10

### Fixed

- `User.from_omniauth` raised `ActiveRecord::RecordInvalid (Validation failed: Email has already been taken)` on the very first SSO sign-in when the user had previously been seeded via `autodev:seed_admin`. The seeded row carries an email but no `microsoft_uid`; the existing `find_or_initialize_by(microsoft_uid:)` then missed and tried to insert a new row that collided on the unique-email index. Fix: fall back to a case-insensitive email lookup when the uid lookup misses, and attach the Entra uid to the existing row instead of duplicating. Hit during the alpha.14 verification on bobette as the OAuth flow finally completed end to end.

## [1.0.0-alpha.14] - 2026-06-10

### Fixed

- POST `/users/auth/entra_id` still 422'd "Can't verify CSRF token authenticity" after alpha.13's `assume_ssl` fix. The origin check now passed (confirmed by the warning's message text), but the masked-token-vs-session-`:_csrf_token` comparison kept failing under our Rails 8 + ActiveRecord::SessionStore combo. Rather than chase the deeper interop bug in `omniauth-rails_csrf_protection` 1.0.2, set `OmniAuth.config.request_validation_phase = nil` to skip the gem's verifier. The OAuth `state` parameter (generated by OmniAuth at request phase, validated on callback) is the canonical CSRF defence for the OAuth handshake; combined with `allowed_request_methods = [:post]`, the registered redirect URI in Entra, and the NetBird mesh trust model, the defence-in-depth posture stays reasonable.

## [1.0.0-alpha.13] - 2026-06-10

### Fixed

- POST `/users/auth/entra_id` from the alpha.12 `/sign_in` form 422'd with `InvalidAuthenticityToken` even when the CSRF token was correct. Root cause: the NetBird reverse proxy terminates TLS at `https://autodev.netbird.<tenant>` and forwards plain HTTP to Puma on `127.0.0.1:4567`. Rails saw `request.scheme = "http"`, so `request.base_url = "http://127.0.0.1:4567"` while the browser sent `Origin: https://autodev.netbird.<tenant>`. `verified_request?` calls `valid_request_origin?` which compares the two — mismatch → false → 422. Fix: `config.assume_ssl = true` in `config/environments/production.rb`. Flips `env['HTTPS']` to "on" on every request so `request.scheme` returns "https" and `base_url` matches `Origin`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.12] - 2026-06-10

### Changed

- POST-only OmniAuth request phase, via an interim `/sign_in` page. Reverts the alpha.11 `OmniAuth.config.allowed_request_methods = [:get, :post]` relaxation in favour of the canonical Devise + omniauth posture (POST-only, CSRF token required). New `SignInController#new` renders a standalone Phlex page (no layout chrome — the layout opens an EventSource to `/stream` which is gated and would redirect-loop in the browser console) with a single `<form method=post action=/users/auth/entra_id>` carrying a hidden `authenticity_token`. One click → POST → OmniAuth strategy intercepts → redirect to Microsoft. `EntraIdFailureApp.redirect_url` now points anonymous callers at `/sign_in` instead of `/users/auth/entra_id`. New locale keys `web_sign_in_title` / `web_sign_in_subtitle` / `web_sign_in_button` (FR + EN). Gating tests assert the redirect lands on `/sign_in` and that the page carries a POST form pointing at the omniauth path. 516 → 518 tests.

## [1.0.0-alpha.11] - 2026-06-10

### Fixed

- `/users/auth/entra_id` still 404'd via `Users::OmniauthCallbacksController#passthru` even with the path_prefix (alpha.9) and credentials (alpha.10) fixes in place. Root cause: OmniAuth 2.x ships with `OmniAuth.config.allowed_request_methods = [:post]` as a CSRF hardening default — a request-phase GET passes through the strategy middleware untouched. Our flow is GET-driven: `EntraIdFailureApp` returns a 302 redirect to `/users/auth/entra_id` and the browser follows with GET. Fix: set `OmniAuth.config.allowed_request_methods = %i[get post]` in `config/initializers/devise.rb` so both verbs route through the strategy. The defence-in-depth this disables is the gem-level CSRF check on the request phase; the OAuth `state` parameter (set by OmniAuth and validated on callback) is the canonical defence against forged sign-in initiations, and autodev runs behind a NetBird mesh so the attack surface is already narrow. Hit during the alpha.10 verification on bobette: middleware args were correctly populated (real Azure credentials + path_prefix) yet the GET still bypassed the strategy.

## [1.0.0-alpha.10] - 2026-06-10

### Fixed

- `azure:` config-file block silently ignored — Devise was registering the omniauth provider with stub credentials even when `~/.autodev/config.yml` had valid Microsoft 365 credentials in it. Root cause: `config/initializers/load_autodev_config.rb` populates `Web.config` inside a `Rails.application.config.after_initialize` block, which fires AFTER every initializer has run. By the time `config/initializers/devise.rb`'s `azure_config = … Web.config['azure']` read happens, `Web.config` is still nil. The Devise `omniauth_configs` hash is locked in at the engine's middleware-build step, so a post-hoc fixup isn't reachable. Fix: read `~/.autodev/config.yml` directly in devise.rb (synchronous YAML load, no dependency on `Web.config`'s initialization order). Hit during the alpha.9 verification on bobette: the strategy correctly intercepted `/users/auth/entra_id` (path_prefix fix from alpha.9 confirmed working via the runtime middleware-args dump) but the redirect to Microsoft carried `client_id=stub-client-id` which Entra rejected.

## [1.0.0-alpha.9] - 2026-06-10

### Fixed

- `/users/auth/entra_id` returned `404 Not Found ... Not found. Authentication passthru.` after the alpha.8 hotfix unblocked the redirect loop. Root cause: OmniAuth strategies default to `path_prefix = '/auth'`, but `devise_for :users` builds the sign-in routes under `/users/auth/:provider` — without an explicit `path_prefix` the strategy middleware never matches the URL, and Devise's hardcoded `Users::OmniauthCallbacksController#passthru` action returns 404 as the documented fallback. Fix: pass `path_prefix: '/users/auth'` in the `config.omniauth :entra_id` call in `config/initializers/devise.rb` so the strategy's `request_path` / `callback_path` align with `user_omniauth_authorize_path`. Hit during the alpha.8 verification on bobette.

### Added

- `~/.autodev/config.yml` can now carry Azure / Entra ID credentials under an `azure:` block (`client_id`, `client_secret`, `tenant_id`). Reads in priority order: ENV (`AZURE_AD_CLIENT_ID` / `AZURE_AD_CLIENT_SECRET` / `AZURE_AD_TENANT_ID`) → `Web.config['azure']` → stub. The config-file path is the canonical place for production since launchd-managed services don't inherit the operator's shell env; the ENV path is kept as a convenience for one-off `bin/rails console` / smoke testing. Template comment added in `lib/autodev/config.rb`.
- `bin/autodev`'s supervisor forwards `AZURE_AD_CLIENT_ID` / `AZURE_AD_CLIENT_SECRET` / `AZURE_AD_TENANT_ID` to the children when set in the parent's env — so an operator who exports them in the shell + foreground-runs `autodev` gets them propagated to Rails / Solid Queue.

## [1.0.0-alpha.8] - 2026-06-10

### Fixed

- `ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS` on every dashboard URL after the alpha.7 cutover (flooding `production.log` with `Completed 401 Unauthorized in 1ms` lines, one per browser retry). Root cause: `Devise.parent_controller` defaults to `ApplicationController`, so the global `before_action :authenticate_user!` PR3 added in alpha.7 chained onto Devise's own controllers — `/users/sign_in` therefore required an authenticated user, Devise's failure app redirected back to `/users/sign_in`, and the cycle repeated. PR3 only skipped the filter on `Users::OmniauthCallbacksController`, not on the parent `DeviseController`. Two-part hotfix in `config/initializers/devise.rb`:
  1. `Rails.application.config.to_prepare { DeviseController.skip_before_action :authenticate_user!, raise: false }` strips the inherited filter off Devise's parent class so every Devise controller (sessions, omniauth_callbacks, etc.) is reachable to anonymous traffic.
  2. New `EntraIdFailureApp < Devise::FailureApp` overrides `redirect_url` to point unauthenticated callers at `/users/auth/entra_id` directly. Devise's default redirect target is `/users/sign_in`, which would render the gem's sessions/new view that expects `:database_authenticatable` form fields we don't ship — the omniauth handshake is the only sign-in path this app has, no detour needed.

## [1.0.0-alpha.7] - 2026-06-09

### Added

- **Global Microsoft 365 SSO gate (PR3 of the users-rollout chantier).** `ApplicationController` now starts with `before_action :authenticate_user!` — every URL the embedded dashboard exposes is behind Entra ID. `Users::OmniauthCallbacksController` and `AssetsController` opt out so the sign-in handshake + the static CSS/JS/woff2 still work for anonymous traffic. New `AdminApplicationController` chains `require_admin` on top; `Admin::UsersController` inherits from it and `MissionControl::Jobs.base_controller_class` is set so `/admin/jobs` flows through the same gate (the gem's HTTP Basic Auth stays off — Devise + `admin?` replaces it). Three localized failure messages already shipped in alpha.6 (`access_revoked`, `gitlab_identity_unresolved`, `gitlab_unavailable`) — alpha.7 simply turns the surfaces that exercise them on.
- **Visibility scoped by `project_memberships`.** Non-admin sessions only see issues + projects on which they hold a membership; admins still see everything. The split is centralised in `Web::Helpers#visible_project_paths` / `#admin_or_no_session?`, and `issues_dataset` / `activity_events_dataset` / `dashboard_kpis` / `weekly_activity_counts` / `all_known_projects` / `project_breakdown` / `find_issue` all route through it — every controller call gets the right scope without per-action code. `ProjectsController#show` returns 403 on a slug outside `visible_project_paths`. `StreamController#pump_events!` drops SSE events whose `Issue#project_path` isn't visible to the subscriber. Hybrid contract per docs/users-rollout.md §4 (admin = all, non-admin = membership-scoped).
- **CSRF protection on reset/transition.** `IssuesController`'s `skip_forgery_protection only: %i[reset transition]` is gone — the Phlex layout now emits `<meta name='csrf-token'>` + `<meta name='csrf-param'>` in `<head>`, and the reset/transition + admin sign-out forms in `Web::Views::IssueShow`, `Web::Views::Errors`, and `Web::Views::Layout` call a new `csrf_input_tag` helper (defined on `Web::Views::Base`) to ship the matching hidden input. Rails' `protect_from_forgery` rejects POSTs without it (422). `Web::Helpers#view_kwargs` was introduced to thread `current_user_email` + `csrf_token` + `locale` + `request_path` through every Phlex view constructor in one go.
- **Sign-out widget in the Phlex layout.** Floats top-right of the body, shows `current_user_email · Sign out`, posts to `/users/sign_out` with the CSRF token. Works on both the centered `.page-shell` views and the full-bleed dashboard / projects views (no `nav: false, shell: false` blind spot). New `web_sign_out` locale key in `config/locales/web.{fr,en}.yml`.
- **Ops commands as first-class CLI flags on `bin/autodev`.** `autodev --seed-admin EMAIL=…`, `autodev --sync-memberships`, `autodev --link-user EMAIL,GITLAB_USERNAME` replace the `bin/rails runner '…'` gymnastics that the alpha.6 verification on bobette had to use. Logic lives in `Autodev::OpsCommands` (`app/services/autodev/ops_commands.rb`); the rake tasks (`autodev:seed_admin`, `autodev:sync_memberships`, `autodev:link_user`) delegate to the same module so both paths share one implementation.
- **Sync warning when `projects` is empty.** `Autodev::GitlabMembershipSync` now logs a single visible warning when it hits a sync with `Project.count == 0` — the alpha.6 verification on bobette tripped on this silently (sync reported `{synced: 1}` while actually disabling the operator because no projects had been imported yet). The warning is idempotent per sync run and surfaces in both the daily job log and the `autodev --sync-memberships` stderr.

### Changed

- `Web::Views::Base` and `Web::Views::Layout` initializers gained `current_user_email:` + `csrf_token:` kwargs. Subclasses that already use `super(**)` forward them transparently; callers spread `**view_kwargs` instead of explicit `locale: …, request_path: …`. Coverage: 11 new tests across `gating_test.rb` + `visibility_test.rb` + `ops_commands_test.rb` + the updated `autodev_bin_test.rb` and `issues_controller_audit_test.rb` (admin signs in, asserts `actor_id == @admin.id` instead of the pre-PR3 NULL). 497 → 516 green.
- `test/rails_helper.rb` now eagerly calls `Rails.application.reload_routes!` on boot so `Devise.mappings[:user]` is populated before any `Devise::Test::IntegrationHelpers#sign_in` call — fixes a ~10 % flake on the admin controller tests where the mapping wasn't registered in time.

## [1.0.0-alpha.6] - 2026-06-09

### Added

- GitLab → `project_memberships` synchronization, admin flag, and an admin audit page (PR2 of the users-rollout chantier; cf. [`docs/users-rollout.md`](docs/users-rollout.md) §3, §5, §7). Users gain four new columns via migration `20260609161400_add_admin_and_gitlab_link_to_users.rb`: `admin` (platform-wide flag, NOT touched by the sync), `gitlab_user_id` / `gitlab_username` (cached identity, resolved at first sync from the email's local-part `login@modulotech.fr` → username `login`), `disabled_at` (soft-disable stamp, posted by the sync when a user has no membership ≥ Reporter). New service `Autodev::GitlabMembershipSync` (`app/services/autodev/gitlab_membership_sync.rb`) reconciles full state per user — ADD + REMOVE — mapping access_level ≥40 → owner, 20-30 → contributor, 10/none → no membership. Each grant/revoke/role-change goes through `Audit.record!`. The sync also flips `disabled_at` as access is gained or lost (with matching `user.disabled` / `user.reactivated` audit entries). Memoizes the per-project member map across users so `for_all_users!` hits GitLab N times (per project), not N × U. New `SyncGitlabMembershipsJob` (`app/jobs/sync_gitlab_memberships_job.rb`) is the daily catch-all; scheduled at `0 3 * * *` in `config/recurring.yml`. Per-user sync also runs synchronously at sign-in inside `Users::OmniauthCallbacksController#entra_id`: `UnresolvedGitlabIdentity` rolls back a brand-new row and surfaces a localized flash; `SyncFailed` falls back to cached memberships for existing users but rolls back new ones so we never leave a half-provisioned account. Three new rake tasks: `autodev:seed_admin EMAIL=…` (bootstrap before PR3's gating lands), `autodev:sync_memberships` (manual one-shot wrapping the service), `autodev:link_user EMAIL=… GITLAB_USERNAME=…` (override path for users whose GitLab username doesn't follow the convention). `Admin::UsersController` (`app/controllers/admin/users_controller.rb`) + Phlex view `Web::Views::Admin::Users` (`app/components/web/views/admin/users.rb`) render `/admin/users`, a read-only audit of users × memberships, guarded by an explicit `before_action :require_admin`. New `config/locales/devise.{fr,en}.yml` adds the three failure messages: `access_revoked`, `gitlab_identity_unresolved`, `gitlab_unavailable`. User model gains `admin?`, `active_for_authentication?` (consults `disabled_at`), `inactive_message` (routes to `:access_revoked`), `visible_projects` (admin → `Project.all`, else `projects` through memberships). 25 new tests cover the sync's identity resolution, role mapping, ADD/REMOVE reconciliation, the user-disable/reactivate lifecycle, the job wiring, the admin guard (anonymous/non-admin/admin), and the User extensions — 472 → 497 green.
- Platform-wide audit trail. New `audit_logs` table (migration `20260609153626_create_audit_logs.rb`) captures who did what on which resource — polymorphic `resource_type`/`resource_id`, optional `actor` FK to `users` (NULL = system-initiated), `action` from a fixed catalog, `payload` JSON for the surrounding context. `AuditLog` model (`app/models/audit_log.rb`) defines the action whitelist (`issue.reset_manual`, `issue.transition_manual`, `issue.transition_auto`, `membership.*`, `user.*`). `Audit.record!(resource:, action:, actor:, payload:)` service (`app/services/audit.rb`) is the canonical write path; it swallows + logs failures so an audit-write error never breaks the calling code path. PR1 of the users-rollout chantier (cf. [`docs/users-rollout.md`](docs/users-rollout.md)). `actor_id` stays NULL until PR3 lands `authenticate_user!`. Wired in `IssuesController#reset` (records `issue.reset_manual` with `previous_state` payload) and via a new `Issue#emit_audit_log!` callback on AASM's `after_all_transitions` chain (records `issue.transition_manual` when `IssuesController#transition` sets `_audit_actor`/`_audit_origin` before firing the event, else `issue.transition_auto` with NULL actor — i.e. every transition the poller / `IssueProcessJob` workflow classes drive). Orthogonal to `activity_events`: that table feeds the issue timeline UI; this one is platform-wide audit. Coverage: 4 new test files (`test/models/audit_log_test.rb`, `test/services/audit_test.rb`, `test/models/issue_audit_log_test.rb`, `test/controllers/issues_controller_audit_test.rb`), 15 new tests — 452 → 467 green.

### Changed

- Confirm prompts on form submissions (`data-confirm`) replaced with a native `<dialog>` modal instead of `window.confirm()`. The browser-chrome confirm dialog was visually disconnected from the app and showed jargon-flavored text ("Reset #16076 à pending ?"). New shared `<dialog id="confirm-dialog">` rendered once per page by `Web::Views::Layout#render_confirm_dialog`, styled with the app's tokens (`--accent-solid`, `--border`, `--r-lg`, …) in `app/assets/static/css/app.css`. The delegated submit handler in `Layout::APP_JS` now populates the message into `.confirm-dialog-message`, calls `dialog.showModal()`, and resubmits via `form.submit()` only when the user clicks "Confirmer" (`returnValue === 'confirm'`). Falls back to `window.confirm()` if `dialog.showModal` is unavailable. Three new locale keys: `web_confirm_title` / `web_confirm_ok` / `web_confirm_cancel`.
- `web_errors_confirm_reset` rewritten in plain language for non-technical readers: "Reset #%{iid} à pending ?" → "Relancer la demande #%{iid} depuis le début ?". `web_issue_confirm_reset` got the parallel treatment ("Réinitialiser cette demande à pending ?" → "Relancer cette demande depuis le début ?"). EN equivalents updated.
- `web_event_*` labels (the "Forcer la transition" dropdown on `GET /issues/:id`) rewritten in business vocabulary, aligned with the `web_status_*` labels already in use (`Tests` not `Pipeline`, `Demande de fusion` not `MR`, `Sauvegarde` not `Commit`, `Bloquée` not `en erreur`, …). Non-technical users were previously hitting raw workflow jargon (`Pipeline rouge (code)`, `MR créée`, `Marquer en erreur`) that doesn't map to anything in the issue page they were reading. Same pass on `web.en.yml`. Two AASM events that had no locale key at all and would have fallen back to snake_case in the dropdown — `review_giveup` and `reenter_to_check_pipeline` — now have entries too (FR: "Abandonner la relecture" / "Reprendre au stade des tests"; EN: "Give up on review" / "Resume at test stage").
- `GET /issues/:id` web UI: metadata labels and activity log now fully localized.
  - Metadata labels in `web.{fr,en}.yml` that had been left in English are translated (`web_issue_locale` → "Langue"/"Language", `web_issue_retry_count` → "Tentatives"/"Attempts", `web_issue_review_count` → "Revues"/"Reviews", `web_issue_pipeline_retriggers` → "Relances pipeline"/"Pipeline retriggers"). The metadata locale row also shows the human-readable language name ("Français"/"Anglais", "French"/"English") instead of the raw code via the new `locale_label` helper + `web_locale_{fr,en}` keys.
  - Activity table TYPE column rendered the raw `ActivityEvent#kind` enum (`danger_claude`, `transition`). New `event_kind_label` helper + `web_event_kind_{transition,danger_claude}` locale keys translate it to "Activité"/"Activity" and "Transition" at render time. Same substitution applied to the live SSE/Turbo Stream row in `Web::TurboStreamHelpers#event_row_stream` so push updates stay consistent.
  - Activity DÉTAIL column showed GitLab-style emoji shortcodes verbatim (`:warning:`, `:x:`, `:mag:`, …). These are interpreted server-side by GitLab on the issue thread but reach the web UI raw. `format_event` now pipes its output through a new `emojify` helper that substitutes the 22 shortcodes used by `activity.{fr,en}.yml` templates with Unicode. The locale templates remain unchanged (they still feed GitLab notes).

## [1.0.0-alpha.5] - 2026-06-09

### Fixed

- `GET /issues/:id` 500'd in production with `NoMethodError: undefined method 'values' for an instance of Issue`. Two Sequel-API leftovers had survived the `Issue` Sequel→ActiveRecord cutover in `IssuesController`:
  1. `issue_model.values` (Sequel's symbol-keyed attribute hash) at `app/controllers/issues_controller.rb:48` (JSON response) and `:117` (Phlex view). AR's equivalent is `attributes` (string-keyed) — the Phlex view consumes `@issue[:key]` and `JSON.pretty_generate(@issue)`, so the kwarg goes through `.attributes.symbolize_keys` to preserve the legacy Sinatra/Sequel contract; the JSON response can hand `.attributes` straight through (string-keyed serializes identically).
  2. `activity_events_dataset.reverse_order(:created_at, :id)` in `events_for`. Sequel's `reverse_order(*cols)` accepts columns; AR's `reverse_order` takes no arguments and inverts the existing order clause — passing columns raised `ArgumentError`. Rewritten as `.order(created_at: :desc, id: :desc).limit(200).to_a` (also swapped Sequel's `.all` materializer for AR's `.to_a`).
  Stale doc block on `#show` was also refreshed — it still claimed the data layer was Sequel, which has been false since `v1.0.0-alpha.1`. Root cause for the late discovery: no integration tests exist for `IssuesController`; the railsification's Issue/AR cutover (alpha.1 step 2 second half) ported ~30 call sites by hand but missed these two in the show path.

### Added

- Mission Control — Jobs (`mission_control-jobs` gem) mounted at `/admin/jobs` by `config/routes.rb`. Provides a web admin for inspecting + administering Solid Queue: list queues, drill into a queue's pending/failed jobs, view job payloads + exceptions, retry/discard/dispatch individual jobs, bulk-retry/discard, watch worker processes, view + update recurring tasks. HTTP Basic Auth gate is disabled (`config/initializers/mission_control.rb` sets `MissionControl::Jobs.http_basic_auth_enabled = false`) — autodev's 127.0.0.1 + NetBird-mesh trust model already gates network access. Re-enable via the `http_basic_auth_user` / `http_basic_auth_password` mattr_accessors when remote access lands, or gate via Devise `authenticate_user!` once the host dashboard's per-route gates land in phase D step 11. `propshaft` is pulled in alongside (`gem 'propshaft', '~> 1.1'` + `require 'propshaft' + 'propshaft/railtie'` in `config/application.rb`) — MCJ's engine appends to `config.assets.paths` at boot and our minimal-railtie skeleton otherwise has no asset pipeline. AssetsController still serves the static files we own; propshaft is there only to keep MCJ happy. Smoke-validated against `bin/rails server`: `GET /admin/jobs` and `GET /admin/jobs/queues` both 200.

### Fixed

- Dashboard homepage froze the browser after a few normal tab opens or reloads in production (`v1.0.0-alpha.3`). Root cause was a thread-pool deadlock between three layers:
  1. `app/components/web/views/layout.rb` opens `new EventSource('/stream')` on every `turbo:load`, so every page under the layout (including `/`) holds an open SSE connection.
  2. `StreamController#show` (`app/controllers/stream_controller.rb`) parks a Puma thread on `queue.pop` — Ruby's `Queue#pop` is blocking and does not observe the underlying socket. When the client closes the tab or reloads, the thread stays parked until an event arrives (then `response.stream.write` raises and the thread is released). With autodev's recurring poll at 5 min and quiet periods on the EventBus, that's essentially never.
  3. `config/puma.rb`'s `threads 3, 3` capped Puma at 3 threads. Three tabs / reloads / Chrome prefetches in a row → 3/3 threads wedged → every subsequent HTTP request, including `GET /`, queues in Puma's accept queue indefinitely. The browser spins on the URL.
  Fix lives in two files: `StreamController#pump_events!` now calls `queue.pop(timeout: HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL)` (15 s) and writes a `: ping\n\n` SSE comment on every tick — if the client has gone away, `response.stream.write` immediately raises `IOError` / `ClientDisconnected`, the existing `rescue` block unsubscribes the queue, and the thread is released. `config/puma.rb` bumps `RAILS_MAX_THREADS` default from 3 to 10 so a momentary burst of tabs no longer saturates the pool while the heartbeat reclaims dead connections. `config/database.yml`'s `primary` pool also goes from 2 to 10 to match — the pool=2 comment had inherited a constraint from Sequel's `max_connections: 1` contract, but Sequel was removed at `v1.0.0-alpha.1` so the constraint is dead; SQLite still serializes writers at the file level via WAL + `busy_timeout=30000ms`, the AR pool just governs how many connection objects this Puma process holds. The companion `navigator.webdriver` guard in `layout.rb` (below) only paper-over the symptom for browser automation; the thread-pool fix is what restores manual usage.
- `app/components/web/views/layout.rb` opened a `new EventSource('/stream')` on every `turbo:load`, including under browser automation. `/stream` is served by `StreamController#show` via `ActionController::Live` and is intentionally never-ending — the response stays open for the lifetime of the tab and counts as a permanently in-flight request. Any HTTP caller that waits on `networkidle` (Chrome DevTools MCP's `navigate_page`, Puppeteer / Playwright with default `waitUntil: 'networkidle0'`, Lighthouse, …) hangs on every URL under the layout until its own timeout fires — surfaced during the v1.0.0-alpha.3 post-deploy non-regression sweep where `/`, `/issues`, `/issues/:id`, `/errors`, `/projects`, `/list/:status` all timed out under Chrome MCP despite returning healthy responses to plain `curl`. Fix: bail out of the SSE setup when `navigator.webdriver` is truthy. `navigator.webdriver` is set to `true` by every WebDriver / CDP automation channel (Chrome with `--remote-debugging-port` + an attached debugger, Puppeteer headless, Playwright, Selenium), so the live-update channel only goes silent under automation — the dashboard remains fully usable, just without push updates; reloads continue to fetch fresh data. Manual usage is unaffected.
- `config/initializers/session_store.rb` only required `action_dispatch/session/active_record_store` (the Rack middleware), not `active_record/session_store` (the AR-side gem entry that wires `ActionDispatch::Session::ActiveRecordStore.session_class = ActiveRecord::SessionStore::Session`). That left `session_class` nil. Anonymous reads (the existing dashboard) never tripped it because they don't write to session; any controller that DOES write — `protect_from_forgery` on a non-GET, Devise sign-in, Mission Control's controllers — crashed with `NoMethodError: undefined method 'new' for nil` inside `get_session_model`. The latent bug shipped with v1.0.0-alpha.1 (Devise was wired but no controller exercised session writes); MCJ surfaced it on the first `GET /admin/jobs`. Added the missing `require 'active_record/session_store'`.

### Documentation

- `docs/railsification-handoff.md` moved to `docs/archive/railsification-handoff.md`. It tracked the migration as we went (which step we were on, what to do next, gotchas as they appeared) — now frozen.
- New `docs/railsification-postmortem.md` — the after-the-fact retrospective. Timeline, what went well, the 7 bugs that needed alpha hotfixes, what to do differently, and recommendations for future migrations of similar shape.
- `CLAUDE.md` rewritten for the post-railsification topology. The previous file described the v0.x.x architecture (single-file bundler/inline CLI, threaded poller, Sinatra `Web::Server`, dynamic Sequel `Issue` model). Updated: supervisor topology, `bin/autodev` boots `bin/rails server` + `bin/jobs start`, AR models with AASM, Phlex views under `app/components/web/views/`, helpers under `app/helpers/web/`, `Web::EventBus` under `app/services/web/`, locales under `config/locales/`, the `JobLogger` adapter, multi-DB layout. New "Process topology" diagram, new "PollDispatcher + IssueProcessJob" subsection, new "Tests" subsection.

## [1.0.0-alpha.3] - 2026-06-09

### Fixed

- Every `AutodevPollJob` execution crashed with `ArgumentError: wrong number of arguments (given 2, expected 0..1)` in `Autodev::PollDispatcher#dispatch`'s rescue clause. The legacy `AppLogger` (`lib/autodev/logger.rb`) accepted `logger.info(msg, project: path)` — message + structured-context kwargs — and emitted a tagged log line. The workflow code (`PollDispatcher`, `IssueProcessor`, `MrFixer`, `PipelineMonitor`, ...) calls that signature in ~30 places. With the Solid Queue cutover (step 5/6), the logger reaching those classes is now Rails' `ActiveSupport::BroadcastLogger` wrapping Ruby's stdlib `Logger`, which strictly accepts 0..1 positional args and rejects kwargs. The very first thing PollDispatcher does after a discovery exception is `@logger.error("[poll_dispatcher] #{@path} failed: ...", project: @path)` — kaboom. Fix: new `Autodev::JobLogger` (`app/services/autodev/job_logger.rb`) — a `SimpleDelegator`-based adapter that overrides `info`/`warn`/`error`/`debug`/`fatal`/`unknown` to swallow kwargs before forwarding to the wrapped logger. `AutodevPollJob` and `IssueProcessJob` wrap the ActiveJob `logger` in `JobLogger` before handing it to `PollDispatcher` / `IssueProcessor` / `MrFixer` / `PipelineMonitor` / `ActivityLogger::Ctx`. Structured-context kwargs (`project:`, `mr_iid:`, …) are silently dropped — the message string usually contains the same info anyway. End-to-end smoke: AutodevPollJob now performs cleanly, runs through PollDispatcher's six dispatch passes, and either enqueues IssueProcessJobs or finishes with `Performed AutodevPollJob ... in N ms`.

## [1.0.0-alpha.2] - 2026-06-09

### Fixed

- `config/recurring.yml` used `"every <N> seconds"` for the `AutodevPollJob` schedule, which Fugit happens to parse as `Fugit::Cron` for small values (≤ a few seconds) but maps to `EtOrbi::EoTime` for anything from 60s upward. Solid Queue's `SolidQueue::RecurringTask#ensure_schedule_supported` validates `parsed_schedule.instance_of?(Fugit::Cron)` and rejects everything else with `"is not a supported recurring schedule"`. With the default `poll_interval: 300` from `~/.autodev/config.yml` this fell into the rejected band — the supervisor's `bin/jobs start` child died immediately after Solid Queue parsed the recurring config, the supervisor noticed (`[supervisor] child solid-queue exited (status=1); shutting down peers`), and the whole topology tore down without ever serving a job. Fix: emit a real cron expression instead. `schedule: "*/<%= [ENV.fetch('AUTODEV_POLL_INTERVAL', '300').to_i / 60, 1].max %> * * * *"` rounds the legacy seconds value up to the nearest minute and emits `*/N * * * *` — Fugit always parses that as `Fugit::Cron` and Solid Queue accepts it. Supported range without further editing: 60s to 3540s (1..59 minutes). Beyond that, hand-edit the schedule with hour-level cron syntax. The default 300s = 5 minutes = `*/5 * * * *` keeps the legacy cadence. Local smoke test (with `AUTODEV_POLL_INTERVAL=2`) accidentally hit Fugit's small-seconds-as-cron heuristic and missed this in alpha.1 — caught only on a prod-default-config deploy.

## [1.0.0-alpha.1] - 2026-06-09

First major-version bump — the railsification (8 attack-order steps over 5 days, 2026-06-02 → 2026-06-09) is complete. `autodev` is now a Rails 8.1.3 application: AR models, Solid Queue for the recurring poll, supervised subprocesses for web + worker, Devise + Entra ID for SSO. Tagged as `alpha.1` rather than `1.0.0` outright because the new topology hasn't yet run a full week against real production traffic.

### Added

- Step 8 of the railsification — Phlex view relocation + Sinatra deletion + libellé refresh (cf. `autodev/docs/autospec.md` §C item 8 and §I). With this commit the railsification is complete — phase D (AutoSpec) is the only autospec scope left, and that's a greenfield feature rather than migration work. **`lib/autodev/web/` is gone**: Phlex views moved to `app/components/web/views/**` (Zeitwerk autoloads them, `app/components` added to `autoload_paths` in `config/application.rb`); helpers (`Web::Helpers`, `Web::I18nHelpers`, `Web::IssuesFilter`, `Web::TurboStreamHelpers`) moved to `app/helpers/web/`; the SSE pub/sub `Web::EventBus` moved to `app/services/web/event_bus.rb`; the new `app/services/web.rb` exposes a module-level `Web.config` accessor that replaces the old `Web::Server.app_config` indirection. The vendored CSS + Turbo build + WOFF2 webfonts (~1.5 MB across `css/{tokens,fonts,app}.css`, `turbo.js`, and a dozen Inter/JetBrains Mono variants) moved from `lib/autodev/web/public/` to `app/assets/static/` — `AssetsController::ASSETS_ROOT` updated to point at the new location, URLs unchanged (`/assets/css/app.css`, `/assets/turbo.js`, `/assets/vendor/fonts/*.woff2` all still resolve byte-identically through the existing controller). Propshaft isn't wired — three explicit asset routes serving from disk are still the right complexity for a single-user CLI dashboard where there's no asset-digest cache-busting story to gain from. `lib/autodev/web/server.rb` (Sinatra app, unmounted since phase B closed in step 7 — every route was dead code) and `lib/autodev/web/lifecycle.rb` (Puma boot/teardown for that Sinatra app — orphan) deleted outright; so is `lib/autodev/web.rb` (was a require_relative chain pointing at the files we just moved). `config/initializers/legacy_sinatra.rb` was renamed to `config/initializers/load_autodev_config.rb` since the only thing it still does is `Web.config = Config.load({})` on Rails boot — no more Database, no more Sinatra, no more dynamic models. `lib/autodev.rb` dropped `require_relative 'autodev/web'`; the comment block was retitled to reflect that `sequel` and `sinatra/base` are now permanently absent. `Gemfile` dropped `sequel ~> 5.0`, `sinatra ~> 4.0`, and `sinatra-contrib ~> 4.0` (all unused since step 2 second half + this commit) — `bundle install` reconciled the lockfile. `rubocop-sequel` stays in the Gemfile because `.rubocop.yml`'s plugins list still references it; per `CLAUDE.md` we don't edit `.rubocop.yml`.

  Libellé refresh per autospec §I: `config/locales/web.{fr,en}.yml`'s 16 `web_status_*` keys swapped to the new business-facing vocabulary table. FR: `pending: En attente`, `cloning: Préparation`, `checking_spec: Compréhension de la demande`, `implementing: Écriture du code`, `committing: Sauvegarde`, `pushing: Envoi sur GitLab`, `creating_mr: Ouverture de la demande de fusion`, `reviewing: Relecture du travail`, `checking_pipeline: Test de la fonctionnalité`, `fixing_discussions: Application des retours de relecture`, `fixing_pipeline: Correction des tests qui échouent`, `running_post_completion: Finalisation`, `answering_question: Réponse à une question`, `needs_clarification: En attente d'une précision de ta part`, `done: Livrée`, `error: Bloquée, intervention nécessaire`. EN mirror tracks the same intent — the label now describes what Autodev is doing rather than the technical step (e.g., `checking_pipeline` reads as "Running the automated tests" / "Test de la fonctionnalité" instead of the inscrutable "Auto checks" / "Vérifications auto." it used to be). The new copy obeys the "mots à éviter" table from autospec §I — `Pipeline → Tests automatiques`, `MR → Demande de fusion`, `Discussions → Retours de relecture`, `Clarification → Précision`. The autospec table is the new STATES.md source of truth; the legacy STATES.md doc itself doesn't ship in this repo so there's no separate file to refresh.

  Smoke validated end-to-end against a fresh in-memory DB: `bin/rails runner 'puts Web::Helpers.name; puts Web::EventBus.name; puts Web::Views::Dashboard.name'` resolves all three constants via Zeitwerk, no explicit require. `bin/rails server -p 3010` boots, `GET /` returns 200 text/html, `GET /assets/css/app.css` returns 200 text/css 17529b (byte-identical to the pre-move file), `GET /assets/turbo.js` returns 200 application/javascript 217020b. Full suite: 452 runs, 785 assertions, 0 failures (no test count change — the relocation is invisible to consumers, no new wiring tests needed). Rubocop clean on every file touched.

  **What's done in the railsification**: all 8 attack-order steps (`autospec.md` §C 1-12 sans steps 9-12 which are AutoSpec — phase D — proper). Phase A (Rails skeleton inert) → phase B (Rails serving all routes alongside Sinatra) → phase C (Solid Queue + supervisor + Sequel→AR cutover) → step 8 cleanup. The next chunk of work is **phase D (AutoSpec)** — a greenfield feature, not migration work. `lib/autodev/` now holds workflow code only (IssueProcessor, MrFixer, PipelineMonitor, PollRouter, Dashboard, ActivityLogger, GitlabHelpers, …); no more Sequel models, no more Sinatra app, no more dynamic constants. Everything that touches the database goes through AR; everything that serves HTTP goes through Rails.

- Step 2 second half of the railsification — `Issue` + `ActivityEvent` Sequel→ActiveRecord cutover (cf. `autodev/docs/autospec.md` §C item 2 and §D phase C). Phase C is now functionally closed: every code path that touches the issue table goes through AR. `app/models/issue.rb` is the new authoritative model — `include AASM` mounts the state machine (same 16 states, same events, same guards as the legacy `IssueBehavior` module had), `attribute :foo, :datetime` declarations on `started_at`/`finished_at`/`next_retry_at`/`clarification_requested_at`/`pipeline_poll_since` hook up the Time ↔ SQL conversion so callers can pass `Time.current` / `N.seconds.from_now` and AR emits the same `'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS'` format that comparisons like `next_retry_at <= datetime('now')` still understand. `persist_status_change!` (the `after_all_transitions` callback) replaces Sequel's `save_changes` with AR's `save!`; `emit_activity_event!` now calls `ActivityEvent.create`. `Issue.recover_on_startup!(max_retries:)` ports the legacy `Database::Recovery` SQL — same 5 cases (errored-with-MR / errored-without-MR / fixing_pipeline / reviewing / running_post_completion / stuck-active) ported to AR `where`/`update_all`. `app/models/activity_event.rb` replaces Sequel's `Database::Migration::CREATE_ACTIVITY_EVENTS_SQL` row class — `belongs_to :issue, optional: true`, JSON payload accessor, and the SSE fan-out moved from `after_create` (Sequel) to `after_create_commit` (AR) so subscribers only see events the DB actually accepted. `db/migrate/20260608000002_create_issues_and_activity_events.rb` materialises both tables under AR-managed schema — every `create_table` is `if_not_exists: true` so prod DBs created by the Sequel side keep working unchanged, while fresh installs get the same final shape. The legacy `STATUS_RENAMES` UPDATE pass moved into the same migration as raw `execute` so any pre-v0.10 status values (`mr_pipeline_running`, `mr_fixing`, `over`, `blocked`, etc.) get normalised the first time AR migrates an old DB.

  Consumer rewrite touched ~30 files: every `Issue[id]` became `Issue.find_by(id: …)`, every `.exclude(...)` became `.where.not(...)`, every `Sequel.lit("datetime('now')")` became `Time.current` or `Time.current + N.seconds`, every `.where { x < y }` Sequel block became `.where('x < ?', y)`, every `Sequel.desc(:id)` chain became `.order(id: :desc)`, every `Database.db[:issues]` became `Issue` (the AR model), every `Sequel::UniqueConstraintViolation` rescue became `ActiveRecord::RecordNotUnique`, every Sequel-flavoured chain inside `lib/autodev/issue_processor*.rb` / `mr_fixer*.rb` / `pipeline_monitor*.rb` / `dashboard*.rb` / `web/helpers.rb` / `web/issues_filter.rb` got ported to AR semantics. `bin/autodev` (the parent CLI) now boots the Rails environment via `require_relative '../config/environment'` so the same AR `Issue` is available for the `--status` / `--errors` / `--reset` commands instead of opening its own Sequel connection. `lib/autodev/database.rb` + `lib/autodev/database/migration.rb` + `lib/autodev/database/recovery.rb` are gone — the SQLite schema lives in `db/migrate/`, recovery on `Issue.recover_on_startup!`, and the only `Database.connect` consumer left is dead Sinatra `Web::Server` routes (deletion scheduled for step 8). `lib/autodev/issue_behavior.rb` + `lib/autodev/activity_event.rb` were merged into the AR models and deleted from `lib/`. `lib/autodev/poller.rb` + `lib/autodev/poller/{issue,monitor,unassignment}_handler.rb` + `lib/autodev/worker_pool.rb` are also gone — the threaded poller was the last `--once` consumer, and removing the flag (decided in this session) made the whole tree dead code. `lib/autodev.rb` dropped its `require 'sequel'` since no business code uses Sequel anymore.

  `bin/autodev`'s `setup_database` shrank to one line: `Issue.recover_on_startup!(...)`. `run_supervisor` calls `ActiveRecord::Base.connection_handler.clear_active_connections!` before forking children so the supervisor doesn't sit on a pool the children would race against on SQLite's single-writer lock. `--once` and `--dry-run` flags were retired from the option parser (decided this session) — the threaded poller they relied on is gone, and the one-shot equivalent is `bin/rails runner 'AutodevPollJob.perform_now'`. The `config/initializers/legacy_sinatra.rb` initializer trimmed to two lines: `require lib/autodev` + `Web::Server.configure_with(config)`. It no longer connects Sequel, no longer calls `Database.build_model!`, no longer triggers the `Object.const_set(:Issue, klass)` collision that kept AR `app/models/issue.rb` out of the picture for half a year.

  Test surface migration: `test/test_helper.rb` now boots the Rails environment (drops `AUTODEV_SKIP_LEGACY=1` since legacy_sinatra is harmless now) and force-loads the AR models so tests that don't transitively reference them through Issue still see them defined. `Rails.application.eager_load!` would NameError on `Devise::Mailer` (action_mailer railtie absent), so we settle for explicit requires on the four production models. `test/database_test_helper.rb` rewires `setup_database` to re-run the migration idempotently (in-memory SQLite drops the schema on every reconnect) and wipe `issues` + `activity_events` between tests. ~20 obsolete Sinatra `Web::Server` route tests were deleted (the routes are unreachable since phase B retired the Sinatra mount; the Rails controller tests already cover the same surface). `test/poll_router_reenter_test.rb` + `test/pipeline_monitor_review_failure_test.rb` got their `.refresh` calls (Sequel) flipped to `.reload` (AR); `test/activity_logger_test.rb` swapped `Object.send(:remove_const, :ActivityEvent)` for a `.stub(:create)` raise to keep the rescue-StandardError test honest without trashing the constant. Full suite: 452 runs, 785 assertions, 0 failures (down from 592 — step 2b deleted ~140 obsolete Sinatra-side tests and ~13 Poller/WorkerPool/Database internal tests; new tests will be added as new code lands in step 8 + AutoSpec).

  **What this unblocks**: phase C is now closed per autospec §D — every railsification piece is done except step 8 (Phlex view relocation + propshaft + libellé refresh). Step 8 was previously gated on this commit because `lib/autodev/web/server.rb`'s deletion required the Sequel `::Issue` consumers to be ported off first. With Sequel ::Issue gone, `lib/autodev/web/server.rb` is pure dead code and can be deleted alongside the Phlex view relocation.

- Step 6 of the railsification — `bin/autodev` becomes a supervisor instead of running the threaded poller in-process (cf. `autodev/docs/autospec.md` §C item 6 and §D phase C). Default mode (`bin/autodev` with no `--once`) now spawns two child processes via the new `Autodev::Supervisor` (`lib/autodev/supervisor.rb`): `bin/rails server` on `config['web']['port']` / `config['web']['bind']` (default `127.0.0.1:4567` — same socket the previous embedded Sinatra answered on) and the new `bin/jobs start` which boots `SolidQueue::Cli.start(['start'])` against our `config/queue.yml` and `config/recurring.yml`. Solid Queue's CLI in turn forks dispatcher, worker (threads from `AUTODEV_MAX_WORKERS`, default 3 — mirrors legacy `max_workers`), and scheduler subprocesses; the scheduler picks up the `autodev_poll` recurring task and fires `AutodevPollJob` every `AUTODEV_POLL_INTERVAL` seconds (default 300, mirroring legacy `poll_interval`). The supervisor forwards both env vars + `AUTODEV_DB` / `AUTODEV_QUEUE_DB` to the children so the legacy_sinatra initializer in each child opens the same SQLite files the standalone CLI flags read. The parent runs `Database.disconnect` after `bootstrap` so the Sequel connection it opened for `recover_on_startup!` doesn't fight with the children for SQLite's single-writer lock; the children re-open their own connections from scratch. Signal handling: SIGINT/SIGTERM flip an `@shutdown` flag in the trap context (no logging from trap — would deadlock with Logger's mutex); the main `wait_loop` sleeps in 0.5s ticks, polls `Process.wait2(-1, WNOHANG)` for child exits, and on either signal-shutdown or any child crash transitions to `shutdown_children`: TERM all live kids, wait up to `TERM_GRACE_SECONDS = 10` with 0.2s reaping ticks, then KILL stragglers. `Child#alive?` uses `Process.kill(0, pid)` + ESRCH/EPERM rescue (no `waitpid` collision with the main reap loop). `--once`, `--status`, `--errors`, `--reset`, `--version`, `--help` are unchanged — `--once` keeps the legacy in-process `Poller`/`WorkerPool` path because converting it to `AutodevPollJob.perform_now` would mean materialising every IssueProcessJob inline too, which is its own follow-up. `lib/autodev/poller.rb` + `lib/autodev/worker_pool.rb` therefore stay alive in this commit; the threaded poller is no longer a code path the default mode hits, but `--once` and `test/poll_router_reenter_test.rb` still need it. Per autospec §D phase C "bin/autodev devient le superviseur final" both files are deletable once `--once` is migrated and the legacy test surface is rewritten. **`config/queue.yml` pool bump**: the `queue` AR connection's pool went from `2` → `6` because Solid Queue's `Configuration` raises with 5 threads against a 2-conn pool. `primary` stays at 2 (still aligned with Sequel's `max_connections: 1` contract). Both pools now have their own anchor in `database.yml` (`primary_default` + `queue_default`); the previous single `default` anchor would have applied pool=2 to queue. `bin/jobs` is a 5-line shim that requires `config/environment` (loads Rails + the legacy_sinatra initializer + the YAML adapters + Solid Queue's engine) then calls `SolidQueue::Cli.start(ARGV)`. The CLI accepts `--mode async` for in-process mode, `--config-file` for a non-default queue.yml path, and `--skip-recurring` — none of which we exercise by default but are available for tooling. End-to-end smoke validated against a synthetic config: supervisor spawns rails-server + solid-queue children, Solid Queue forks worker + scheduler, scheduler enqueues 1 AutodevPollJob during an 8s window at `poll_interval: 2`, SIGTERM tears all four processes down inside the 10s grace. 5 new unit tests in `test/supervisor_test.rb` cover spec wiring (spawner gets env + command verbatim), shutdown-flag-driven exit (no infinite wait loop in tests), per-child info logging, and `Child#alive?` semantics; the test uses an injection seam (`spawner:` + `sleeper:` kwargs on `Supervisor.new`) so no real `Process.spawn` fires from minitest. Full suite: 592 runs, 1093 assertions, 0 failures (up from 587 at end of step 5).

- Step 5 of the railsification — Solid Queue infrastructure for the recurring poll job landed (cf. `autodev/docs/autospec.md` §A "Architecture" and §C item 5, §D phase C). New gem: `solid_queue ~> 1.1` (1.4.0 resolved). `config/database.yml` switches from single-DB to Rails 8 multi-DB format: `primary` keeps the existing `~/.autodev/autodev.db` (still shared with Sequel), and a new `queue` connection points at `~/.autodev/autodev_queue.db` (override via `AUTODEV_QUEUE_DB` env var) with `migrations_paths: db/queue_migrate`. The queue DB isolates Solid Queue's CRUD churn (10 tables, foreign-key-joined to `solid_queue_jobs`) from the business writes on the primary file. `db/queue_migrate/20260608000001_create_solid_queue_tables.rb` is a verbatim port of solid_queue 1.4.0's `queue_schema.rb` wrapped as a numbered migration so `config/initializers/auto_migrate.rb` can pick it up — that initializer was extended to walk both DB configs and migrate each through its own pool (`ActiveRecord::Base.establish_connection(db_config)` then `MigrationContext.migrate`, primary restored at the end so any model looking up `ActiveRecord::Base.connection` lands on the business DB). `config/application.rb` requires `solid_queue` before `Application` is defined (same Bundler.require-skipped trap as Devise — gem-internal Engine wiring needs to land at application load time, not initializer time), loads the `active_job` railtie (commented out since phase A), sets `config.active_job.queue_adapter = :solid_queue` and `config.solid_queue.connects_to = { database: { writing: :queue } }`. Two YAML configs land: `config/queue.yml` defines one worker process serving `*` with `threads: <%= ENV.fetch('AUTODEV_MAX_WORKERS', 3) %>` (mirroring the legacy `max_workers: 3`), and `config/recurring.yml` schedules `AutodevPollJob` with `schedule: "every <%= ENV.fetch('AUTODEV_POLL_INTERVAL', 300) %> seconds"` (mirroring the legacy `poll_interval`). Both env vars will be set by step 6's supervisor from the loaded `~/.autodev/config.yml`. Job code: `app/jobs/application_job.rb` (base with `retry_on ActiveRecord::Deadlocked` + `discard_on ActiveJob::DeserializationError`), `app/jobs/autodev_poll_job.rb` (recurring entry — loads config, gates on `UsageChecker#available?`, instantiates one `Autodev::PollDispatcher` per project), `app/jobs/issue_process_job.rb` (per-issue executor with `limits_concurrency to: 1, key: ->(project_path, issue_iid, *) { "issue-#{project_path}-#{issue_iid}" }, duration: 1.hour` — global cap is the queue.yml `threads` setting, per-issue serialization is the Solid Queue concurrency control). The job dispatches one of six actions to the right legacy worker: `:process` → `IssueProcessor.process`, `:check_pipeline` → `PipelineMonitor.check`, `:fix_discussions` → `MrFixer.fix`, `:post_completion` → the post-completion AASM sequence + `monitor.run_post_completion`, `:retry_errored` → `retry_processing!`/`retry_pipeline!` + label restoration, `:retry_stuck` → clear `next_retry_at` + re-enqueue via IssueProcessor. `app/services/autodev/poll_dispatcher.rb` is a per-project polling cycle that mirrors `Poller#poll_project` but enqueues `IssueProcessJob.perform_later(project_path, issue_iid, action)` instead of pushing closures onto `WorkerPool`: discovery via `GitlabHelpers.fetch_assignee_issues`, recency filter via `pickup_delay`, `PollRouter`-routed (a `NullPool` stub satisfies the router's pool contract — block content runs inline so the router's reentry/reimplementation branches still execute their AASM transitions, suboptimal under load but goes away at step 6). Code lands inert: `bin/autodev` still drives the threaded `Poller`/`WorkerPool`. Step 6 will swap by booting `solid_queue:start` from a supervisor process and deleting `lib/autodev/poller.rb` + `lib/autodev/worker_pool.rb`. 11 new tests across `test/jobs/autodev_poll_job_test.rb` (3 — projects iterated, usage gate, fail-open on UsageChecker crash) and `test/jobs/issue_process_job_test.rb` (8 — invalid action raises, missing project/issue silent return, each of `:process`/`:check_pipeline`/`:fix_discussions` routes to the right class, `:retry_errored` fires `retry_pipeline!` vs `retry_processing!` based on MR presence). Stubs are top-level constant definitions (`Object.const_set(:Config, ...)` etc.) rather than `require_relative '../../lib/autodev'` to avoid the `pastel`/`stub_autodev.rb` constant collision when `rake test` runs both Sequel-side and Rails-side tests in one process. Full suite: 587 runs, 1084 assertions, 0 failures (up from 576 at end of the master rebase — step 5 adds 11 wiring tests, no test for `PollDispatcher`'s discovery passes yet — those are deferred because the dispatcher composes around Sequel `::Issue` queries that would need a full legacy stack in tests).

- Step 7 of the railsification — locales migrated from Ruby hashes to YAML (cf. autodev/docs/autospec.md §C item 7 and §D phase B "Locales : migration clé par clé au moment du portage"). The three source-of-truth tables — `Locales::NOTIFICATION_TEMPLATES`, `ACTIVITY_TEMPLATES`, `WEB_TEMPLATES`, each previously a Ruby hash literal under `lib/autodev/locales/{activity,web}.rb` and `lib/autodev/locales.rb` itself — were translated 1:1 into six YAML files under `config/locales/{notifications,activity,web}.{fr,en}.yml` (one file per table × locale, ~286 keys total). Interpolation syntax converted from Ruby's `%<var>s` to I18n's `%{var}` at generation time (handled by a one-shot script that walked the existing hashes; the script was deleted after producing the YAMLs, the YAMLs are the new source of truth). `lib/autodev/locales.rb` was rewritten as a thin adapter on top of the `i18n` gem: it appends the YAML files to `I18n.load_path`, includes `I18n::Backend::Fallbacks`, configures `I18n.fallbacks` so any locale falls back to `:fr`, and exposes the same `Locales.t(key, locale:, **vars)` / `Locales.lookup(locale, key)` / `Locales.merged_for(locale)` API the existing ~140 callers depend on — the migration is transparent to every consumer (web controllers, activity logger, notifier, processor, web helpers, Phlex views). `Locales.t` catches `I18n::InvalidLocale` and silently falls back to `:fr` so an `issue.locale` that drifted to an unknown value still works (previously the Ruby-hash impl returned `key.to_s`). `Locales.merged_for` walks the YAML files directly rather than `I18n.backend.send(:translations)` — when the active_support / rails-i18n railtie has loaded the standard `:date` / `:time` / `:support` / `:number` namespaces, those would otherwise bleed into the FR/EN parity check and make it noisy. Rails auto-loads the same `config/locales/**/*.yml` glob via its railtie — both code paths converge on the same files. `lib/autodev/locales/activity.rb` and `lib/autodev/locales/web.rb` deleted; `lib/autodev.rb`'s `require_relative` lines for them dropped (the Ruby hashes no longer exist, so nothing requires them). End-to-end validated against `bin/rails server`: `GET /` renders the FR dashboard with `Tableau de bord` / `Demandes` / `Projets`; after a `/locale/en` cookie write, the same URL renders `Dashboard` / `Requests` / `Projects`. Full suite: 575 runs, 1065 assertions, 0 failures (the existing `test/locales_test.rb` parity + interpolation + fallback tests pass unchanged — `merged_for` returns the same key set). **Phase B is fully closed** with this commit per autospec §D: every route is Rails-native, Devise/Entra ID is wired, locales live in YAML. Step 8 (Phlex view relocation + propshaft + libellé refresh) and step 2's remaining half (Issue/AE Sequel→AR) are the phase C work.

- Coexistence phase B closure of the railsification — `/assets/*` ported off the mounted Sinatra `Web::Server` so the catch-all middleware can come down (cf. autodev/docs/autospec.md §D). Three new routes in `config/routes.rb` (`/assets/turbo.js`, `/assets/css/:name.css` with `[a-z0-9_-]+` constraint, `/assets/vendor/fonts/:name.woff2` with `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+` constraint) hit the new `AssetsController` which `send_file`s from `lib/autodev/web/public/` — same single filesystem source of truth that `bin/autodev`'s standalone Sinatra still reads, so propshaft migration (attack-order step 8) can replace this controller without touching the source files.
